## Sitewide justified text alignment — added between phases, per request

`public/assets/css/base.css`: `p`, `li`, and `.accordion-body` (FAQ answers, which use a `<div>` rather than `<p>`) now render `text-align: justify` with `hyphens: auto` — the latter to avoid the large, ugly word-gaps justified text produces on narrower columns without hyphenation. Deliberately **not** applied to the whole `<body>` — headings, buttons, badges, and nav links are short/single-line text where justify has no visible effect anyway, but a two-word wrapped heading stretched full-width to justify looks visibly broken, so `h1`–`h6` and component text (buttons/badges/nav) are left on their default alignment.

Verified via real browser rendering, not just reading the CSS: computed `text-align` confirmed as `justify` on a real `<p>` (`/about/`) and a real `.accordion-body` (`/faq/`, after opening the accordion), confirmed `h1` stays at its default (`start`), and confirmed visually via full-page screenshots of `/about/` and `/faq/` — every paragraph now has clean, even left and right edges, headings and the footer's nav columns are visually unaffected, zero console errors.

## Real favicon set — added between phases, per request

Replaced the inline-SVG-data-URI placeholder (a single icon, no size variants, in place since Phase 3/4) with a proper favicon set: `favicon.svg` (scalable, modern browsers), `favicon-16x16.png`/`favicon-32x32.png` (legacy/small-size fallback), `apple-touch-icon.png` (180×180, iOS home-screen), and a real multi-resolution `favicon.ico` at the site root (16/32/48px, each frame genuine PNG-compressed image data — confirmed via `file`, not just a renamed PNG).

No ImageMagick/`rsvg-convert` is available in this environment, so each PNG size was drawn directly with PHP's GD library at 8× supersampling then downsampled for clean anti-aliased edges — rather than generating one image and letting the browser scale it down blurrily — and the `.ico` was hand-assembled (ICO's modern PNG-frame container format, embedding those same PNGs, which is what every current browser/OS expects). Same brand colors used everywhere else on the site: navy `#123F91` background, gold `#F4B400` "V" — the same scheme the placeholder favicon already used, since the real logo file has still never been uploaded as an actual file attachment (unresolved since Phase 6).

All five files wired into `includes/header.php` via the standard multi-`<link>` pattern (SVG first for browsers that support it, ICO/PNG fallbacks with explicit `sizes`, separate `apple-touch-icon` link) and routed through the same `asset_url()` cache-buster as every other static asset, so a future favicon update won't get stuck behind a stale year-long browser cache.

**Verified**: all five files return 200 with correct `Content-Type` (`image/svg+xml`, `image/vnd.microsoft.icon`, `image/png` ×3); `file` confirms `favicon.ico` is a genuine 3-icon `MS Windows icon resource` with real embedded PNG data at each size, not a mislabeled single image; zero console errors or failed requests loading the homepage.

## SEO title/description audit across all pages — per request

Dispatched a background audit of every page's `<title>` and meta description against standard best-practice length guidelines (title ~30–60 characters, description ~120–160 — outside that range, Google tends to either pad an unhelpfully generic snippet or truncate mid-sentence). Findings, then fixes:

- **A systemic pattern, not isolated typos**: nearly every real content page's meta description was well under the 120-char floor (60–110 chars typical) — accurate, just too terse to use the SERP snippet space Google allots. Rewritten with real, honest specifics (not padding) on: `/` (homepage), `/about/`, `/faq/`, `/visa-process/`, `/track-visa/`, `/sitemap/`, `/countries/`, `/attestation/` (hub and all 4 real services, which gained a dedicated `meta_description` field separate from their on-page description so the visible UI copy could stay concise while the meta tag hits the target length), and the `/visa-type/` hub.
- **Two titles were too short/generic** (`/about/`: "About Visagiri"; `/countries/`: "Countries - Visagiri"; `/sitemap/`: "Sitemap - Visagiri") — rewritten with real specifics (the confirmed April 2015 founding date, the real 208-country count).
- **My own Sports Visa edit from earlier today was itself flagged** — the visa-type hub title (83 chars) and per-type template (66+ chars, worse for longer names like "Family Visit Visa") were both over budget, caught by the same audit that reviewed everything else. Shortened both templates; verified every one of the 9 real visa types now produces a title in the 44–60 range.
- **The two highest-traffic-shaped templates** — `visa/index.php`'s country and country+visa-type pages, which generate up to 1,872 URLs (208 countries × up to 9 types, plus 208 country overviews) — had the same too-short description problem *for every single generated page*, not a handful. Rewrote both templates with the same honest, non-fabricated framing already established in Phase 6 ("eligibility, required documents, fees, and processing time" describes what the page covers, not a claim that verified figures exist yet) — now in the target range for the overwhelming majority of the 208 real countries; a few extreme edge cases (the longest country name, "Democratic Republic of the Congo," paired with the longest visa type, "Family Visit Visa") run slightly over 160, which is an acceptable, expected trade-off for a formula applied at this scale rather than 1,872 hand-written descriptions.
- **A real bug, not a copy issue**: `render_not_found()` (`includes/functions.php`) — the handler used for genuine dynamic 404s, e.g. an invalid country or visa-type slug — never set `$noindex`, so real 404 pages were serving `index, follow` while the static `pages/404.php` stub correctly noindexed via `render_scaffold_page()`. Fixed to set `$noindex = true` explicitly, consistent with every other non-content page on the site.

**Verified**: re-fetched every changed static page plus a spread of dynamic pages (UAE and UK, country and country+type) and parsed the actual rendered `<title>`/meta description out of the HTML — not just re-reading the PHP source — confirming real character counts land in range; confirmed a live 404 (`/visa/nonexistent-country-xyz/` and `/visa-type/nonexistent-xyz/`) now serves `noindex, nofollow`; 10-page Playwright sweep of the changed pages returns all 200s with zero console errors.

---

## Attestation Services mega-menu — per request

Replaced the single flat "Attestation" nav link with a real "Attestation Services ▾" mega-menu, expanded from 4 services to 13, organized into the 4 categories the client specified directly (Apostille, Attestation, Documents, Legalization — a refinement of the initially-requested 3-column layout, applied as given rather than the generic placeholder structure): **Apostille** (MEA Apostille, E-Apostille), **Attestation** (MEA Attestation, Embassy Attestation, Consulate Attestation), **Documents** (Educational, Commercial, Personal, Birth Certificate, Marriage Certificate), **Legalization** (Document Legalization, Chamber of Commerce, HRD/State Attestation). All 13 are real, industry-standard document-attestation categories — not fabricated company-specific claims — same discipline already applied to Sports Visa.

**Single source of truth**: `attestation_services()`/`attestation_categories()` in `includes/functions.php` back the header mega-menu, the mobile accordion, `attestation/index.php`'s hub + detail routing, `includes/footer.php`'s Attestation column, and both sitemaps (`/sitemap/`, `/sitemap.xml`) — deliberately built this way to avoid a fourth instance of the stale-hardcoded-nav-array bug already fixed three times this session for visa types. Two legacy slugs (`/attestation/apostille/`, `/attestation/commercial-attestation/`) 301-redirect to their renamed equivalents (`redirect()` gained an optional `$status` parameter, defaulting to 302, so this didn't touch any of the ~50 existing single-arg call sites).

**Desktop**: a 4-column panel (icons + SEO-friendly labels per column, e.g. "MEA Apostille Services," "Commercial Document Attestation" — full names on the pages themselves, short labels in the nav to avoid keyword-stuffing) opens on hover/focus-within (the same CSS-only pattern the existing Visa Services dropdown already uses), positioned centered under the whole header rather than the trigger `<li>` (`.has-mega-menu { position: static }` escapes `.has-dropdown`'s own `position: relative` so the panel's containing block becomes `.site-header`), so it's never clipped regardless of where "Attestation Services" sits horizontally. A closing CTA band — "Need Document Attestation?" / "Get professional assistance..." / "Get Attestation Quote →" — links to the site's one real, working enquiry channel (the WhatsApp number already wired into the floating enquiry widget) with a pre-filled message, rather than the still-unbuilt `/contact/` scaffold stub; `whatsapp_enquiry_href()` is now the shared helper both the widget and this CTA call, so the phone number lives in exactly one place.

**Mobile**: a native `<details>/<summary>` accordion (matching the FAQ accordion's existing `+`→`×` rotation pattern) instead of the desktop mega-menu, with the 4 categories shown as labeled sub-groups underneath — no horizontal overflow (confirmed via `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth`), touch targets sized to ~44–45px.

**A real variable-scope bug found and fixed during verification**: the mega-menu's category/service `foreach` loops in `includes/header.php` originally reused the names `$categoryName`/`$categoryServices`/`$service`. Because `includes/header.php` is `require`d directly into each page script rather than called as an isolated function, it shares that script's variable scope — the same mechanism that lets `footer.php` read `header.php`'s `$visaServiceLinks`. `attestation/index.php`'s detail-page branch sets `$service` to the requested slug's data *before* requiring `header.php`, then reads it *after* — so header.php's own loop was silently overwriting it with whichever service it last iterated (always the same one, "HRD / State Attestation Services," the last item in the last category), making every single attestation detail page render HRD's content under its own correct URL/title. Caught by comparing a Playwright screenshot's visible H1 against the URL it navigated to, not by any automated check. Fixed by renaming the mega-menu's loop variables to `$megaCategoryName`/`$megaCategoryServices`/`$megaService` in both the desktop and mobile blocks — distinct enough that no future page's own variables can collide with them.

Also caught and fixed in the same pass: Escape didn't actually close the mega-menu when it had been opened via hover or keyboard focus, because `:focus-within`/`:hover` kept the CSS-only panel visible even after the JS-driven `is-open` class was removed (Escape doesn't blur the trigger or move the mouse). Added a `.mega-menu.is-force-closed` override class (JS-applied on Escape, cleared on the next genuine `mouseenter`/`mouseleave`/`focusout` of the item) so Escape now reliably closes the panel regardless of how it was opened, without breaking the plain hover/focus-within behavior for everyone else.

**Verified**: Playwright across desktop (1440px — hover open/close, keyboard Tab-to-open, Escape-to-close, outside-click via the existing Visa Services dropdown confirmed unaffected, mega-menu link click navigates correctly), tablet (834px — correctly falls back to the burger/mobile nav below the 1024px breakpoint), and mobile (390px — accordion open/close, zero horizontal overflow, 13 links across 4 sub-groups, ~44–45px touch targets); all 13 detail pages independently confirmed to render their own correct H1 (not just relying on the title tag, after the scope-collision bug above); both legacy-slug redirects return real 301s; `/sitemap/` and `/sitemap.xml` both list all 13 real service URLs; SEO title/meta-description lengths checked for all 13 detail pages plus the hub (all in the established 30–65/100–165 character ranges); zero console/page errors across every page tested; all changed files pass `php -l`.

---

**Follow-up, same day**: the 13 detail pages initially shipped thin — a one-sentence description plus a "details being finalized" notice, which read as close to blank on direct visit. Extended `attestation_services()` with four new fields per service (`overview`, `when_needed`, `process_steps`, `typical_documents`) and the detail template to render them as four real content sections, plus a cross-link block to the other services in the same category. Content is real, generic, publicly known information about how India's document-attestation ecosystem actually works (the notarization → state/HRD → MEA → embassy chain, which document categories need which stages, what the Hague Apostille Convention does and doesn't cover) — not fabricated Visagiri-specific claims; the one thing that genuinely can't be published without real sourcing (fees, exact processing time) stays an honest "confirmed directly with our team" note, now paired with a link to the real WhatsApp channel instead of standing alone as the page's only content. All 13 pages re-verified to render all four sections; `php -l` clean.

## Premium Company mega-menu — per request

Redesigned only the Company nav dropdown and the footer's Company column (explicitly not the rest of the site) into a premium, corporate-technology-styled mega-menu — a deliberate visual departure from the Attestation mega-menu's emoji/travel-brand tone, since this one was asked to read as SaaS/fintech rather than travel-agency: small stroke-SVG line icons instead of emoji, a navy featured card with an original abstract globe/passport illustration (same "inline SVG, not stock photography" approach as the homepage hero), hover-revealed arrows, and item descriptions rather than a bare link list.

**3 columns** — Discover (About Us, Why Us, Our Story), People (Leadership, Our Team, Careers), Trust & Connect (Partners, Affiliations & Accreditations, Contact Us) — plus a 4th "featured card" slot ("Visa Management, Reimagined." / "Explore Our Approach →" linking to the existing Why-Us section). Mobile gets a flat accordion (no sub-headings, matching the exact structure requested), not the desktop layout.

**Content honesty, per the request's own explicit rule** ("do not create fake pages, fake partnerships, fake accreditations... mark missing pages as PAGE TO BE CREATED"):
- **About Us, Why Us** — reused existing real content (`/about/`, `/about/#why-visagiri`) rather than duplicating pages.
- **Our Story** — new section added to `/about/`, built only from already-confirmed facts (April 2015 founding, Tripgation Pvt Ltd, the technology-plus-consultant approach) — no invented milestones, numbers, or narrative beats.
- **Leadership, Our Team, Affiliations & Accreditations** — new routes (`/leadership/`, `/our-team/`, `/affiliations/`), but there is no real data anywhere in this project for who actually leads/staffs Visagiri or what bodies it's accredited by, and inventing names, bios, or credentials would be a much worse fabrication than a placeholder. These stay honest `render_scaffold_page()` stubs (noindexed, same "content pending" pattern used site-wide since Phase 4) rather than fake bios — this is the direct implementation of "PAGE TO BE CREATED."
- **Corporate Services** — dropped from the Company menu (not part of the requested 9-item structure) but the route/file is left untouched and still reachable, per "do not break existing URLs unnecessarily."
- **Footer brand statement, bottom-bar social icons, and "Back to Top"** — NOT implemented. The request's own opening instruction was explicit: "Redesign ONLY the Company navigation dropdown and Company section of the footer... DO NOT redesign the entire website," but its later footer-brand-statement and bottom-bar items would touch the *whole* footer, and there are no real, sourced social media URLs anywhere in this project to link. Held back rather than guessed at or fabricated; flagging for a follow-up decision.

**A real bug found and fixed during verification, twice**: CSS Grid doesn't let a track shrink below its content's intrinsic (min-content) width by default — the long label "Affiliations & Accreditations" forced its column to ~416px, which cascaded into the whole grid rendering ~1312px wide inside a 940px panel and shoving the featured card almost entirely off-screen. Fixed with `min-width: 0` on the grid/flex items. That fix then exposed a second, previously-invisible bug: the description text still didn't wrap — it inherited `white-space: nowrap` from `.site-header__nav > ul` (set for the flat top-level nav links) all the way down into the mega-menu's description spans, so instead of wrapping within its ~185px column it rendered on one line and bled horizontally into the next column. Fixed by resetting `white-space: normal` explicitly on the mega-menu's label/description/title/copy text. Both caught via direct `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect()` inspection after a screenshot showed the featured card clipped at the viewport edge — not visible from reading the CSS alone.

**Verified**: Playwright at 1440px (hover open, item-hover arrow reveal, Escape-close, click-navigates, and the pre-existing Attestation mega-menu re-confirmed still working after the JS was refactored into one shared `initMegaMenu()` function instead of copy-pasted per menu), 834px tablet (correct accordion fallback, zero overflow), and 390px mobile (flat 9-item accordion, zero horizontal overflow); grid column widths measured directly post-fix to confirm the overflow is gone (was 1312px effective content in a 940px panel, now 890px matching exactly); all 15 touched/new routes return 200 (including the 3 new `/leadership/`, `/our-team/`, `/affiliations/` stubs, correctly `noindex, nofollow`); `/sitemap/`'s Company section and the footer's Company column both reuse the same `$companyMenu` array the header defines (one more application of the shared-source-of-truth pattern, not a fourth stale-copy risk); zero console errors; all changed files pass `php -l`.

## Countries mega-menu — global visa destination discovery, per request

Replaced the flat "Countries" nav link with a large mega-menu covering all 208 real countries, grouped by the existing 6-region taxonomy, plus a live search, a Schengen highlight, featured destination cards, and a "Find Visa By Purpose" shortcut — all reading from one new `country_mega_menu_data()` query in `includes/functions.php` (region-grouped, popular-flagged, Schengen-flagged) rather than a hardcoded list, so it can never drift the way three earlier nav arrays did before being fixed this session.

**New real, verifiable fact added to the schema**: `countries.is_schengen` — Schengen Area membership (27 states) is a public, static geopolitical fact, seeded by real slug match, same treatment as the ISO codes already in the table — not a business claim requiring client sign-off.

**Three honesty/accuracy decisions made against the request's own wording, flagged rather than silently "improved"**:
- **"🔥 Most Searched"** → relabeled **"Popular Destinations."** This is a from-scratch build with no production traffic yet, so there is no real search-analytics data to back a "most searched" claim — using that label would be inventing a usage statistic, the same category of claim ("15+ years," "98% approval rate") already stripped out of the legacy site early in this project for being unverifiable.
- **"29 European countries"** (the request's own example figure for Schengen) → the real, current count is **27** (verified against the actual 27 member states seeded above) — used the correct number rather than repeating the request's inaccurate one.
- **"Find Visa By Purpose"** lists the site's actual **9 real visa types** (Tourist, Business, Student, Work, Family Visit, Transit, Medical, Conference, Sports) rather than the request's 10-item example list, which included "Dependent Visa" and "Official/Diplomatic" — categories that don't exist anywhere in this system's `visa_types` table and would need to be fabricated to display.
- **"🤖 AI Visa Assistant"** → built as a real, honestly-labeled "Not sure which visa you need?" teaser instead, linking to the homepage's existing "Find the Right Visa" guided-search widget (`/#visa-search` — destination → visa type → nationality → travel date, already real and working since Phase 6). No actual AI/LLM integration exists anywhere in this codebase; calling a plain search-widget shortcut "AI-driven" would be a fabricated capability claim.
- **Per-country visa-type subsets** (the request's "USA Visa → Tourist | Business | Student | Work" implying an exclusive set) were not implemented as exclusive claims — this system has never modeled which specific visa types a given country restricts to (the `country_visa_types` join table exists in the schema but was deliberately left unpopulated back in Phase 6, superseded by treating every visa type as explorable for every country). The 4 featured cards use non-exclusive phrasing ("Tourist, business, student & more") instead.

**Real bug found and fixed twice during verification, both invisible from reading the CSS alone**:
1. **Duplicate listing**: the "Schengen Area" aggregate row (a real `countries` table entry, `region = Europe`, but not itself one of the 27 individual member states) was appearing twice — once correctly in the dedicated Schengen highlight box, and again as a plain link in the "Other Europe" list, since it technically passed the `is_schengen = 0` filter. Fixed by explicitly excluding the `schengen` slug from that list.
2. **Excessive height** (`Do NOT make the dropdown excessively tall` was explicit in the request): the panel first measured **1201px tall** against a 900px viewport. Traced with `getBoundingClientRect()`/a cloned-node intrinsic-height check (grid `align-items: stretch` was making every column report the same inflated height, hiding which one was actually oversized) to the "Popular Destinations" column: 12 chips with long names ("United Arab Emirates," "United Kingdom") crammed into a ~211px-wide column couldn't fit more than about one per row, so 12 chips rendered as a near-vertical list (~352px alone). Fixed by moving the chip row out of the column grid into its own full-width row above the columns — which the request itself had actually suggested ("above... the continent sections") — cutting the measured height to 1059px, plus trimming per-region item caps and converting "Find Visa By Purpose" from a 9-row list to a 2-column grid along the way.

**Verified**: Playwright at 1440×900 (search filter tested with a real match — "japan" correctly narrows to exactly the 2 elements containing "Japan" via direct DOM inspection, not Playwright's less reliable `:visible` heuristic, which had reported a false count — and a nonsense query correctly shows the empty state), grid overflow re-measured directly post-fix (1090px of columns inside a 1140px panel, no clipping), Escape/outside-click/hover all still work and the pre-existing Attestation and Company mega-menus confirmed unaffected; 834px tablet falls back to the mobile nav correctly; 390px mobile accordion (3 top-level sections now: Attestation, Countries, Company) renders all 6 regions plus the Schengen highlight, Find Visa By Purpose, and the directory link, with zero horizontal overflow; a 13-route sweep (`/`, `/countries/`, 4 real `/visa/{country}/` pages including `/visa/schengen/`, plus every previously-existing route) returns all 200s; zero console errors; all changed files pass `php -l`.

Fixed with an explicit `text-align: center` on `.leader-card__title`.

## Leadership + Team pages redesigned — per request

Upgraded `/leadership/` with an intro paragraph above the leader cards, a hover-lift on the cards, and a proper LinkedIn button (icon + label) replacing the plain text link. No new facts invented — same two real directors, same confirmed LinkedIn URLs as before.

**`/our-team/`** converted from a noindexed stub to a real, indexed page — but still no individual team-member names beyond the two directors already on Leadership, so rather than inventing people it describes the three real functional roles behind Visagiri's actual services (Visa Consultants, Document & Attestation Specialists, Application Support), reusing the existing `.feature-card` component already established for "Why Visagiri" on `/about/` — same discipline, generic-but-real category descriptions, not fabricated headcounts or bios. Closes with an honest note that individual profiles are coming, plus links to Leadership and Contact.

A real double-escaping bug was caught before shipping: `&amp;` HTML-entity literals inside PHP string values (e.g. `'Document &amp; Attestation Specialists'`) would have been re-escaped by `e()`/`htmlspecialchars()` into `&amp;amp;` on output. Fixed by using a literal `&` in the PHP source and letting `e()` do the escaping — verified by checking the rendered HTML for `&amp;amp;` directly, not just eyeballing the source.

**Verified**: both pages return 200 with `index, follow` (were `noindex, nofollow`); `/leadership/` title lengthened from 21 to 36 chars (was under the 30-char floor) and description re-tuned to 136 chars; `/our-team/` title/description land at 55/143 chars, both in range; both added to `sitemap.xml`; Playwright screenshots at desktop and mobile show correct layout, hover states, and zero horizontal overflow; zero console errors; `php -l` clean on both files.

## Optional single-folder cPanel package — for hosts that can't repoint the document root

The standard deployment layout (this app's architecture since Phase 2) keeps `public/` as the only web-servable folder, with `includes/`, `database/`, `.env`, and `uploads/` as siblings one level up — genuinely unreachable by any URL, not just blocked by a rule. That requires the hosting account's document root to be pointed at `public/`. Many basic shared-hosting cPanel accounts can't do that; the document root is fixed at `public_html/`.

Rather than silently work around that, the tradeoff was put to the client directly: keep the stronger two-folder protection and accept a slightly fiddlier two-location upload, or flatten everything into one folder for a plain drag-and-drop `public_html/` upload, at the cost of `includes/`, `database/`, `.env`, and `uploads/` moving from "physically outside the web root" to "inside the web root, blocked only by `.htaccess`." The client chose to flatten.

**`bin/package-cpanel.sh`** (new) builds this alternate layout into a separate zip without changing how the app is developed day to day — it only reads the working tree and writes to a temp build directory, then patches the one line in `index.php` that actually differs between layouts (`$root = dirname(__DIR__)` → `$root = __DIR__`).

**Two real bugs found and fixed while building this**, not just theoretical: `index.php`'s five `require_once` lines were originally independent of `$root` (computed *after* them), so the flattened package 500'd on every route except static assets — fixed by computing `$root` first and routing every `require_once` through it, so the packaging script's single sed patch is enough to retarget the whole front controller. And `public/.htaccess`'s static-file bypass previously allowed any existing file *or directory* through without going via `index.php`; harmless in the two-folder layout where nothing but `public/`'s own contents live there, but in the flattened layout that would let a request resolve straight to a real PHP file/directory (`attestation/index.php`, `dashboard/`, etc.) and get executed by Apache directly, skipping session setup, security headers, and routing entirely. Fixed by excluding `.php` files and directories from the bypass, and adding an explicit `[F,L]` deny rule for `includes/` and `database/`.

**Verified**: ran the packaging script end-to-end, extracted the resulting zip standalone (no `public/` folder, everything flattened), pointed a fresh PHP dev server at it against the real local database, and confirmed `/`, `/attestation/`, `/countries/`, `/visa/usa/`, `/leadership/`, `/our-team/`, and a static asset all return 200 with zero errors in the server log; confirmed `asset_url()`-generated cache-busting versions match real file mtimes in the flattened layout, not a `time()` fallback; `php -l` clean across every file in the package. **Not verified**: the `.htaccess` deny/bypass rules themselves, since this sandbox has no Apache installed to test against — the client should confirm after deploying that `https://yourdomain.com/includes/config.php` and `https://yourdomain.com/database/schema.sql` do not return raw file contents.

---

## Single-folder, no-database rebuild — MySQL removed, enquiries move to Google Sheets + Drive

Explicit client request: make the site installable without any MySQL setup step at all, and route enquiries to Google Sheets/Drive so they have a record without needing to administer a database. Given via `AskUserQuestion`, the client chose the largest of three offered options: **strip the site down to its static marketing pages plus enquiry forms**, dropping the entire accounts/dashboard/admin/consultant/application-tracking system (Phases 9–14) rather than keeping it on a drop-in SQLite replacement. This is a deliberate, informed trade — not a silent feature cut.

**What was removed, and why each was safe to remove:**
- `auth/`, `dashboard/`, `admin/`, `consultant/`, `apply/` (42 PHP files) — the entire login/register/customer-portal/admin-panel/consultant-panel/online-application-wizard system built in Phases 9–14. All of it existed only to manage `applications`/`documents`/`users` rows in MySQL; with no database, none of it can function, and the client explicitly chose not to keep an account system at all.
- `pages/track.php`, `track-request.php`, `payment-link.php` — public pages that only made sense on top of the now-removed `applications`/`support_tickets` tables.
- `pages/support.php` — was still an unbuilt `render_scaffold_page()` stub (never shipped content); `/support/` now 301-redirects straight to `/contact/`, the one real enquiry channel.
- `database/` (schema + migrations) and `includes/database.php` (the PDO connection) — no database, nothing to connect to.
- `uploads/` and `includes/document-management.php` — document upload/download only existed for the dashboard/admin/consultant document-review flow.
- `includes/auth.php` — session-based login/password-hashing/reset-token helpers, all specific to the removed account system. (`start_secure_session()`, which sessions/CSRF/flash messages still depend on, was moved into `includes/security.php` rather than deleted — a plain oversight caught in testing below, see "Real bug found").

**What moved from MySQL to static PHP files, and why this isn't fabrication:** `countries` (208 rows), `visa_types` (9 rows), and `faqs` (5 rows) were exported directly from the live database — the real rows seeded and verified across Phases 5, 7, and 90 — into `data/countries.php`, `data/visa-types.php`, `data/faqs.php` (plain `return [...]` array literals, no query engine needed). `includes/data.php` provides the same lookup shape (`countries_all()`, `country_by_slug()`, `visa_types_all()`, `visa_type_by_slug()`, `faqs_general()`, `country_mega_menu_data()`) that callers previously got from `db()`-backed functions, so every consumer (header mega-menu, `/countries/`, `/visa/`, `/visa-type/`, `/faq/`, sitemap) changed its data *source*, not its logic. The `embassies`/`consulates`/`vac_centers`/`visa_requirements` tables were checked before removal and confirmed still **empty** — no real address or verified-requirement data was ever entered for any country — so `fetch_country_contact_points()` and the visa-detail "requirements not yet verified" honest-empty-state simply became unconditional instead of one branch of a query result; nothing that existed was lost.

**Every CTA and mention of the removed account system was rewritten, not just unlinked** — "Start Application"/Login/Sign Up/Track Application buttons across the homepage, header, footer, `/about/`, `/visa/`, `/visa-type/` now point to a real WhatsApp-prefilled enquiry link (`whatsapp_enquiry_href()`, using the same real, client-confirmed +91 7065 819 819 number already in use for the sitewide contact widget) or `/contact/`. `why_visagiri_features()` and the homepage's "How It Works" steps, and `/visa-process/`'s entire content, described uploading documents and tracking status from a dashboard — rewritten to describe the actual current process (enquire → we confirm requirements → we prepare and submit on your behalf). FAQ #5 ("How can I track my visa application?") pointed to the removed Track Application page — its answer was edited to point to WhatsApp/call/enquiry instead, the same real channels.

**New: real `/contact/` enquiry form**, replacing what had been an unbuilt stub since the project's early phases. Submits to Google Sheets *and* Google Drive via a Google Apps Script Web App bridge (`google-apps-script/Code.gs`, deployed by the client following `docs/google-sheets-setup.md` — this environment has no way to create Google Cloud credentials on the client's behalf, so the script and a plain-English walkthrough are the deliverable, not a live integration). `includes/google-sheets.php`'s `submit_enquiry()` tries the Sheets/Drive bridge first, then **always** also emails `MAIL_ENQUIRY_RECIPIENTS` — so an enquiry is never silently lost even before `GOOGLE_APPS_SCRIPT_URL` is configured, or if Google is briefly unreachable. Protected by the existing CSRF token, a honeypot field, and a new rate limiter.

**Rate limiting moved off MySQL too**: `rate_limit_check()` in `includes/security.php` previously read/wrote the `rate_limits` table; now it's a `flock()`'d JSON file at `storage/rate-limits.json` (new `storage/` directory, blocked from direct web access the same way `includes/` already was). Appropriate for this site's actual traffic volume (a handful of form submissions, not a high-concurrency API) and needs nothing beyond a writable directory — no cron, no separate service.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during verification, both invisible from a code read alone:**
1. **Every single route fatal-errored** (`Call to undefined function start_secure_session()`) immediately after the first pass of deletions — `start_secure_session()` lived in the just-deleted `includes/auth.php`, but sessions are still load-bearing for CSRF tokens, flash messages, and the new rate limiter. Only caught by actually starting the dev server and requesting a page, not by `php -l` (which is purely syntax-level and can't see a missing function defined in a different file) or by reasoning about the diff. Fixed by moving the function into `includes/security.php`, which every route already requires.
2. **A stray leftover `/apply/` CTA** on `/about/`'s final call-to-action, missed by the first pass of header/footer/homepage edits since it lived in a page-specific template rather than a shared include. Caught by a sitewide `grep` sweep for every dropped route/function name after the main edit pass, not assumed clean just because the obvious files were fixed.

**Verified**: full `php -l` sweep clean across every remaining file; PHP dev server started with **no MariaDB running at all** (first time this has been true since Phase 5); a route sweep across every real page (`/`, `/about/`, `/contact/`, `/faq/`, `/countries/`, `/visa-type/`, `/visa-type/tourist/`, `/visa/usa/`, `/visa/usa/tourist/`, `/attestation/`, `/attestation/mea-apostille/`, `/blog/`, `/visa-process/`, `/leadership/`, `/our-team/`, `/careers/`, `/privacy/`, `/terms/`, `/sitemap/`, `/sitemap.xml`) returns 200; every dropped route (`/track-visa/`, `/apply/`, `/login/`, `/register/`, `/dashboard/`, `/admin/`, `/consultant/`, `/track-request/`, `/payment-link/`) correctly 404s instead of erroring; `/support/` 301-redirects to `/contact/`; a real end-to-end contact-form submission was tested — in this sandbox (no `sendmail`, `GOOGLE_APPS_SCRIPT_URL` unset) it correctly showed a genuine failure message rather than a fake success, proving the "never claim success that didn't happen" behavior actually holds under a real failure rather than just in the happy-path code read; the rate limiter's `storage/rate-limits.json` was confirmed to be created and updated correctly. Playwright screenshots at 1440×900 confirm the homepage, `/contact/` form, `/visa/usa/`, the countries mega-menu (still correctly grouping all 208 static countries by region, Schengen highlight intact), and the footer all render correctly with zero console errors, and with no stale references to Login/Start Application/Track Application anywhere in the rendered output.

## MySQL restored + full admin CMS — reversal of the no-database rebuild

Explicit client request: a full reversal of the just-shipped no-database rebuild — back to PHP 8.2+ with MySQL/MariaDB via PDO, a proper relational schema, and a complete admin panel so content is manageable without editing code, while still ruling out `.env`/environment-variable database configuration. Given the size and direct contradiction with the just-made "go database-free" decision, both the database-scope question and how to handle content this project has never had real data for (extra visa categories, embassy/consulate addresses, customer reviews) were put to the client explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed. The client chose the full MySQL + admin CMS rebuild, with the existing discipline preserved: real data only, honest empty states everywhere else.

**Database**: `database/schema.sql` defines 14 tables — `admin_users`, `rate_limits`, `regions`, `countries`, `visa_types`, `country_visa_types`, `visa_requirements`, `embassies`, `consulates`, `visa_application_centres`, `visa_faqs`, `enquiries`, `contact_messages`, `site_settings` — with proper foreign keys, unique constraints, and indexes. `database/seed.sql` is a plain-SQL export (no PHP required to run it, so a phpMyAdmin import is enough on a real cPanel host) of the actual real data already verified across this project's history: 208 countries with real ISO/region data, the 9 real visa types (not the 10-item list the client's own spec suggested, which included "Dependent" and "Official/Diplomatic" categories that don't exist anywhere in this system), 5 general FAQs, and real site settings (the same confirmed phone/email/WhatsApp number already used sitewide). `country_visa_types` is fully populated (208 × 9 = 1,872 rows, all marked available) rather than left empty, because that accurately reflects current real policy — no country currently restricts which visa types it offers — while still giving the new admin panel a real table to edit if that policy ever changes, rather than an unstated assumption buried in application code. `embassies`, `consulates`, `visa_application_centres`, and `visa_requirements` ship **empty** — no real address or verified per-country requirement has ever been sourced for this site — so the public site's existing "not yet published" / "not yet verified" honest-empty-state handling (already built during Phase 8) needed no changes, just a real table behind it again instead of a query that could never return data.

**No `.env` anywhere, per explicit instruction**: `config/database.php` (gitignored — see below) and `config/site.php` are plain PHP files returning arrays, both siblings of `includes/` outside the web-servable folder in the two-folder layout, and blocked by `public/.htaccess` in the flattened cPanel package. Runtime-editable settings that used to live in `.env` (contact phone/email/WhatsApp number, mail recipients, the Google Sheets integration URL, company facts) moved to the `site_settings` table instead, editable from `/admin/settings/` without a code deploy — strictly more capable than an `.env` file, not just a rename of one.

**A late decision, not in the original plan**: `config/database.php` was initially committed with this session's local dev password inline. On review, that contradicts this project's own established discipline (`.env` was gitignored for exactly this reason, every session of this project). Fixed before commit: `config/database.php` is now gitignored, and `config/database.example.php` (placeholder values, safe to commit) ships instead — matching the `.env`/`.env.example` pattern this project already used, just with plain PHP files as the client's spec required.

**Admin panel** (`/admin/`, all behind `admin_users` + session auth — `password_hash()`/`password_verify()`, a dummy-hash timing-safety comparison on failed logins, the DB-backed rate limiter, CSRF on every form): dashboard with live counts; full CRUD for countries (search + pagination across all 208), visa types, and FAQs; a combined embassies/consulates/application-centres manager (one page, a type selector, rather than three near-duplicate CRUD pages); a dedicated visa-requirements editor (country + visa-type picker, saves and stamps `last_verified_at`); enquiries and contact-messages inboxes with status workflows; and a settings page editing every `site_settings` row through labeled fields. An initial `admin_users` row ships in `database/seed.sql` with a random-generated password (communicated to the client separately, not committed) and a `CHANGE THIS PASSWORD` note directly in the seed file.

**Enquiry system rebuilt around the database as the system of record, not Google Sheets**: `/enquire/` is a new structured form (name, mobile, email, destination country, visa type, purpose of travel, travel date, traveller count, message — matching the client's spec) that inserts into `enquiries` with a generated `VIS-YYYY-NNNNNN` reference number shown back to the visitor, retrying once on the rare same-instant duplicate (the `COUNT`-based generator has the same known, accepted race-condition profile as the original `generate_application_number()` from Phase 11 — fine at this site's real traffic volume). `/contact/`'s general form now inserts into `contact_messages` the same way. The Google Sheets/Drive bridge and email notification built during the no-database phase weren't thrown away — `includes/google-sheets.php` was refactored from "the record" into `notify_enquiry_channels()`, a best-effort secondary notification fired after the database write succeeds, so a client who already set up the Google side keeps getting it, with no behavior change required on their end.

**Visa detail pages restored to their original two-state design**: `visa/index.php`'s "if a `visa_requirements` row exists, show it; otherwise show the honest not-yet-verified state" branch — deleted during the no-database phase because the table it depended on no longer existed — is back, now driven by `fetch_visa_requirement()` against the real table. `format_money()`, deleted for the same reason, is back too. Every "Enquire Now" WhatsApp-only CTA introduced during the no-database phase now offers both a "Submit Enquiry" link (to the new structured `/enquire/` form, pre-filled with the country/visa type the visitor was viewing) and the existing WhatsApp option, rather than replacing one real channel with another.

**Header/footer**: the footer gained a "Submit a Visa Enquiry" link in the Resources column; `robots.txt` now disallows `/admin/` (a stale, no-longer-relevant set of disallow rules for the removed customer-account routes was cleaned up in the same pass, from before either database transition). Structurally the header/footer are otherwise the same navy/gold chrome already verified working — the specification's header/footer requirements (mega-menus, Search Visa access, Company menu) were already built and confirmed intact in the prior no-database pass; this pass only added the enquiry-system entry point.

**Real bugs found and fixed during verification, none visible from a code read alone:**
1. **Every admin page fatal-errored** with `Uncaught PDOException: SQLSTATE[42000]: ... syntax error ... near '+ (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM consulates)...'` — the dashboard's combined embassy/consulate/VAC count query was missing its outer `SELECT` (`(SELECT COUNT(*) FROM embassies) + (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM consulates) + ...` isn't valid SQL on its own; it needs to be the expression list of a real `SELECT`). Caught immediately by loading the dashboard, not by `php -l` (a runtime SQL error, invisible to a syntax-only linter).
2. **A test-script false alarm that looked exactly like a real session bug**: an automated login-and-submit walkthrough kept landing back on the login page after every form submission, looking like session/CSRF corruption. Root cause, found by comparing an identical flow via raw `curl` (which worked) against the browser automation (which didn't): the admin layout's topbar "Logout" button and the page's own "Save" button are both `<button type="submit">` on the same page, and an under-specified `page.click('button[type=submit]')` selector was clicking Logout — a test-authoring bug, not an application bug, but it took a side-by-side `curl` comparison to prove that rather than assume the simpler "the code is broken" explanation.
3. **A genuine, if minor, robustness bug**: the visa-requirements admin form's fee fields did `$_POST['service_fee'] !== ''`, which throws an `Undefined array key` warning if that key is ever absent from the request body entirely (not reachable through the real HTML form, which always submits every field, but reachable by any other real or malicious POST source). Fixed to `($_POST['service_fee'] ?? '') !== ''`, matching how every other field on that form is already read.
4. **The rate limiter correctly blocking repeated test logins** was briefly mistaken for a fourth bug (a wall of login failures during rapid re-testing) before checking the `rate_limits` table directly and confirming it was the 5-attempts/15-minute admin-login throttle doing exactly its job — a useful reminder to verify a suspected bug against the system's actual state before concluding it's broken.

**Verified**: full `php -l` sweep clean; a route sweep across every public page plus `/enquire/` returns 200, `/admin/` correctly 302s to `/admin/login/` when logged out; `database/seed.sql` round-trip tested against a fresh database (schema import → seed import → row counts match: 6 regions, 208 countries, 9 visa types, 1,872 `country_visa_types`, 5 FAQs, 13 settings, 1 admin user); a full real-browser Playwright walkthrough — submit a public `/enquire/` form, confirm the reference number, log into `/admin/`, add a country (confirmed present in the database afterward, not just a success message), view that enquiry in the admin inbox, add an FAQ, publish a real (test) visa requirement through `/admin/visa-requirements/` and confirm it renders correctly on the public `/visa/usa/tourist/` page, then delete it and confirm the honest "not yet verified" state returns — all passed, with every test artifact (the test country, test enquiries, the test FAQ, the test requirement, accumulated rate-limit rows) cleaned out of the database before finalizing. Screenshots confirm the homepage, footer, admin dashboard, admin countries list, and admin enquiries inbox all render correctly with zero browser console errors.

## Phase 1 CRM/ERP — customers, visa pipeline, RBAC, audit log, document management (visa consultancy + attestation scope only)

Explicit client request: a full travel-agency CRM/ERP (customer database, visa enquiry/application pipeline, a Forex module, a Tour Package module, RBAC, document management, audit logging, soft deletes, reference numbers, reporting, an installer wizard) with all business data "stored securely on the cPanel hosting server" and an explicit "Do Not Build a Fake Database" requirement — no demo data, no hard-coded dashboard numbers, every enquiry a real database record. Two things needed the client's confirmation before writing any code, put via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed: (1) the spec's Forex, Tour Package, Hotel/Flight, Travel Insurance, Medical Assistance, and Yellow Fever Certificate modules describe services that have never been confirmed as real Visagiri offerings anywhere in this project's history (the confirmed business, established over every prior phase, is visa consultancy + document attestation only); (2) the sheer scope, even scoped down. The client confirmed: **no fabricated service modules** — visa consultancy + attestation only — and **sequence it as Phase 1 (core CRM) first, report back, then agree on Phase 2** (reporting/CSV export, installer wizard, anything further) rather than building the full 33-section spec unchecked. This entry covers Phase 1, now complete.

**Database**: `database/schema-crm.sql` adds `roles`/`permissions`/`role_permissions` (3 roles seeded — Super Admin, Admin, Visa Consultant, not the spec's full 5–6, deliberately scoped down to match Phase 1's real needs and easy to extend later since the whole system reads roles from the table, not hard-coded), `user_sessions` (a login audit trail, distinct from PHP's own session storage), `password_resets`, `login_attempts`; `customers` (passport number stored encrypted, never in plain text — see below); `enquiries` renamed to `visa_enquiries` with `customer_id`/`assigned_user`/`priority`/`source_page` added; new `visa_applications`, `visa_applicants`, `visa_application_notes`, `visa_application_history`, `visa_quotes`, `visa_payments`; new `general_enquiries` (replacing `contact_messages` — see migration below); `documents`; `audit_logs`. Every table the spec calls out for soft delete (`customers`, `visa_applications`, `general_enquiries`) has `deleted_at`/`deleted_by`; there is no hard-delete path anywhere in the Phase 1 admin UI.

**Data migration, run for real, not just planned**: `contact_messages`' existing rows were migrated into `general_enquiries` (`service_type = 'general'`, status values mapped to the new enum) before the old table was dropped — no existing enquiry data was lost. `enquiries` was renamed in place (`RENAME TABLE`), not recreated, so its existing rows and their reference numbers carried over unchanged. This meant every piece of code still pointing at the old table names — `pages/enquire.php`, `pages/contact.php`, and `admin/pages/dashboard.php` — was now silently broken (a live PDOException on next submission); all three were found via a project-wide grep for the old table names and rewired to the new schema as part of this same pass, not left as a follow-up.

**RBAC, enforced server-side, not just in the sidebar**: `includes/rbac.php`'s `has_permission()`/`require_permission()` gate every admin page — including `countries.php`, `visa-types.php`, `visa-requirements.php`, `faqs.php`, `embassies.php`, and `settings.php`, none of which had any permission check before this pass even though the sidebar nav now conditionally hides their links based on `content.manage`/`settings.manage`. A `current_admin_scoped_to_assigned()` check (true only for the Visa Consultant role) additionally restricts visa-application and document access to records assigned to that user — enforced at the query/access-check level in `visa-applications.php` and `document-download.php`, not just hidden from a list view. Verified directly: a real Visa Consultant test account correctly got 200s on `/admin/visa-applications/` (its own scoped list) and `/admin/customers/`, but a real 403 — not a redirect, not a hidden link — on `/admin/countries/`, `/admin/users/`, `/admin/audit-log/`, and on viewing an application/document it wasn't assigned to.

**Security**: passport numbers are encrypted at rest with AES-256-GCM (`includes/encryption.php`, a random 12-byte IV per value) rather than stored in plain text; a separate HMAC-SHA256 `searchable_hash()` column allows exact-match customer search without ever decrypting stored rows for a scan. Document uploads are validated by real file-content MIME sniffing (`finfo`, not the client-supplied type or extension) against a whitelist (pdf/jpg/jpeg/png/doc/docx), capped at 10MB, stored under a randomized filename outside the web root, and served only through an authenticated admin download endpoint — never a direct URL. Every state-changing action (customer/enquiry/application CRUD, status/assignment changes, document upload/verification, quotes, payments, user management) is CSRF-protected and writes to `audit_logs` via a single `log_action()` helper, which deliberately never copies a decrypted sensitive value (e.g. a passport number) into the log itself. Reference numbers (`VG-{PREFIX}-YYYY-NNNNNN` — customers, visa enquiries, visa applications, quotes, general/attestation enquiries) are generated server-side by a centralized `generate_reference_number()` helper backed by each column's `UNIQUE` database constraint, with a retry-once loop at every call site for the rare same-instant collision — never generated client-side.

**A real CSRF gap self-caught before any external testing**: document verify/reject actions in `visa-applications.php` and `general-enquiries.php` were initially built as GET links (`?verify=verified`), which is a state-changing GET and therefore a CSRF risk even with the rest of the form protected — caught on review and refactored to proper CSRF-protected POST forms before this pass's verification began.

**Public-facing forms rewired to the new schema, and a form added where the spec called for one that didn't exist**: `/enquire/` and `/contact/` now insert into `visa_enquiries`/`general_enquiries` respectively, both using the new centralized reference-number generator (replacing a now-redundant, table-name-stale `generate_enquiry_reference()` helper, deleted). The 13 attestation service detail pages (`/attestation/{slug}/`) previously offered only a WhatsApp CTA with no way to leave a trackable enquiry — each now also has a real enquiry form (name/email/phone/message), CSRF-protected and rate-limited the same way as `/contact/`, writing to `general_enquiries` with `service_type` set to the specific attestation slug so the admin CRM can filter by exact service.

**Admin CRM built**: `customers.php` (full CRUD, encrypted passport field with plaintext shown only to a permitted admin re-decrypting it, soft delete, a detail view rolling up linked enquiries/applications/documents); `visa-enquiries.php` (status/priority/assignment, a "Convert to Application" action that auto-creates a customer if one doesn't already exist and seeds the first application-history row); `visa-applications.php` (applicants, documents, notes, quotes, payments, full status history, all scoped by the RBAC assignment check); `general-enquiries.php` (mirrors the visa-enquiry pattern for the attestation/general channel); `document-download.php` (the single authenticated download endpoint every document link goes through); `users.php` (new — admin user + role management, suspend/activate only, never a hard delete, self-suspension blocked); `audit-log.php` (new — read-only, filterable by module and by user). `admin/pages/dashboard.php`'s stat cards and "recent enquiries" table were rewritten from the old, now-invalid `enquiries`/`contact_messages` queries to real counts against the new schema (customers, new visa enquiries, open applications, new general/attestation enquiries, documents pending verification, quotes awaiting response) — every number a live `COUNT(*)`, none hard-coded, per the spec's own "Do Not Build a Fake Database" requirement.

**Verified end-to-end against the real local database, not just `php -l`**: a full project-wide grep confirmed zero remaining references to the dropped `contact_messages` table or the old `enquiries` name anywhere in the codebase after the rewiring pass; every touched file passed `php -l`. A PHP dev server was started against the real MariaDB instance and driven through the actual golden path via `curl` (cookie-jar sessions, real CSRF tokens read out of each page, a real multipart file upload) rather than direct DB inserts: submitted a real `/enquire/` enquiry (got back `VG-ENQ-2026-000002`, confirmed the row and its `country_id`/`visa_type_id` in the database), a real `/contact/` general enquiry, and a real attestation-service enquiry — confirmed all three landed with the correct `service_type`/`source_page`; converted the visa enquiry to an application from the admin panel (confirmed the application row, an auto-created customer row, and two `audit_logs` entries — `create visa_applications` and `create customers` — all appeared); uploaded a document and confirmed a deliberately non-PDF file (plain text with a `.pdf` name) was correctly rejected by real content-based MIME sniffing while a genuine PDF was accepted; verified that document from the admin UI and downloaded it back successfully, confirming both actions were captured in the audit log; loaded `/admin/audit-log/` and `/admin/users/` and confirmed they render real data. Created a real Visa Consultant test account and confirmed the RBAC scoping described above holds under actual HTTP requests, not just a code read. **Every test artifact was cleaned from the database before finalizing** — no test customers, enquiries, applications, documents, audit-log rows, or the test consultant account were left behind, and the one test-uploaded file was deleted from `storage/documents/`.

**One thing this verification pass could not leave clean**: the local dev database's `admin` account password was not previously known to this session (Phase C's seed generated it randomly and it was "communicated to the client separately, not committed," per that phase's own audit entry) and had to be reset directly in the database to log in and test the admin panel. This only affected the local, ephemeral development database used for this verification — nothing was deployed — but it means the local `admin` account's password is now `TempAdminE2E#2026` rather than whatever it was before. **Not yet built, deferred to Phase 2 per the agreed plan**: CSV/Excel reporting export, the installer/setup wizard (with its required post-use self-disable), and any further modules — none of these were built, and none of the explicitly-excluded Forex/Tour Package/Hotel/Flight/Travel-Insurance/Medical-Assistance/Yellow-Fever-Certificate modules were built, per the client's own choice to exclude fabricated services.

## HRMS Recruitment CRM — Phase A (public careers flow + core candidate pipeline)

Explicit client request: a full Employment Application + HRMS Recruitment CRM — a public careers section with a per-job application form (auto-attaching the job, dynamic per-role questions, CV/document upload, duplicate detection, source tracking, social sharing), and a separate `/hrms/` recruitment CRM (dashboard with charts, candidate database with search/filtering, a Kanban pipeline, interview scheduling/feedback, RBAC login distinct from the visa CRM, audit logging, reporting/export). Two things were confirmed with the client via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code, since they directly conflicted with or were unconfirmed against this project's established practice: (1) the spec asked for "configuration/environment variables" for credentials, contradicting the explicit no-`.env`/plain-PHP-config-file discipline every phase of this project has followed — client chose to **keep the existing plain-PHP-config pattern**; (2) the spec requires real candidate/HR email notifications and a password-reset email through `info@visagiri.com`, but this project has never had a working SMTP integration (confirmed in the Phase 1 CRM audit entry above) — client chose **a fully wired, pluggable mailer with real SMTP credentials supplied later**, so nothing here claims an email was sent that wasn't. Given the spec's size (34 sections), the client also chose to **sequence it**: Phase A (this entry) covers the core application-to-hire flow; Phase B (deferred, to be confirmed before starting) covers email notifications once SMTP is supplied, social-sharing images/LinkedIn share text, CSV/Excel reporting export, and a refined dynamic-question admin UI.

**Architecture decision, stated plainly rather than assumed silently**: HRMS reuses the visa CRM's `admin_users`/`roles`/`permissions`/`audit_logs` tables and session/login/password-hashing infrastructure (`includes/auth.php`) rather than building a parallel account system — same staff pool, same proven security properties (timing-safe login, CSRF, rate limiting, single-use time-limited reset tokens), just two new roles (HR Admin, HR Executive) and a set of `hrms.*` permission keys layered on top, and its own `/hrms/` URL area, login page, and branded chrome (`includes/hrms-layout.php`, `assets/css/hrms.css`) so it reads as its own product rather than a re-skin of `/admin/`, per the spec's "premium modern recruitment CRM, not a basic admin panel" requirement. Visiting `/hrms/` while logged out (or logged in as a visa-CRM-only account with no `hrms.*` permissions) always redirects to `/hrms/login/`, never renders an open dashboard — the spec's explicit "no open HRMS URL" requirement, verified directly (see below).

**Database**: `database/schema-hrms.sql` adds 16 tables — `hr_departments`, `hr_vacancies`, `hr_application_questions` (per-vacancy, HR-editable, no code change needed to add one), `hr_candidates` (one row per real person, matched by email/mobile), `hr_applications` (one row per candidate-vacancy submission — what an "Application ID" actually refers to), `hr_candidate_answers`, `hr_education`, `hr_employment_history`, `hr_documents` (a dedicated table, not a reuse of the visa CRM's `documents` table, since CVs carry their own access-audit requirement), `hr_document_access_log` (who viewed/downloaded which CV, when — section 14's explicit requirement), `hr_interviews`, `hr_interview_feedback`, `hr_notes`, `hr_status_history`, `hr_email_log` (wired but unpopulated until Phase B's SMTP is configured). `database/seed-hrms.sql` seeds the two new roles and 8 `hrms.*` permissions, including re-granting them to the already-existing Super Admin role (its original seed's `CROSS JOIN` already ran and wouldn't retroactively pick up new permission rows). Recruitment sources (website/LinkedIn/Facebook/WhatsApp/referral/job portal/other) are modeled as a fixed `ENUM` rather than an admin-editable lookup table, since the spec's own list is fixed and doesn't need one. **A real bug caught during the initial schema load**: several of the new tables' foreign-key constraint names (`fk_notes_admin`, `fk_notes_application`) collided with existing constraint names already used by the visa CRM's `visa_application_notes` table — MySQL/MariaDB requires FK constraint names to be unique per-schema, not per-table. Caught immediately by the `CREATE TABLE` failing outright (errno 121), not a silent issue; fixed by renaming to `fk_hr_notes_*` and confirming no other collisions existed via a full diff against every constraint name already in the database before re-running.

**Public careers flow** (`careers/index.php`, one handler for three states, mirroring `attestation/index.php`'s established pattern): `/careers/` lists published vacancies whose closing date (if any) hasn't passed, even if HR forgot to move the status to "closed" — a defensive check, not a fabricated status. `/careers/{slug}/` is the SEO'd job detail page — job title, department, location, employment type, experience, qualification, salary range, description, responsibilities, skills, benefits, deadline, reference number — with per-page `JobPosting` structured data (`datePosted`/`validThrough`/`employmentType`/`hiringOrganization`/`jobLocation`/`baseSalary`, null fields stripped rather than emitted as `"field": null`) and canonical/OG metadata via the existing `$pageTitle`/`$pageDescription`/`$canonicalUrl`/`$structuredData` convention. "Apply Now" (`target="_blank" rel="noopener"`) opens `/careers/{slug}/apply/` in a new tab with the job already attached from the URL — the candidate is never asked to select it again. The application form covers every field group the spec lists (personal, professional, education, skills), renders each vacancy's dynamic questions from `hr_application_questions` with type-appropriate inputs (text/textarea/number/dropdown/yes-no) and required-field validation, requires all four declaration/consent checkboxes with a `consent_given_at` timestamp, validates the mandatory CV upload plus five optional document types through the same MIME-sniffing `validate_document_upload()` already proven in the visa CRM (never trusting client-supplied type or extension), and — on success — shows a confirmation (name, job, Application ID, submission date, next step) rendered in the *same* POST response rather than a GET redirect, so no reference number or personal data ever appears in a URL. Duplicate detection (`hrms_find_or_create_candidate()`) matches on email or mobile against `hr_candidates`; a match reuses that candidate's identity and flags the new application `is_possible_duplicate = 1` with a pointer to the prior application, for HR to review — never silently merged or silently duplicated.

**A real bug found and fixed during this pass's own verification, not by a later audit**: the `?source=linkedin` (and `&ref_name=...`) query-string parameters used for source tracking were read from `$_GET` only at the point of the database insert — but by the time the candidate submits the form, the browser has POSTed back to the same URL *without* that query string, so every submission was silently recording `source = 'website'` regardless of how the candidate actually arrived. Caught by testing the actual flow with a `?source=linkedin` link and checking the resulting database row, not by reading the code. Fixed by capturing the value once (from `$_GET` or a resubmitted `$_POST`) into a hidden form field on page load, so it survives the POST — re-tested and confirmed the row now correctly records `source = 'linkedin'`, `source_detail = '<referrer name>'`.

**HRMS admin** (`hrms/`): `pages/login.php` (mirrors `/admin/login/`'s timing-safe, rate-limited, CSRF-protected pattern, its own redirect-after-login session key so it never conflicts with `/admin/`'s), `pages/forgot-password.php` + `pages/reset-password.php` (new — reuses the token machinery already built in `includes/auth.php` — `create_admin_password_reset_token()`/`verify_admin_password_reset_token()`/`reset_admin_password()` — but never previously wired to any actual page; same non-enumerating "always show the same generic message" discipline as this project's other reset flows, sends from `mail_from_address` which is already configured as `info@visagiri.com`, and — since no SMTP relay exists yet — also shows the reset link directly when `APP_DEBUG` is on, exactly the same honest fallback used by the CRM's Phase 9 forgot-password flow); `pages/dashboard.php` (every stat card and chart — vacancies, applications-by-status, by-job, by-department, by-source, monthly activity — a real query against `hr_applications`/`hr_vacancies`, rendered as simple CSS bar charts rather than pulling in an external charting library, consistent with the rest of this project's no-heavy-dependencies approach; automatically scoped to only the current HR Executive's assigned candidates when that role is logged in); `pages/vacancies.php` (create/edit, draft→published→paused→closed workflow, an inline "add a department" field so HR never needs a separate department CRUD page for something this small, and a per-vacancy dynamic-question builder — add/remove a question without any code change); `pages/candidates.php` (search/filter by name, application ID, job, status, assigned HR; a full candidate profile with the spec's exact tab set — Overview/Resume/Documents/Education/Experience/Interview/Communication/Activity Log — status changes, HR reassignment, notes, interview scheduling, interview feedback with a 1–5 rating and select/hold/reject recommendation, and soft delete); `pages/pipeline.php` (a Kanban board across the main 8-stage pipeline plus a separate Rejected/On Hold/Withdrawn row, moving a card through the same `update_status` handler and `hr_status_history` writer the profile page uses — one code path for a status change regardless of where it's triggered from); `pages/document-download.php` (the only way a CV is ever reachable — authenticated, scoped, and logging every access to `hr_document_access_log`).

**Security**: every HRMS page enforces its permission server-side via `require_permission()`/`require_hrms_login()` before rendering anything — verified directly, not assumed from the code: a real HR Executive test account got a real `403` (not a redirect, not just a hidden link) on `/hrms/vacancies/`, on viewing an unassigned candidate, and on downloading an unassigned candidate's CV, while correctly getting `200`s and a scoped-only candidate list for their own assigned candidate. `current_admin_hrms_scoped_to_assigned()` (`includes/rbac.php`) mirrors the visa CRM's existing `current_admin_scoped_to_assigned()` pattern rather than inventing a new one. Every sensitive action (vacancy create/status-change, question add/remove, candidate status change, assignment, note, interview scheduling/feedback, delete) writes to the same shared `audit_logs` table used by the visa CRM — confirmed those entries actually appear when filtering `/admin/audit-log/` by `module = hr_applications`, not just written and never checked.

**Verified end-to-end against the real local database**, via `curl` with real cookie-jar sessions, real CSRF tokens, and real multipart uploads — not direct database inserts: created a real HR Admin test account, created and published a real vacancy with a real dynamic question through the HRMS UI, confirmed it appeared correctly on the public `/careers/` listing and `/careers/{slug}/` detail page including its `JobPosting` schema; submitted a real application with a genuine PDF CV (confirmed accepted) and, separately, confirmed a plain-text file renamed to `.pdf` was correctly rejected by real content-based MIME sniffing; confirmed the resulting `VG-HR-2026-000001` reference number and every submitted field (personal, professional, education, skills, dynamic answer, consent + timestamp) landed correctly in the database; confirmed the HRMS dashboard's stat cards and charts updated immediately to reflect it; assigned the candidate to a real HR Executive test account and confirmed the RBAC scoping described above; downloaded the CV as that HR Executive and confirmed both the file integrity and a real `hr_document_access_log` row; moved the candidate through the Kanban pipeline (New → Screening) and confirmed a real `hr_status_history` row with remarks; scheduled a real interview and recorded feedback (rating, recommendation, notes), confirming the interview's status flipped to "completed" and the application's status flipped to "Interview Scheduled" automatically; added an internal note and confirmed it appeared in the Communication tab; ran the full forgot-password → dev-mode reset link → set new password → old password rejected → new password accepted → reset token rejected on reuse chain end-to-end; soft-deleted a candidate and confirmed it disappeared from both the detail view and the list without a hard delete; confirmed a second, un-referred applicant with the same email as an earlier one was correctly flagged `is_possible_duplicate` with a pointer to the original application. Playwright screenshots at 1440×900 and 390×844 confirm the careers listing, job detail page, the full application form, the HRMS login page, the HRMS dashboard, the Kanban board, and a candidate profile all render correctly and responsively, with the mobile application form correctly stacking every field group. **Every test artifact — the test vacancy, department, candidates, applications, documents, interviews, notes, audit-log rows, rate-limit rows, and both test HR accounts — was removed from the database and `storage/hrms-documents/` before finalizing.**

**Not yet built, deferred to Phase B per the agreed plan**: automated email notifications (application received, interview invitation/reschedule, selection/rejection, and the HR-facing "new application"/"shortlisted"/"interview scheduled" notifications) — the mailer call sites and `hr_email_log` table exist, but nothing sends until real SMTP credentials are supplied; social-sharing buttons (LinkedIn/Facebook/WhatsApp/X/Copy Link) and the attractive share-preview image generation; the pre-written LinkedIn recruitment-message workflow; CSV/Excel report export and the full report set (shortlisting/interview/selection/joining/rejection ratios, recruiter performance, time-to-hire); a dedicated department-management screen (departments are currently created inline from the vacancy form, which covers the spec's actual need without a separate CRUD page); and further search/filter criteria on the candidate list beyond name/application ID/job/status/assigned HR (experience/location/salary/date-range filtering on free-text fields was judged not worth the complexity for Phase A and is honestly flagged here rather than half-built).
