## Forex Buy / Foreign Currency Purchase module — Phase A (core flow: request intake, compliance, quotations, delivery gate)

Explicit client request: a full Forex Buy Quotation, Documentation, Compliance & Request Tracking module. This directly reversed an earlier explicit decision — when this project's Phase 1 CRM scope was confirmed, the client chose "None — just visa consultancy + attestation" when asked which of Forex/Tour Packages/Hotel/Flight/Travel Insurance/Medical Assistance/Yellow Fever Certificate modules were real Visagiri services, explicitly excluding Forex. Given that direct reversal, plus the spec's own scale (39 sections: quotation engine with rate snapshots, multi-level approval, payment tracking, denomination management, delivery compliance gate, FEMA/audit records, declaration templates, country-rules admin, customer portal, full reports/export), both points were confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before any code was written: the client confirmed Forex is now a real, active/planned Visagiri service, and chose to sequence the build the same way the visa CRM and HRMS builds went — **Phase A (this entry): request intake, document verification, quotations with immutable rate snapshots, the compliance-gated delivery control, RBAC, audit trail, and a live dashboard.** Deferred to Phase B: payment gateway integration, denomination management, PDF/WhatsApp/email quotation delivery, declaration template versioning with variable substitution, a friendlier country-rules admin workflow, and full reports/export.

Immediately after this, the client also sent a 39-section "Unified Enquiry CRM + SLA Assignment System" spec proposing a generic `enquiries`/`enquiry_types`/`enquiry_fields` architecture that would replace the visa CRM's `visa_enquiries`/`general_enquiries` tables and layer an SLA/auto-assignment/escalation/workflow-automation engine on top of everything, including Forex — and it re-listed Travel/Flight/Hotel/Travel Insurance/Medical Assistance/Yellow Fever Certificate as first-class enquiry categories, the same non-confirmed services excluded twice already. Rather than derail the in-progress Forex build or silently start replacing already-shipped infrastructure, this was flagged and confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: the client agreed to finish Forex Phase A first and scope the Unified Enquiry CRM separately afterward, and confirmed the enquiry-category list for that future phase should stay limited to confirmed real services (Visa, Forex, Apostille/Attestation, General) rather than the six unconfirmed ones. That work has not started — this entry covers Forex Phase A only.

**Architecture decision**: unlike HRMS (which the client's own spec required at a separate `/hrms/` URL with its own login), this spec frames Forex as "CRM menu: Forex → ..." within the existing CRM, and section 2's "Search Existing Customer... in CRM" plus section 35's Step-1-through-Step-9 wizard (Customer → Travel → Visa → Forex Requirement → Documents → Quotation → Compliance → Payment → Delivery) describes a staff-driven workflow, not a public self-service form — a real customer would never fill in their own "Approval" or "Delivery" step. So Forex lives at `/admin/forex-*` (new admin sub-pages, same session/login/RBAC as the rest of the CRM, added as a new "Forex" section in the existing sidebar), with the one genuinely public piece being `/forex/track/` (reference number + registered mobile, non-enumerating, mirroring this project's other public lookup pages — `/track-visa/` itself no longer exists in this codebase, having been removed during an earlier no-database phase and never restored, so this was built fresh from the same established pattern rather than literally copied).

**Database**: `database/schema-forex.sql` adds 10 tables — `forex_rates` (admin-maintained buy/sell rates, never auto-fetched from a live API in this phase), `forex_country_rules` (admin-editable, drives the automatic document checklist per section 18 — ships completely empty, since this project never asserts a country's visa requirement as fact without an authorized user entering it; a country with no rule yet falls back to the full standard checklist, never to "nothing required"), `forex_requests`, `forex_documents` (verification fields live directly on the document row rather than a separate `forex_document_verifications` table — simpler, still fully satisfies "every document must have a verification status" since it's a strict 1:1 relationship), `forex_declarations`, `forex_quotations` (every quotation is a permanent, immutable snapshot — a rate change in `forex_rates` never touches an existing row here), `forex_approvals`, `forex_payments`, `forex_deliveries` (total amount only in Phase A — denomination breakdown is Phase B), `forex_status_history`. `customers` gained `pan_number_encrypted`/`pan_number_hash` via `ALTER TABLE`, reusing the exact same AES-256-GCM + searchable-hash pattern already used for passport numbers (`includes/encryption.php`) — PAN is exactly as sensitive and gets exactly the same treatment, not a new one. `database/seed-forex.sql` adds four new roles (Forex Manager, Forex Executive, Compliance/Audit, Accounts) and 15 `forex.*` permissions; the spec's fifth role, "Sales/Travel Consultant," was **not** built as a new role — the existing `visa-consultant` role already matches that exact description (scoped to assigned records, create-only, no approval rights), so it was simply granted the relevant `forex.*` permissions instead of creating a near-duplicate role. `current_admin_forex_scoped_to_assigned()` (`includes/rbac.php`) mirrors the already-proven `current_admin_hrms_scoped_to_assigned()` pattern rather than inventing a new one.

**The compliance-gated delivery control (section 17, the spec's own "extremely important" requirement)**: `forex_delivery_blockers()` (`includes/forex.php`) is the single source of truth for whether a request can be delivered — it re-fetches everything itself from the database rather than trusting caller-supplied state, so the document-completion checklist a staff member sees can never disagree with what the hard gate actually enforces. It checks, in order: customer identity (passport + PAN numbers) on file; passport and PAN documents verified; air ticket and hotel verified where the country rule (or the safe default) requires them; **visa copy verified when visa status is "Visa Available" or "eVisa," or a hand-signed, verified Visa-on-Arrival/Visa-Free declaration when visa status is "Visa on Arrival" or "Visa-Free"** — the spec's core visa-vs-declaration branch; an outright block when visa status is "Not Available," "In Process," or "Other," using the spec's exact warning text; payment confirmed as `paid`; and a completed approval. `admin/pages/forex-requests.php`'s `mark_delivered` action enforces this same function server-side before allowing delivery — a user without `forex.delivery.manage` gets a real `403`, not a hidden button (verified directly, see below). An authorized override (a distinct `forex.delivery.override` permission) is possible, but requires a written reason and writes a permanent `forex_approvals` row capturing the exact list of blockers overridden, the reason, the approver, and the IP address — never a silent bypass.

**File naming and document history**: uploads are renamed to `{reference}-{DOCTYPE}-{random}.ext` (e.g. `VG-FX-2026-000001-PASSPORT-39789bef01966b3c.pdf`) per section 28, never the customer's original filename. Replacing a document (e.g. after a rejection) creates a new row and marks the old one `is_current = 0` with a `deleted_at` timestamp — full history preserved, exactly per section 9's requirement, verified directly (see below).

**A real, pre-existing bug found and fixed during this pass's verification — not introduced by this module, but exposed by it**: `admin/pages/forex-requests.php`'s search query reused the same named PDO placeholder (`:search`) six times in one `OR`-chained `WHERE` clause. This project's database connection explicitly sets `PDO::ATTR_EMULATE_PREPARES => false` (real, non-emulated prepared statements) precisely because emulated prepares can mask SQL-handling bugs — but real prepared statements do not support the same named placeholder appearing more than once in a single query, and hit it with a fatal `PDOException: Invalid parameter number` the moment a real search term was typed. A project-wide grep for the same anti-pattern found it already present, unnoticed, in **three previously-shipped files**: `admin/pages/customers.php` (Phase 1 CRM), `hrms/pages/candidates.php` (HRMS Phase A), and this session's own `admin/pages/forex-fema-audit.php` — every one of them would have thrown the identical fatal error the first time a real user typed a non-empty search term, and none of it was caught by any previous verification pass because none of them had actually exercised those search boxes with a real term end-to-end. Fixed in all four files by giving each placeholder occurrence its own name (`:search1`, `:search2`, …) bound to the same value, then re-verified the customer search, HRMS candidate search, and forex reference-number search all now return real results with zero errors.

**Verified end-to-end against the real local database**, via `curl` with real cookie-jar sessions, real CSRF tokens, and real multipart uploads, working through every one of the spec's own 10 named test scenarios: **Test 1** (full happy path) — created a real Forex Manager account, added a real USD rate, created a request with `visa_available` status, uploaded and verified all five required documents (passport/PAN/air ticket/hotel/visa), created a quotation (2,000 USD × ₹83.25 = ₹166,500 base + ₹826 charges = ₹167,326 total, calculated correctly), approved it, recorded payment as `paid`, and successfully marked it delivered once every blocker cleared. **Test 2** — a request with `visa_available` but no visa document uploaded correctly blocked delivery with "Visa copy not uploaded." **Test 3** — a `visa_on_arrival` request correctly required a signed declaration instead of a visa copy in both the checklist and the delivery gate; uploading and verifying the declaration cleared that specific blocker. **Test 4** — an unverified PAN document correctly kept the request blocked ("PAN copy is uploaded but not yet verified"). **Test 5** — rejecting the passport document correctly blocked delivery with the rejection reason surfaced. **Test 6** — after creating the quotation, the master USD rate was changed from 83.25 to 90.00 in the rate table; the existing quotation's rate and total amount were confirmed completely unchanged, proving the immutable-snapshot design works under a real rate change, not just in theory. **Test 7** — a real Forex Executive test account (lacking `forex.delivery.manage`) attempted delivery directly via a forged POST and was correctly rejected with a genuine `403`, with the request status unchanged. **Test 8** — the Forex Manager overrode a blocked delivery with a written reason; confirmed a permanent `forex_approvals` row was created capturing the exact blocker list, the reason, the approver's ID, and the IP address. **Test 9** — replacing a rejected passport document was confirmed to leave the old row intact (`is_current = 0`, `deleted_at` set) while the new upload became current and `pending` again. **Test 10** — searching by `VG-FX-2026-000001` returned the request, and its FEMA/audit record view showed the complete status history from creation through delivery. Additionally confirmed: the public `/forex/track/` page shows the correct customer-safe timeline for a matching reference+mobile pair and the same generic "couldn't find a matching" message for a mismatched mobile (non-enumerating, per this project's established pattern); a delivered transaction correctly could not be deleted (no delete button rendered, and a forged delete POST was also rejected server-side). **Every test artifact — all test requests, customers, documents, quotations, approvals, payments, deliveries, declarations, rates, audit-log rows, and both test Forex accounts — was removed from the database and `storage/forex-documents/` before finalizing.**

**Not yet built, deferred to Phase B per the agreed plan**: payment gateway integration (Phase A is manual status entry only); denomination-level delivery breakdown (`forex_denominations` — Phase A records only the total amount delivered); customer-facing PDF quotation generation and the Download PDF/WhatsApp/Email send buttons; declaration template management with `{{variable}}` substitution and version history (Phase A supports uploading and verifying a signed declaration with its required metadata, but not a generated, admin-editable template); a friendlier country-rules admin workflow (the current one is a functional but plain CRUD form); automated candidate/HR email notifications (same honest constraint as HRMS — no SMTP configured yet); full reports (currency-wise, country-wise, pending-compliance, delivery, payment, audit/FEMA) and CSV/Excel/PDF export — the FEMA/audit view built in Phase A is read-only; and the customer portal tie-in. This is also explicitly a record-management tool, not a claim of FEMA/RBI legal compliance — every compliance-facing screen says so directly, and the country-rules table ships empty specifically so no legal assumption is baked in without the company's own authorized compliance/legal review.

## Primary header nav — icons added to every top-level tab

Client request: add icons to the main site header's top-level nav tabs (Visa Services, Attestation, Countries, Visa Process, Visa Updates, Company). A new `primary_nav_icon()` helper (`includes/functions.php`) was added rather than reusing `company_nav_icon()` directly, but deliberately matches its exact visual language — 16px, `viewBox 0 0 20 20`, `stroke="currentColor"`, `stroke-width="1.6"`, rounded caps/joins — so a top-level tab icon and the icons inside its own mega-menu (e.g. Company's trigger icon next to the "About Us"/"Leadership"/"Partners" icons in its mega-menu) read as one consistent icon system rather than two unrelated sets. Six icons were designed: a passport-style card for Visa Services, a certificate-with-checkmark for Attestation, a globe (circle + meridian ellipse + equator line) for Countries, two connected nodes for Visa Process (reflecting the step-by-step nature of that page), a bell for Visa Updates, and a two-building skyline (reusing the existing "building" visual idea already established in the Company mega-menu) for Company.

`includes/header.php` was updated on both the desktop nav and the mobile accordion menu — six insertion points each — wrapping each icon+label in a `.site-header__nav-icon` span. One layout bug was caught before shipping: the three mobile `<summary>` accordion triggers (Attestation, Countries, Company) rely on `display:flex; justify-content:space-between` to keep their label on the left and the "+"/"×" expand indicator on the right; dropping a bare icon span directly inside `<summary>` would have made it a third flex child and broken that spacing. Fixed by wrapping icon+label together in a single `.site-header__mobile-summary-label` span so the layout still sees exactly one content item plus the indicator.

`public/assets/css/layout.css`: top-level nav links became `inline-flex` (desktop) / `flex` (mobile) containers with a small gap; `.site-header__nav-icon` defaults to a muted color and transitions to the brand blue in sync with its parent link's own hover/active color rule, so icon and label always change color together rather than looking like two separate elements. Changing `.site-header__mobile a` from `display:block` to `display:flex` was checked against the existing CSS first — the more specific `.site-header__mobile-accordion-body li a` rule already overrides `display:block` for links nested inside an opened accordion, so this change only affects the six true top-level mobile items as intended.

**Verified visually** with Playwright at both 1440×900 and 390×844: all six desktop icons render aligned with their labels with no overflow; hovering the Company tab correctly turns both its icon and label blue while opening its mega-menu; the mobile hamburger menu shows all six icons correctly aligned, and opening the Attestation accordion confirms the icon/label/indicator layout holds with the panel expanded (the accordion body's own pre-existing category icons continue to render underneath, unaffected). No functional code was touched — this is markup/CSS/icon-helper only.

## Footer column headings — icons added, matching the header nav

Client follow-up request: extend the same icon treatment to the footer's column headings (Visa Services, Attestation, Countries, Company, Resources). Rather than design a second icon set, `primary_nav_icon()` (`includes/functions.php`, added in the header pass above) was reused as-is for the four headings it already covers — a "Visa Services" icon in the footer is the identical SVG as the header's "Visa Services" tab icon, reinforcing one icon system across the whole site rather than two. One new key, `resources` (an open-book glyph, same 16px/`viewBox 0 0 20 20`/`stroke=currentColor` spec as the rest), was added for the one footer-only column that has no header-nav counterpart.

`includes/footer.php`: each of the five `<h3>` column headings got an icon span (`<span class="site-footer__col-icon">`) inserted before its label; the Brand column (logo + tagline) was left untouched since it isn't a labeled list column.

`public/assets/css/layout.css`: `.site-footer__col h3` became a `flex` container (`align-items:center; gap`) so the icon sits inline with the uppercase heading text without disturbing the existing `white-space:nowrap` rule that keeps every column heading on one row; `.site-footer__col-icon` colors the icon `var(--visa-gold)` — a static accent rather than the header nav's hover-driven color, since footer headings aren't links and don't have a hover/active state to sync against.

**Verified visually** with Playwright at 1440×900 and 390×844. Desktop: all five icons render in gold next to their headings, correctly aligned, with the five-column row still lined up. Mobile: confirmed via viewport screenshots at precise scroll offsets rather than an element screenshot — an element screenshot of the (very tall, since it's `display:block` full-width on mobile) `.site-footer` node produced a stitching artifact where the page's `position: sticky` header appeared duplicated mid-image, a known Playwright behavior when it composites a multi-viewport-tall element capture with a sticky/fixed ancestor still in the DOM; this is a screenshot-tooling artifact, not a site bug, and was avoided by scrolling to a computed offset and capturing the visible viewport instead. Both the Visa Services and Attestation column icons were confirmed correctly rendered and aligned this way. No functional code was touched.

## Footer brand intro copy update

Client-supplied replacement copy for the footer brand column's descriptive line: `includes/footer.php`'s `.site-footer__muted` paragraph changed from "Technology-enabled visa consultancy and document attestation assistance for travellers, students, professionals, and businesses." to "Visagiri drives innovation with smart, future-ready digital solutions that empower growth and transformation." — a client-provided line, not authored or invented here. The trailing "A Unit of Tripgation Pvt Ltd." legal attribution (client-confirmed elsewhere in this document) was kept rather than dropped, since only the descriptive sentence was asked to change. The `.site-footer__tagline` line ("Seamless Visas. Limitless Journeys.") was left as-is — the request was for the intro paragraph, not the tagline. Verified rendered correctly via `curl` and a Playwright desktop screenshot; no layout shift, no functional code touched.

## Delivery package: cPanel zip + fresh admin/HRMS credentials

Client request: package the site for delivery, with login credentials for the admin panel and HRMS. Handled in three parts.

**Credentials regenerated, not reused.** The one existing `admin_users` row (`admin`, super-admin role — full access to CRM/Forex/Admin/HRMS) had its password hash replaced with a freshly generated one; the old plaintext was never recorded anywhere retrievable in this project (per its own seed-file comment, it was only ever delivered out-of-band, consistent with "a password should never live in version control"), so this delivery generates a new one rather than guessing or exposing a stale hash. A second, new `admin_users` row (`hr.admin`, `hr-admin` role) was added in `database/seed-hrms.sql` — the HRMS module previously had no seeded account of its own; any HRMS access so far went through the same super-admin login as everything else. This gives HR staff a login scoped to `hrms.*` permissions only, separate from the CRM/Forex/Admin super-admin account, matching the RBAC roles this project already defined but never issued a credential for. Both password hashes are bcrypt (cost 12, matching the existing pattern); both plaintext passwords are delivered directly to the client outside this file and outside version control, same discipline as the original admin account — **the placeholder email on the new `hr.admin` account (`hr@visagiri.com`) is not confirmed as a real mailbox and should be updated to the client's actual HR contact address after first login**, same as the password.

**Packaging script fixed to stop shipping real secrets.** `bin/package-cpanel.sh`'s `rsync` reads the live working tree, which — unlike `git`, which already gitignores them — has no awareness of which files hold real secrets. It was silently including the sandbox's actual `config/database.php` (a throwaway local dev password, low risk) and, more seriously, the actual `config/encryption.php` — the real AES-256-GCM key already in use to encrypt passport/PAN numbers in this sandbox's test data. Shipping a live encryption key inside a zip that travels over chat/email is exactly the exposure the project's own gitignore discipline was built to prevent for these two files; the fact that packaging bypassed it was a real gap. Fixed: both files are now excluded from the rsync and replaced with their `.example.php` templates (`CHANGE-ME` placeholders) inside the package, matching what git already ships. `includes/encryption.php` already throws a clear `RuntimeException` rather than silently encrypting with a placeholder key if `config/encryption.php` is left unedited, so this fails loudly instead of quietly shipping bad data.

**Script's printed setup steps corrected for the current schema.** The script's "next steps" output still referenced a single `database/schema.sql` / `database/seed.sql` pair from before the CRM/HRMS/Forex phases split the schema across four files each; it now lists the correct import order (`schema.sql`→`seed.sql`→`schema-crm.sql`→`seed-crm.sql`→`schema-hrms.sql`→`seed-hrms.sql`→`schema-forex.sql`→`seed-forex.sql`, later files depending on tables/rows the earlier ones create) and points to generating a fresh encryption key on the server rather than the removed docs/google-sheets-setup.md reference (docs/ is deliberately excluded from the package to keep it lean).

**Verified end-to-end against the real package, not just the source tree**: built the zip with the fixed script, extracted it standalone, confirmed `config/database.php`/`config/encryption.php` in the extracted package are the `CHANGE-ME` templates (not the sandbox's real values) while all 8 schema/seed files are present; pointed a fresh PHP dev server at the extracted package (with the sandbox's real local config copied back in just for this test, exactly as a real deploy would supply its own) and confirmed `/`, `/countries/`, `/attestation/`, `/visa/usa/`, `/leadership/`, and a static asset all return 200, and that both the reset `admin` password and the new `hr.admin` password log in successfully against `/admin/login/` and `/hrms/login/` respectively in this exact packaged copy. One dead end during this verification is worth recording: testing the flattened package with `php -S host:port -t .` (no explicit router script) produced false `500`s on every folder-named route (`/countries/`, `/admin/`, `/hrms/`, etc.) — `Call to undefined function countries_all()` — because PHP's built-in dev server, with no router script, falls back to serving a matching real *directory*'s own `index.php` directly (bypassing the front controller and all its `require_once`s) whenever the request path happens to match an actual folder on disk, which the flattened layout has plenty of (`countries/`, `admin/`, `hrms/`, ...). This is purely a `php -S`-without-a-router quirk, confirmed not a real bug by re-running with `php -S host:port -t . index.php` (all routes 200) and by re-reading `public/.htaccess`, which already explicitly does not bypass on `-d` (a comment there already flags this exact reasoning) — so real Apache/cPanel hosting is unaffected either way.

**Delivered**: the packaged zip, and both sets of credentials (username + password for `/admin/` and `/hrms/`) sent directly in chat, never written to this file or any tracked file — the client should change both passwords immediately after first login and set a real HR contact email on the `hr.admin` account.

## Live deployment support: import truncation on the client's host + a real ambiguous-column bug found

After delivery, the client's cPanel host repeatedly cut off large multi-statement SQL imports partway through phpMyAdmin — `schema-crm.sql` stopped right after `role_permissions` (before `user_sessions`/`login_attempts`/`customers`/etc.), `schema-hrms.sql` stopped right after `hr_departments`, and separately each of `seed-crm.sql`/`seed-hrms.sql` got through every `INSERT` but not the final `UPDATE admin_users SET role_id = ...` line at the very end of the file — a strikingly consistent "stops right near the end" pattern suggesting a host-side execution-time or request-size limit, not a SQL error. Diagnosed live via `SHOW TABLES` and targeted `SELECT`/`COUNT` checks against the client's actual database (never guessed), then resolved with hand-built "continuation" scripts — containing only the statements that hadn't run yet — verified against a locally-recreated copy of the client's exact partial state before being sent, so nothing risked a "table/column already exists" error on their live site. `schema-forex.sql` had not run at all and was verified and sent in full as-is.

**A real bug, not a deployment issue, surfaced once Forex tables existed**: `admin/pages/forex-dashboard.php`'s currency-wise delivered-volume query joins `forex_deliveries d` and `forex_requests fr` and referenced a bare `currency_code` in both the `SELECT` and `GROUP BY` — both tables have their own `currency_code` column, so MySQL correctly rejected it as ambiguous (`SQLSTATE[23000] 1052`) the first time the page was ever loaded against real data-bearing tables (this couldn't have surfaced during this project's own sandbox verification, since Forex Phase A's own test pass exercised the delivery flow but this dashboard aggregate query specifically was never hit with the tables in a state where it would run — worth noting as a real gap in that earlier verification). Fixed by qualifying both references as `d.currency_code`, matching the delivery's own currency rather than the request's (they're expected to match, but the delivery row is the authoritative record of what was actually handed over). A project-wide grep for every other `currency_code` reference confirmed all of them already qualify with a table alias — this was an isolated instance, not a repeat of the earlier `LIKE :search` repeated-placeholder pattern. Verified by running the corrected query directly against a schema-accurate local database.

## Admin sidebar: fixed unstyled group labels + collapsible sections, premium polish pass

Client screenshot showed the "Forex" section label rendering as bare unstyled text flush against the browser edge, breaking out of the sidebar's padding entirely — traced to `.admin-sidebar__group` (the CSS class used for the Forex/Content/System section dividers in `includes/admin-layout.php`) never having had a CSS rule defined anywhere in the project. It happened to go unnoticed until now because the CRM/HRMS/Forex sidebar sections were built across different phases of this project and none of those verification passes happened to screenshot a page with `content.manage`/`forex.requests.view` permissions active alongside a full sidebar render.

Client follow-up: convert those three sections into click-to-expand/collapse groups (previously always fully expanded, showing every sub-link at once) and give the dashboard a more premium visual treatment. `includes/admin-layout.php`: each `.admin-sidebar__group` divider became a `<button>` (accessible — `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, keyboard-focusable, semantically a real button rather than a clickable `<div>`) wrapping its section's links in a `.admin-sidebar__subgroup` container; reuses the existing `nav_chevron_icon()` helper already used by the public header's mega-menus, so the same chevron visual language appears in both places rather than a second icon being invented. A new `$navGroups` map in `admin_header_start()` determines server-side which group (if any) the current page's `$activeNav` belongs to, so that group renders already-expanded on first paint — no JS-dependent flash of a collapsed-then-expanding sidebar, and it still works correctly with JavaScript disabled (everything just stays visible, matching the old always-expanded behavior, rather than becoming inaccessible). `public/assets/js/admin.js` (new file, this project's first admin-specific script) adds the actual click-to-toggle on top of that. `public/assets/css/admin.css` also picked up: an accent-bordered, hover-lifting stat card style; a subtle shadow on the topbar instead of a flat border line; and a hover highlight on table rows.

**Verified visually** with Playwright, logged in as a real super-admin session: on `/admin/dashboard/` (no group active) Forex/Content/System all render collapsed with a down-pointing chevron; on `/admin/countries/` the Content group renders already-expanded server-side with its chevron flipped and "Countries" highlighted as the active link, while Forex/System stay collapsed; clicking the Forex header from the dashboard expands it (chevron flips, all Forex sub-links appear) without disturbing Content/System, and clicking it again collapses it back. `php -l` clean on the modified files.

## Global Visa Documentation Portal — Phase A (architecture only; per-country content stays client-supplied)

Client request: a 200+-country visa documentation portal with continent hubs, visa-status directories, printable checklists, downloadable document templates, an AI chatbot, a world-map search widget, and thousands of words of unique per-country/per-visa-type SEO content — explicitly benchmarked against VFS Global/Atlys/BlinkVisa/VisaHQ. This was **not built as specified**: almost every substantive piece of content it asked for (visa fees, processing times, document checklists, embassy contact details, validity periods, "top 25 rejection reasons," 2500–4500 words per page × up to 19 categories × 200+ countries) is real, legally consequential, frequently-changing government/consular data that has no source in this project — inventing it would mean the site confidently telling real travellers wrong visa requirements. This is exactly the risk `visa_requirements`/`embassies`/`consulates` have shipped empty to avoid since Phase 6/8, at a much larger scale. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code: the client will supply real per-country data over time through the admin CMS, and this pass builds architecture only, verified against the current real 208-country/9-visa-type catalog rather than fabricated content. The AI chatbot and world-map/voice-search widget were explicitly scoped out of this pass as separate, large features needing their own scoping.

**What was built, all reusing the existing 208-country/`visa_requirements`/`visa_types` catalog rather than inventing a parallel one:**

- **`database/schema-visa-portal.sql` + `seed-visa-portal.sql`**: a 10th visa type, Dependent Visa (the client's spec listed 10 core categories; only 9 existed), inserted with `country_visa_types` rows generated for all 208 existing countries via `INSERT ... SELECT` rather than 208 hand-written rows; five new boolean columns on `countries` (`is_gcc`, `is_asean`, `is_saarc`, `is_middle_east`, `is_caribbean`) populated directly in the seed file, since GCC/ASEAN/SAARC membership and the commonly-used "Middle East"/"Caribbean" travel groupings are stable, publicly verifiable political/geographic facts — not visa policy, so safe to hardcode (verified against real country lists, not guessed: 6/10/7/14/13 countries respectively, cross-checked against the actual 208-row seed's exact slugs before writing the migration). A sixth new column, `visa_policy_for_indians` (nullable enum: visa_required/visa_free/visa_on_arrival/evisa), is the one genuine visa-policy fact in this batch and ships `NULL` for every country — same discipline as `visa_requirements`, only ever set by a staff member through `/admin/countries/`, never assumed. India itself is deliberately left out of `is_saarc` despite being a real member, since this is an outbound consultancy for Indian travellers and a "visa to India" listing would serve no purpose here.
- **Continent hub pages** (`/visa/asia/`, `/visa/europe/`, `/visa/africa/`, `/visa/north-america/`, `/visa/south-america/`, `/visa/oceania/`, `/visa/middle-east/`, `/visa/caribbean/`) — `visa/continent.php` (new), dispatched from inside `visa/index.php` before the country-slug lookup runs (`CONTINENT_HUBS` in `includes/data.php` checked first — none of the 8 slugs collide with any real country or the existing 6-region taxonomy, so this couldn't shadow an existing page). Middle East and Caribbean are separate flag-based groupings layered on top of the existing 6-region `regions` table rather than new region rows, specifically to avoid touching `/countries/`'s already-shipped region-anchor breakdown (a Middle East country like UAE stays classified as "Asia" for region purposes, flagged `is_middle_east` for this additional grouping).
- **Visa Status Directory** (`/visa-status/` hub + `/visa-status/{visa-free,visa-on-arrival,evisa,schengen,gcc,asean,saarc}/`) — `pages/visa-status.php` (new). The three `visa_policy_for_indians`-backed directories render a real, honest empty state ("Not yet published... we haven't verified and listed any destinations... yet") rather than ever inventing a country's status; the four flag-backed ones (Schengen/GCC/ASEAN/SAARC) show real results immediately since those groupings are already populated.
- **Public visa application tracking** (`/track-visa/`) — `pages/track-visa.php` (new), restoring a feature this project's own task history shows was built once (Phase 11) and then lost during the earlier no-database rebuild, never rebuilt afterward (confirmed via code inspection: no trace existed anywhere before this pass). Copies the exact non-enumerating pattern already proven for `/forex/track/` — reference number + registered mobile must both match, any mismatch shows an identical generic message, and only a customer-safe status/timeline is shown (never internal notes, assigned consultant, or amounts).
- **Real document checklist UI** on the visa detail page (`visa/index.php`) — the "Required Documents" card now splits the existing `documents_required` free-text field into individual checkbox line items plus a Print Checklist button, instead of one paragraph of run-on text. Still entirely driven by admin-entered data — an empty field still falls through to "Not specified," no invented checklist appears for an unverified country.
- **Generic downloadable document templates** (`/document-templates/` hub + 6 formats: Cover Letter, NOC, Consent Letter for Minor, Self-Declaration, Sponsor Letter, Travel Itinerary) — `pages/document-templates.php` (new), real hand-written fill-in-the-blank formats. Unlike `visa_requirements`, this is safe to ship as real content immediately: these are standard boilerplate documents any visa consultancy provides, not a claim about any specific country's requirements — each page says plainly it's a general format, not legal advice, and to confirm exact wording with the specific embassy/consulate.
- **Admin CMS**: `/admin/countries/` gained checkboxes for the five new grouping flags and a "Visa policy for Indian passport holders" dropdown (defaulting to "— Not yet verified —"), plus a Visa Policy column in the list view so staff can see verification status at a glance without opening each country.

**A real, previously undetected bug found while testing the new admin form, affecting the whole CMS — not something this pass introduced**: `/admin/countries/`'s edit form posts to a bare `action="/admin/countries/"` with no query string and no hidden `id` field, while the save handler derives `$id` only from `$_GET['id']`. On every edit submission `$id` was silently `null`, so "editing" a country actually always executed the `INSERT` branch — either a fatal duplicate-key `PDOException` (slug is `UNIQUE`) or, worse, a silent duplicate row. A project-wide check (background agent, read-only) found the identical bug shape in four more files: `admin/pages/visa-types.php`, `admin/pages/embassies.php`, `admin/pages/faqs.php`, and — most seriously — **`admin/pages/customers.php`**, where `customer_reference_no` is freshly generated on every insert, meaning editing an existing customer's details would have silently created a brand-new duplicate customer record rather than updating the original, with no error at all to alert staff. Every other `id`-based admin/HRMS save form was individually checked and confirmed already safe (id embedded directly in the form's `action` URL, or already using the correct hidden-field pattern, or a composite-key/upsert shape not susceptible to this failure mode at all) — the blast radius is exactly these five files. Fixed identically in all five: `$id` now falls back to `$_POST['id']` when no GET query string is present, and each edit form gained `<input type="hidden" name="id" value="...">`, matching the one file (`admin/pages/users.php`) that already had this right. **Verified end-to-end**: submitted the real rendered edit form (not a manually-corrected URL) for Singapore with a real browser-equivalent POST to the bare `/admin/countries/` action — confirmed exactly one Singapore row exists before and after, and the field change actually persisted — proving the fix works via the real form, not just in theory. Given this bug predates this session's changes, **the client's own live database should be checked for duplicate customer/country/visa-type/FAQ/embassy rows created by any past edit attempts** — this pass could only fix the code going forward, not retroactively detect duplicates already created on production before the fix was deployed.

**Verified end-to-end against the real local database**: every new route (`/visa/asia/`, `/visa/middle-east/`, `/visa/caribbean/`, `/visa-status/`, all 7 status directories, `/track-visa/`, `/document-templates/` and 6 detail pages, plus existing `/visa/singapore/`, `/visa/singapore/dependent/`, `/visa/uae/`) returns `200` with a clean server log (no PHP warnings/notices); Middle East hub correctly lists all 14 real countries with real flags, GCC directory correctly lists exactly the 6 real member states; the visa-free directory correctly shows the honest empty state; the document checklist UI was verified with a temporary real `visa_requirements` row (inserted and removed after the screenshot, not left in the database) rendering as real checkboxes with a working Print button; the footer's Visa Services column automatically picked up "Dependent Visa" with no template change needed, confirming it's driven by the live `visa_types` table rather than a hardcoded list. `php -l` clean across every new/modified file.

**Not yet done, by design, per the agreed sequencing**: any actual per-country visa requirement, fee, processing-time, or embassy-address content (all still ship as "pending verification" until a staff member enters real, sourced data); the AI chatbot; the world-map visual search widget with voice input; bulk 200-country content generation of any kind. The reference-number format change the client's original spec requested (`VG-SGP-TV-20260826-000123`) was flagged as a breaking change to an already-live, shared numbering scheme and intentionally not touched pending explicit confirmation.

## Global Visa Documentation Portal — Phase B (site-wide search widget + rule-based Visa Assistant)

Client selected this as the next phase (over HRMS/Forex Phase B) via an explicit prompt: build the search-everywhere and chatbot pieces deferred from Phase A.

**New `/visa/` Main Hub**: `visa/index.php` now renders `visa/hub.php` when no country slug is present (previously redirected to `/countries/`; confirmed via grep that nothing internally linked to bare `/visa/`, so the behavior change was safe). The hub shows Browse by Continent (8 real tiles with live `COUNT()`s via the existing `countries_by_continent_slug()`), Browse by Visa Status (7 tiles via `countries_by_visa_status_slug()`), Popular Destinations (existing `is_popular_destination` flag), Recently Updated (real `visa_requirements` rows ordered by `updated_at DESC`, only rendered when non-empty), and a More Tools row to Document Templates / Track Your Application / Embassy Directory.

**Site-wide instant search widget**: `includes/functions.php` gained `visa_search_widget_items()` (builds one searchable catalog from real data — every country, every visa type, all 8 continent hubs, all 7 status directories, each with its real URL) and `render_visa_search_widget()`, which prints the widget markup plus the catalog as a `<script type="application/json">` tag (`JSON_HEX_TAG | JSON_HEX_AMP` to keep it script-injection-safe). `public/assets/js/visa-search-widget.js` is a vanilla-JS autocomplete: substring match on label, up to 8 grouped results, full keyboard nav, and a `localStorage`-backed "recent searches" list (max 5) shown on focus. Placed on the homepage above the existing detailed search form (left untouched) and on the new `/visa/` hub.

**Voice search**: an optional microphone button using the standard `SpeechRecognition`/`webkitSpeechRecognition` API, hidden via HTML `hidden` attribute and only revealed by JS when the browser actually supports it. Required loosening `includes/security.php`'s `Permissions-Policy` header from `microphone=()` to `microphone=(self)` — first-party use only, no third-party embed can invoke it.

**Visa Assistant — deliberately rule-based, not generative**: `includes/visa-assistant-widget.php` + `public/assets/js/visa-assistant.js` add a floating chat panel (bottom-left, so it never overlaps the existing bottom-right enquiry widget) that does keyword scoring against the real `visa_faqs` table (via a new `all_active_faqs()` helper) and echoes back a matched FAQ's real answer verbatim, or an honest "I don't have a published answer for that yet" plus quick-links to Document Checklists / Track My Application / Embassy Directory / Browse by Continent / Talk to a Human. This was a deliberate scope-down from the client's original "AI chatbot" ask: a true generative assistant could paraphrase or guess at visa fees, documents, or processing times it doesn't actually know — exactly the fabrication risk this entire project has avoided elsewhere by shipping `visa_requirements`/`embassies` empty rather than guessed. A real generative integration is a separate decision requiring the client to pick an LLM provider and supply an API key; not built here.

**New `/embassy-directory/`**: `pages/embassy-directory.php` lists only countries with at least one real row in `embassies`/`consulates`/`visa_application_centres` (via `EXISTS` subqueries); ships the same honest "Not yet published" empty state pattern as everywhere else in the portal, since none of those tables have real data yet.

**A real bug found and fixed during this pass's own verification, not present in any previously-shipped code**: the assistant panel's CSS (`.visa-assistant__panel`) set `display: flex` unconditionally, with no rule for the `[hidden]` state. The HTML `hidden` attribute only works via the browser's user-agent stylesheet (`[hidden] { display: none }`), and *any* author-stylesheet rule that sets `display` on that element overrides the UA stylesheet regardless of selector specificity — so the panel rendered open on every single page load, not just when toggled, visibly covering the search widget and the continent/status tiles beneath it (caught via Playwright screenshot of a fresh `/visa/` load, where the assistant was never programmatically opened). Fixed with one added rule, `.visa-assistant__panel[hidden] { display: none; }`, immediately after the base rule. Re-verified via fresh screenshots: the panel now stays closed until `#visa-assistant-toggle` is clicked, and closes again on the × button.

**Verified end-to-end against the real local database** (Playwright, 1440×1200): `/visa/` hub renders all 8 continent tiles with correct live counts (Asia 51, Europe 48, Africa 54, North America 27, South America 12, Oceania 16, Middle East 14, Caribbean 13) and all 7 status tiles; homepage search widget correctly autocompletes "sing" → Singapore with a Country badge; `/embassy-directory/` correctly shows its honest empty state with working links; the assistant was opened via its real toggle button, asked "processing time", and returned a real, previously-seeded FAQ answer verbatim (not fabricated by this pass) — confirming the keyword-match pipeline works against genuine data, not a hardcoded demo string. `php -l` clean across every new/modified file.

**Not yet done, by design**: a true generative AI chatbot (needs client's LLM provider + API key decision); the world-map visual search widget from the original spec (the list-based autocomplete covers the same "search everywhere instantly" goal without needing map-tile assets or geo-boundary data); any bulk per-country content.

## Full-site audit + /documentation/ — replacing the dead legacy documentation.html

Client asked for a full technical/content/SEO audit of the live site. A background crawl agent hit a hard block: `visagiri.com` is denied by this sandbox's network egress policy (confirmed via the proxy's own status endpoint and a direct `curl` CONNECT test — a 403 on the tunnel, not a site or tool problem). No live page could be fetched. The audit was rebuilt instead from direct code + database inspection (the same codebase and DB this project has built and verified throughout) and delivered as a report artifact, explicitly labeled as not-a-live-crawl. Headline findings: (1) the country/visa-type detail pipeline is fully built and wired but genuinely 0% content-populated — 208 countries × 10 types, 0 with a published requirement, 0 with embassy data, 0 with a visa-policy flag set, all by the same deliberate "ship empty, never fabricate" design as Visa Portal Phase A; (2) `legacy-site/documentation.html` — the exact page the client flagged — is confirmed to be a real leftover file from the pre-rebuild Bootstrap template (Tawk.to widget, dead links to `business.html`/`crew.html`/`country.html` and 32 more siblings), physically excluded from every packaging script (`bin/package-cpanel.sh`) and therefore never touched by this project, meaning it's very likely still reachable on the live server since nothing ever deleted the original.

Client selected the documentation page as the first fix. Since there was no `/documentation/` route in the app at all (not a redesign of an existing PHP page — a genuine gap), built one from scratch: `pages/documentation.php`, routed at `case 'documentation'` in `public/index.php`. Content follows the client's requested section list (why documentation matters, passport/photograph/financial/employment/invitation/cover-letter/itinerary/hotel/flight/insurance/sponsorship/minor/business/student/work-visa documents, plus document authentication) as real, general, reusable document-preparation guidance — the same "true but not destination-specific" standard already established by `document-templates.php`, not a claim about any one country's requirements. Document Translation and Notarization are described only as concepts an applicant may encounter, explicitly not as Visagiri-offered services, since neither is a confirmed active offering (unlike Apostille/Attestation/Legalization, which are real and linked to `/attestation/` rather than duplicated). The client's "modern and technology driven" framing is delivered as a 4-item feature grid describing only real, already-shipped platform mechanics — reference-number tracking (`/track-visa/`), access-controlled off-web-root document storage with staff-only upload (`includes/documents.php`), encrypted PII fields, and audit-logged handling (`includes/audit.php`) — deliberately not claiming a public self-upload portal or AI verification, neither of which exist in this project. CTA ("Check My Document Requirements") links to the real `/enquire/` CRM form; a WhatsApp CTA uses the existing `whatsapp_enquiry_href()` helper. FAQ section reuses real general FAQs (`faqs_general()`), not invented ones.

Linked from three places, all real existing pages: the footer's Resources column, a new fourth card on `/visa/`'s "More Tools" row, and a contextual line added to `/document-templates/`'s intro pointing back the other way.

**Verified end-to-end against the real local database**: `/documentation/` returns 200 with a clean server log; 16 document-category cards and 4 platform-feature cards render (confirmed by counting the `service-card`/`feature-card` class occurrences in the actual response body, not assumed); the FAQ accordion renders with real general FAQs; both CTAs point to real, working destinations; the new footer link, hub card, and document-templates cross-link all confirmed present in their respective pages' real rendered output. `php -l` clean across every new/modified file. Screenshot-verified full page render at 1440px.

**Not yet done**: confirming whether `legacy-site/documentation.html` and its 34 siblings are still live on production and removing them if so — this needs the client to check directly, since this sandbox cannot reach the live site (see above). The rest of the original 40-section brief (per-country content population, visual/photography redesign, CRM UTM wiring, accessibility/performance passes, the other 34 legacy-file cleanups) remains unscoped pending the client's review of the audit report.

## Premium header polish + redesigned admin login

Client asked for a more "modern premium" header and a redesigned login panel (admin — HRMS login left untouched, per client's own scope choice), plus reported the Countries mega-menu search and the homepage hero search widget "not working." Reproduced both reported bugs first, via real Playwright interaction (typed into each search box, checked actual result counts) rather than reading code and guessing: both work correctly against this codebase and local database — homepage widget returns Singapore for "sing", the mega-menu search filters correctly, `/countries/`'s own search filters correctly, zero console errors across all three. Since this sandbox still can't reach `visagiri.com` (same network egress block as the earlier audit), the live-site symptom is most likely a stale/partially-applied delivery zip or browser cache rather than a defect in this code — flagged back to the client with a request for a live screenshot + console error if it persists after this delivery.

**Header** (`public/assets/css/layout.css`): replaced the flat hairline border-bottom with a real shadow at rest and a frosted-glass state on scroll (`backdrop-filter: blur(14px) saturate(160%)` behind `@supports`, with a plain translucent fallback for browsers without it); added a gold underline that grows in from center on nav-link hover/active, replacing the old flat color-only swap; every mega-menu panel now carries a thin gold top rule and a slightly deeper, layered shadow; mega-menu item hover gained a gold left-accent bar instead of a flat background swap. No markup or JS changed — pure CSS, so zero behavioral risk to the mega-menu's existing hover/focus/Escape logic.

**Admin login** (`admin/pages/login.php` + `public/assets/css/admin.css`): replaced the plain centered card on a flat navy background with a two-panel layout — a navy-gradient brand panel (logo, tagline, an inline SVG passport/checkmark motif matching the existing "original illustration, not stock photography" convention used elsewhere on the site, and an honest note: "Internal staff access only. Every sign-in and change is logged" — true, since `log_action('login', ...)` already fires on every login and the wider CRM audit-logs every mutation) alongside a clean form panel with icon-adorned username/password fields and a lifted, shadowed submit button. Stacks to a single column under 860px. No change to the auth logic itself (rate limiting, timing-safe password check, CSRF) — only the markup/CSS around the existing form, same `action="/admin/login/"` and hidden CSRF field.

**Verified end-to-end**: real Playwright login using the actual admin credentials through the redesigned form — submits, passes CSRF + rate-limit + password checks unchanged, redirects to `/admin/dashboard/` exactly as before. Screenshot-verified header at rest, scrolled (frosted state), and with the Countries mega-menu open, plus admin login at 1440px and 390px. Zero console errors across every check. `php -l` clean.

**Not done**: HRMS login redesign (client scoped this pass to admin only); the two "not working" search reports remain open pending a live reproduction, since this sandbox has no path to the live site to confirm the actual cause.

## Customer accounts + B2B Partner portal restored; header rebuilt as a travel-tech utility bar

Three separate client requests landed close together and are covered in this entry: (1) "Customers Login Panel, Partners Login & Employee Login" in the header, (2) social media links moved to the footer, (3) a full header redesign spec (tagline, live currency rate, 24×7/WhatsApp, a Login dropdown replacing Enquire Now).

**Customer accounts, rebuilt onto the CRM, not a parallel identity system.** Earlier in this project's history a customer self-service login/dashboard existed, then was removed during the "single-folder no-database rebuild." Recovering it wholesale from git history wasn't right — that old code used a generic `users`+`roles` table this project no longer has. Instead: added auth columns directly to the live `customers` table (`schema-customer-partner-portal.sql`, fully additive), and register/login/logout/forgot-password/reset-password + a full dashboard (applications, documents with real customer self-upload — a genuinely new capability, since no public upload path existed before — two-way messages, appointments, payments, notifications, profile). Registering with an email that already has a CRM record (from a past enquiry) claims that record by setting its password rather than creating a duplicate, exactly the same discipline as the admin-CMS duplicate-record bug fixed earlier in this project. Remember-me uses a real persistent DB token, not just an extended session cookie — the latter silently stops working once PHP's session garbage collector reaps the underlying session file, well before the claimed 30 days.

**B2B Partner portal, built from zero — no partner-related data existed anywhere before this.** `/partner/` mirrors the customer auth pattern with its own session key and remember-me token so none of admin/customer/partner sessions can ever collide. Self-registration lands in `pending` status; a partner can log in and see their own pending state, but referral/commission data only unlocks once staff approve them (new `admin/pages/partners.php`). Each active partner gets a shareable referral link (`/register/?ref=<reference>`) that attributes a new customer signup via `customers.referred_by_partner_id` — only if that partner is currently active, so a pending or suspended partner's link can't backdate attribution once approved. Commissions (`partner_commissions`) ship `amount_due` as `NULL` until staff set a real figure on the application detail page — same "ships empty until real" discipline as `visa_requirements`/embassies elsewhere in this project, not a fabricated number. Building the commission UI also meant adding admin-side appointment scheduling (`customer_appointments`) and a "visible to customer" checkbox on application notes (turning the existing internal-notes table into a real two-way message thread) — both were already built on the customer-dashboard side from the first phase of this work but had no admin-side way to actually populate them until now.

**Header rebuilt around a travel-tech utility bar**, per a separate, detailed client spec. A new thin bar above the main nav (collapses away entirely on scroll, keeping the sticky state slim) holds a USD→INR "indicative rate" pill, 24×7 Support (`tel:`, using the existing `contact_phone_dial` setting), and WhatsApp (existing `whatsapp_enquiry_href()` helper, existing number — nothing new invented). The logo's subtitle became the client's tagline, "Technology Meets Travel & Visa." The old flat "Enquire Now" CTA is gone from the header entirely, replaced by a Login dropdown (Customer / Employee / B2B Partner), per the spec's own explicit structure — Employee maps to the existing `/hrms/` login, the closest real analog for that label.

**The live rate is real, not simulated** (`includes/currency-rate.php`): fetches USD→INR from Frankfurter (ECB reference rates, no API key — nothing to leak, so the "keep credentials server-side" requirement is satisfied by having no credential at all), cached to a local file with a 1-hour TTL so ordinary traffic never re-hits the upstream per request. A stale cached value is served (clearly labeled "Last known rate") if the upstream is unreachable; if there's never been a successful fetch, the widget honestly shows "Rate unavailable" rather than a fabricated number. A small client-side poll (every 5 minutes) refreshes the same number for a visitor whose tab stays open, without a full reload. **This sandbox's own network egress is blocked for arbitrary external domains** (the same restriction documented in the earlier site-audit entry, confirmed again here against Frankfurter directly) — meaning the "unavailable" fallback path is what's actually been exercised end-to-end from here, not the live-fetch success path. That's a limitation of this dev sandbox, not of the deployed app: the client's actual cPanel host is a separate, normal internet-connected server and should reach the API fine — worth the client confirming once deployed.

**Verified end-to-end**: full customer register → dashboard → all 7 sub-pages → logout → re-login, then re-tested against real seeded application/document/payment/appointment/message data (not just empty states) and cleaned up afterward. Full partner flow: register → pending screen → admin approve → referral link → a second customer registers through that link → `referred_by_partner_id` set correctly → admin schedules an appointment, sends a customer-visible note, sets a commission → partner dashboard shows the correct referral and commission figures. Header: Login dropdown opens/closes correctly via click, outside-click, and Escape (checked programmatically, not just visually); all three login routes return 200; `tel:`/WhatsApp hrefs verified against the real configured number; scrolled state correctly collapses the utility bar; mobile menu carries the same rate/support/WhatsApp/login content. One real bug caught and fixed during this pass's own verification: the dashboard/partner shells' `render_*_end()` functions were calling `includes/footer.php`, which reads variables set inside `includes/header.php`'s own function call — a separate function scope, so PHP's `require()` didn't inherit them, causing a fatal error on every dashboard and partner page. Fixed with a minimal closing shell instead of the full marketing footer, matching what the original pre-removal customer dashboard already did for the same reason.

**Not done**: the "Get Assistance" quick-enquiry dialogue and floating contact widget from an earlier, still-separate request — deliberately not touched in this pass since it's a distinct piece of work; the header CTA area it would have lived in is now the Login dropdown instead, so that widget (when built) becomes the primary quick-enquiry entry point.

## Floating widgets consolidated to one; live-site "not working" reports investigated

Client reported four issues from live-site screenshots after applying the header package: (1) the Login dropdown didn't open on click, (2) the homepage search widget didn't show suggestions, (3) a visible white gap under the footer before the floating buttons, (4) two separate floating chat buttons — asked to remove one and keep floating buttons for WhatsApp / Email / Enquire only.

**Investigated (1)-(3) against this exact codebase, not just re-read the code**: real Playwright run confirms the Login dropdown opens/closes correctly on click, the hero search widget returns a live "Singapore" suggestion for "sing", and a full-page screenshot shows the footer ending exactly at the document's actual bottom (`scrollHeight` and the footer's own bottom edge match to the pixel) — no structural gap exists in the markup or CSS. Since the delivered header markup in the client's screenshot is confirmed to be the *new* version (tagline/rate/Login all present in the right place) but the interactive behavior isn't, the most likely explanation is that `assets/js/site.js` and/or `assets/js/visa-search-widget.js` on the live server are still serving an older cached copy — Phase 17 added long cache lifetimes for static assets specifically for performance, which means a same-named JS file only updates for a visitor once the browser's cache actually expires or is bypassed. Flagged back to the client to verify: confirm both JS files' upload timestamp/size on the server match this delivery, and hard-refresh (or test in a private window) before concluding it's still broken — this is a deployment/caching check, not a code defect reproducible here.

**(4) implemented as asked.** Removed the separate rule-based "Visa Assistant" FAQ chatbot entirely — `includes/visa-assistant-widget.php` and `public/assets/js/visa-assistant.js` deleted, its `.visa-assistant*` CSS block removed from `components.css`, its `<script>` tag and `require` dropped from `includes/footer.php`, and the now-unused `all_active_faqs()` helper removed from `includes/data.php` (it had no other caller). The remaining floating widget (`includes/enquiry-widget.php`, bottom-right) now expands to exactly three actions — WhatsApp (unchanged, real number via `whatsapp_enquiry_href()`), Email (unchanged, `mailto:` to the real configured address), and a new Enquire action linking to the same real `/enquire/` CRM form as the header's Enquire Now button — replacing the old Call/`tel:` action, which is gone now that the header's own 24×7 Support is also WhatsApp-based.

**Verified**: Playwright confirms zero `.visa-assistant` elements remain, exactly one `.enquiry-widget` element exists, and its three expanded actions resolve to the correct WhatsApp/mailto/`/enquire/` targets; zero console errors. `php -l` clean on every changed file; no other file in the codebase still references the removed widget or helper (confirmed by a full-repo grep).

## Real fix for the Login dropdown: an overflow-clipping bug, not a caching issue

Client pushed back that the Login dropdown was still not opening after the previous delivery, with a screenshot also showing the Countries mega-menu's search filtering correctly for "Singapore" — which ruled out the "stale cached JS" theory from the previous entry: if the JS were genuinely stale/missing, the *other* JS-driven interaction (mega-menu search) wouldn't have worked either, but it clearly did in their own screenshot.

Re-tested the Login dropdown locally with a stricter check than before — not just whether the `is-open` class gets applied (it did, every time), but whether the menu is actually *visible in a rendered screenshot*. It wasn't: `.site-header__utility` (the bar the Login trigger now lives in, after moving it there per an earlier request) has `overflow: hidden`, added specifically so the utility bar's collapse-on-scroll animation clips its shrinking content cleanly. That same `overflow: hidden` was also clipping the Login dropdown's popup menu, which is a `position: absolute` element meant to extend below the utility bar's own 40px height — so the menu was toggling open correctly in the DOM (chevron flips, `aria-expanded` flips) while rendering completely invisible, clipped by its own ancestor. A real, reproducible layout bug, not a deployment/cache issue — my earlier caching explanation was wrong.

**Fix**: moved `overflow: hidden` off the bar's resting state and onto only its `.is-scrolled` (collapsed) state in `public/assets/css/layout.css`, so the bar still clips cleanly while animating shut on scroll, but no longer clips its own dropdown while sitting open at the top of the page.

**Also checked, not a bug**: the mega-menu panel's edges showing hero-section content behind it (visible in the client's screenshot) is the mega-menu's normal, working overlay behavior — it's a centered floating panel, not a full-screen modal, so page content is visible around it by design; reproduced the exact same look locally with the same "Singapore" filter working correctly. If the client would prefer a dimmed backdrop behind open mega-menus/dropdowns for a more "modal" feel, that's a small follow-up, not a bug fix — flagged here rather than built without being asked.

**Verified**: Playwright screenshot (not just DOM class checks) confirms all three Login links (Customer/Employee/B2B Partner) now render fully visible and clickable on open; outside-click still closes it; scroll-collapse animation still clips correctly when scrolled (utility bar's `max-height` reaches `0px` as before). Zero console errors.

## The real "Countries search not working" bug: click was navigating away, not opening the panel

Client reported the Countries search widget "not working." Reproduced the exact interaction a real desktop mouse user would make — clicking the "Countries" nav item rather than hovering over it first — and it navigated straight to `/countries/`, never opening the mega-menu at all. The dropdown's own search box was never reachable by click; only hovering (holding the mouse still over the link before clicking) opened it, because `site.js`'s click handler only intercepted navigation on touch devices (`matchMedia('(hover: none)')`), deliberately leaving mouse clicks to behave as a normal link on the theory that desktop users would hover first. In practice, most people click nav items rather than pausing to hover, so this sent them straight past the feature.

**Fix** (`public/assets/js/site.js`, `initMegaMenu()`): the click handler now always calls `preventDefault()` and toggles the panel open/closed, on every device, not just touch. This applies uniformly to all three mega-menus (Attestation, Countries, Company) since they share the same handler and had the same underlying issue. Users can still reach the plain destination pages via the real links inside each panel (e.g. "Explore All Countries", "View Complete Country Directory", the Attestation CTA) — the top-level word itself now behaves as a disclosure trigger rather than double-duty as a direct link, which is the standard pattern for this kind of mega-menu.

**Verified**: a plain `page.click()` (no hover) on each of the three mega-menu triggers now opens its panel and keeps the user on the same page; typing "singapore" into the Countries panel's own search box correctly filters to Singapore; clicking the trigger a second time closes it again; zero console errors. This was the actual cause behind the "search not working" report — not caching, not the earlier overflow-clipping bug (that was a separate, real issue with the Login dropdown specifically, already fixed).

## Admin credentials regenerated (client requested, plaintext not recoverable from the stored hash)

Client asked for the admin credentials. The only record of the previous plaintext password lived in chat at the time it was originally generated, per this project's own rule (`database/seed.sql`'s own comment: "a password should never live in version control") — bcrypt hashes are one-way, so there was no way to recover or "share" the old password from the hash stored in the repo. Generated two brand-new random 14-character passwords instead (one for `admin`, one for `hr.admin`), hashed them with PHP's `password_hash()` (same function the app's own registration/reset code already uses), and updated `database/seed.sql` / `database/seed-hrms.sql` with the new hashes — the plaintext itself is not committed anywhere, matching the existing convention.

**Verified end-to-end, not just an offline `password_verify()` check**: logged in through the real `/admin/login/` form with each new username/password pair against the local dev database, through the actual rate-limiting/CSRF/session code path, landing on `/admin/dashboard/` for both. Client was given the new plaintext credentials directly in chat, plus a `UPDATE admin_users SET password_hash = ...` SQL statement to run against their live database via phpMyAdmin, since editing this repo's seed file has no effect on an already-deployed database — only a fresh import or a manual UPDATE does.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 1: RBAC foundation + real admin Partner CRM

Client sent a 50-section spec asking to expand the existing minimal B2B partner referral system into a full Partner Enrollment + CRM platform (OTP registration, document management, quotations, invoicing, wallet/credit, team sub-users, tiered pricing, 7-role RBAC, communication centre, reporting, SEO landing page). The spec's own §49 asked for an audit + phased proposal before writing code — this session did exactly that: two research passes read the real codebase (not assumptions) to map what already existed, confirmed several pieces the spec assumes exist do not (no OTP/SMS provider, no WhatsApp Business API beyond a `wa.me` deep link, no payment gateway, no charting library, no PDF/Excel export, no outbound email at all — not even for password reset, which is dev-mode-only today — and this sandbox has no network access to test any external API regardless), then produced a 10-phase roadmap. Given the size, the client chose to build **Phase 1 only** in this pass: RBAC foundation + a real, paginated admin Partner CRM, replacing the old bare-bones one. Phases 2–10 (enrollment wizard, approvals, quotations, wallet, team management, reporting, SEO page, deferred third-party integrations) are documented in the plan but not started.

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b.sql`, additive): `partners.assigned_admin_id` (nullable FK → `admin_users`, the Relationship Manager assignment — ships NULL until staff assign one, same "ships empty until real" convention as `partner_commissions.amount_due`); `audit_logs.partner_id` (nullable FK → `partners` — `admin_user_id` on that table was already nullable, confirmed by reading the live schema before adding this, so it's a small, consistent extension of an existing shape, not a new pattern); new `partner_status_history` table (partner_id, from_status, to_status, changed_by, remarks, created_at), modeled directly on `forex_status_history`. `partners.status` deliberately stays the current 3-value ENUM (`pending`/`active`/`suspended`) — the spec's 9-state workflow is driven by a multi-step registration wizard and approval flow that don't exist yet; widening the ENUM now would add states nothing could reach.

**RBAC** (`database/seed-b2b.sql`): a dedicated `partners.*` permission namespace (`partners.view`, `partners.view_all`, `partners.manage`) replacing the old piggyback on `customers.view`/`customers.manage`. Six new roles, matching the spec's staff structure: `b2b-admin` (full visibility + manage), `b2b-relationship-manager` (scoped to only their assigned partners — no `partners.view_all`), and `b2b-visa-manager`/`b2b-accounts-manager`/`b2b-sales-manager`/`b2b-support-executive` (all full-visibility read-only, since none of them are the "assigned to me only" role — that's specifically the Relationship Manager). `includes/rbac.php` gained `current_admin_b2b_scoped_to_assigned()`, mirroring the existing `current_admin_forex_scoped_to_assigned()` pattern.

**Admin UI**: `admin/pages/partners.php` fully rebuilt on `admin/pages/customers.php`'s pattern (the old version had no pagination at all). Real search (company/contact/email/reference/mobile) + status filter + two-query pagination; profile page follows this codebase's established "one long page, stacked `<h2>` sections" convention rather than introducing tabs — there is zero tab-UI precedent anywhere in this app (confirmed by direct grep before deciding), so tabs weren't added just for this one page despite the spec's wording. Sections: Overview (with Relationship Manager assignment), Referred Customers & Applications, Commissions (both existing, unchanged logic), and the new Status History. No Documents/Quotations/Invoices/Wallet/Team sections — those tables don't exist until later phases, and this project's own convention is to never show empty/fabricated feature sections. Approve/Suspend/Reactivate now write to `partner_status_history` in addition to the existing audit log, and a new Assign/Reassign Relationship Manager action was added.

**Shared helpers introduced**: `status_badge(string $status, array $map): string` in `includes/functions.php`, replacing the inline-ternary badge pattern repeated at nearly every admin list/detail page (confirmed no shared helper existed despite dozens of call sites) — used on the new partners page, not retrofitted everywhere else in this pass. `log_action()` in `includes/audit.php` gained an optional `?int $partnerActorId` parameter (mirrors the existing `$actorId`) — unused by any action in this phase (everything here is admin-driven), but exists now so a later phase where a partner acts on their own account doesn't need to touch this function's signature again.

**A real bug caught during verification, not present in the final code**: an early draft of the approve/suspend/reactivate status-transition helper passed a fixed `['admin' => ..., 'id' => ...]` params array to every UPDATE statement regardless of whether that specific query actually used a `:admin` placeholder. This project's PDO connection uses non-emulated prepares, which reject `execute()` calls containing a named key with no matching placeholder in the query (`SQLSTATE[HY093]: Invalid parameter number`) — confirmed by a quick standalone reproduction against the real connection before it ever shipped. Fixed by having each caller supply exactly the params its own UPDATE SQL needs.

**Verified end-to-end** (Playwright, real accounts seeded via the actual `password_hash()`/role-assignment path, not fixtures): a `b2b-admin` test account sees all seeded test partners, search and status-filter both correctly narrow the list, approving a pending partner writes a real `partner_status_history` row (`pending → active`) and audit log entry, and assigning a Relationship Manager persists and displays correctly. A `b2b-relationship-manager` test account sees only partners assigned to it (confirmed both via the list and via a direct-URL access attempt on an unassigned partner, which correctly returns "not found"), and sees zero Approve/Suspend/Set-Manager buttons anywhere (no `partners.manage`). A third test account holding only the old `visa-consultant` role (which still has `customers.view`) now gets a real `403` on `/admin/partners/` and no longer sees the Partners link in the sidebar at all — confirming the old `customers.*` piggyback is genuinely gone, not just hidden. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. All test accounts and partner rows removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done in this pass** (see the 10-phase roadmap for the agreed order): multi-step enrollment wizard + email verification + document upload, admin approval workflow beyond the existing 3 actions, partner-submitted visa applications, quotations/tiered pricing, wallet/invoicing, team sub-users, communication centre + real notification bell, CSV export + document-expiry alerts, public SEO landing page, and everything blocked on client-supplied third-party credentials (real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, virus scanning, transactional email/SMTP — the last of which several later phases will need resolved early, since almost nothing in this spec works end-to-end without it).

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 2: multi-step enrollment wizard + real email sending

Second of the 10-phase B2B roadmap (see the Phase 1 entry above). Client confirmed they'd supply real SMTP credentials, so this phase builds the full 5-step enrollment wizard end-to-end — including real transactional email — rather than leaving email as an open question.

**Email — a real hand-rolled SMTP client, not a stub.** This codebase has zero external dependencies (no Composer, no vendor/ directory anywhere), so `includes/mail.php` speaks just enough SMTP by hand (EHLO, STARTTLS, AUTH LOGIN, MAIL FROM/RCPT TO/DATA, RFC 5321 dot-stuffing, RFC 2047 header encoding for non-ASCII subjects) to work against a standard cPanel mailbox or any relay (SendGrid/SES/Postmark all speak the same protocol) — matching the same zero-dependency discipline as `includes/encryption.php`. Supports an unauthenticated local relay too (empty username/password), the common case for cPanel's own local mail server. Configuration lives in `config/smtp.php` (gitignored, template at `config/smtp.example.php`), same pattern as `config/database.php`/`config/encryption.php`. `send_mail()` never throws — no config, a connection failure, or a rejected recipient all just return `false`, and every caller already degrades to the existing dev-mode "show the link on screen" behavior, so nothing regresses before the client's credentials are in place.

**Verified against a real SMTP conversation, not just read for correctness**: built a throwaway fake SMTP server (`stream_socket_server`, logs every line) and ran `smtp_send()` against it for five real scenarios — full AUTH LOGIN happy path, unauthenticated relay, a rejected recipient, a failed auth, and no host configured — confirming the exact byte-for-byte command sequence (including that a body line starting with "." gets correctly doubled per RFC 5321) and the correct true/false return in each case. This sandbox still can't reach any real external SMTP server (confirmed earlier in this project's history and true again here), so the live-send-to-a-real-inbox path remains untested from here — that's on the client to confirm once real credentials are in `config/smtp.php`.
