The existing customer and partner "forgot password" flows were also wired onto this same helper while it was being built, since they already had the exact matching dev-mode fallback to preserve.

**The wizard** (`partner/pages/register.php` rewritten as step 1, five new pages, one new helper file `includes/partner-enrollment.php`): Business Info → Contact & Login → Business Profile → Documents → Terms, matching the client's spec order. Key design decisions, each made deliberately rather than following the spec literally:
- **Account creation happens at step 2** (contact + password), not step 5. Steps 1's business-info fields are held in `$_SESSION` only until then — nothing is written to the database for a visitor who abandons before finishing step 2. From step 2 onward the partner is a real, logged-in (if incomplete) account, so document uploads and later steps operate on real rows instead of trying to hold files in session state indefinitely.
- **`partners.status` was deliberately NOT widened** to the spec's 9-state workflow this phase — the wizard produces exactly the same `'pending'` status Phase 1's approve/suspend/reactivate actions already handle. A new `enrollment_completed_at` column (set only at step 5) distinguishes "fully submitted" from "account exists but wizard abandoned partway through," without adding workflow states nothing can reach yet.
- **OTP → email verification link.** No SMS/OTP provider exists or can be tested from this sandbox (confirmed again this phase — no outbound network access to any external host). The client's "do not activate the partner account until verification is completed" requirement is honored literally: `admin/pages/partners.php`'s Approve action now checks `email_verified_at` server-side (not just hiding the button) and refuses to approve an unverified partner — confirmed by a real POST bypass attempt against the endpoint directly, not just clicking through the UI.
- **IATA certificate is the only hard-required document**, exactly matching the spec's own conditional wording ("If Yes: IATA Certificate Upload") — every other document type is optional at registration; verification actions (approve/reject a document) are Phase 3, not this one, so every upload ships `verification_status = 'pending'` and stays there.
- **Duplicate prevention** (spec §27) checks company name/GSTIN/PAN/IATA number at step 1 (before an account exists) and email/mobile at step 2, using one shared `find_partner_duplicate()` — returns the client's exact requested message.
- **Terms & Declaration is honest about what's real**: only Terms & Conditions and Privacy Policy link to actual published pages (`/terms/`, `/privacy/`); the three B2B-specific policies (Partner Agreement, Commission Terms, Document Handling) don't have drafted legal text yet, so the checkboxes say so plainly rather than linking to or fabricating contract language — this project's "never fabricate" discipline extended to legal copy specifically.

**Admin side**: the partner profile page (`admin/pages/partners.php`) gained real Business Profile and Documents sections (reading `partner_business_profiles`/`partner_documents`, both new this phase), a new `admin/pages/partner-document-download.php` (same auth/scoping pattern as the existing `document-download.php`), and Email Verified / Enrollment status shown plainly on every partner's overview. The admin partner list shows an "Incomplete" badge for any pending partner who hasn't finished the wizard yet.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** — two full runs, cleaned up after: (1) the entire public wizard, all 5 steps plus the confirmation page, including the IATA-required block (tried skipping it, got blocked; uploaded a real PDF, got through), the JS-driven submit-button gate on the terms step (confirmed it starts disabled, confirmed the server-side check still holds if JS somehow didn't run), the dev-mode verification link, re-login correctly skipping straight to the dashboard once enrollment is complete instead of re-entering the wizard, the dashboard's unverified-email banner appearing then disappearing after clicking the real verification link, and duplicate-company-name prevention — 22/22 checks passed, zero console errors; (2) the admin side against that same real test partner: Business Profile and Documents sections render the actual submitted data, the document download link serves the real uploaded PDF, and the Approve action correctly transitions `pending → active` with a `partner_status_history` row — 16/17 automated checks passed (the one "failure" was an overly strict test regex; the underlying database state confirmed the real transition happened correctly). A separate unverified test partner confirmed the approve gate: disabled in the UI, and a direct POST bypass attempt against the endpoint was rejected server-side with the database left at `pending` — not just a hidden button. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file.

**Not done this pass** (Phase 3 territory): document verify/reject actions, `partner_notifications`, the admin approval workflow beyond approve/suspend/reactivate. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, and payment gateway integration remain explicitly deferred per the original roadmap — none of those changed this phase.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 3: admin approval/document-verification workflow + partner notifications

Third of the 10-phase B2B roadmap (see Phase 1/2 entries above). Builds the fuller approval workflow the previous two phases left as approve/suspend/reactivate only, plus the badge-only in-app notification feed the roadmap scoped for this phase (the fuller communication centre is a later phase, not this one).

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-phase3.sql`, additive): `partners.status` widened from the 3-value ENUM Phases 1–2 deliberately left untouched to `pending`/`documents_required`/`active`/`suspended`/`rejected`/`deactivated` — this phase is specifically the one building admin UI that can reach the three new states, so the deferred widening from Phase 1's own notes finally happens here, not speculatively ahead of it. New `partner_notifications` table, mirroring `customer_notifications`'s exact shape (`partner_id, type, title, body, link, is_read, created_at`) so it can reuse the same rendering pattern as the existing customer notification bell.

**`notify_partner()`** (`includes/partner-enrollment.php`): a single helper that inserts a `partner_notifications` row — badge-only, no email, matching the roadmap's own "(badge-only for now)" scoping for this phase. Called from every status transition and every document verify/reject action below.

**Admin approval workflow** (`admin/pages/partners.php`, full rewrite of the action-button block): per-status action sets — `pending`/`documents_required` → Approve (still gated on `email_verified_at`, unchanged from Phase 2), Request More Info → `documents_required`, Reject → `rejected`; `active` → Suspend, Deactivate; `suspended` → Reactivate, Deactivate; `rejected`/`deactivated` are terminal (no actions, shown plainly rather than an empty button row). Reject/Request More Info/Deactivate all require a non-empty reason — re-checked server-side (a blocked attempt with an empty reason redirects back with a flash error and leaves the database untouched, confirmed by direct query, not just by not seeing a UI change) rather than trusting a client-side `required` attribute as the real gate, consistent with this project's established pattern for every other business-rule boundary (email-verification gate, delivery-compliance gate in the forex module, etc.). Every transition now also writes its reason into `partner_status_history.remarks` (a column that existed unused since Phase 1) and calls `notify_partner()`. The Status History section on the partner profile page now shows the reason alongside each transition.

**Document verification**: the Documents section's old "verification actions aren't available yet" placeholder is replaced with real Verify/Reject controls per document (inline form, optional remarks field), mirroring the existing pattern already used in the forex module's own document-verification UI (`admin/pages/forex-requests.php`) rather than inventing a new one. Verifying/rejecting updates `partner_documents.verification_status`/`verification_remarks`/`verified_by`/`verified_at` (all columns that existed unused since Phase 2) and fires a `notify_partner()` call. Gated on the existing `partners.manage` permission — no new granular permission introduced this phase, avoiding speculative RBAC proliferation for a single new action.

**Partner-facing notifications**: `partner/pages/notifications.php` (new) is a direct clone of the customer portal's own `dashboard/pages/notifications.php` — same list-with-Mark-All-Read pattern, just reading `partner_notifications` instead of `customer_notifications`. `includes/partner-layout.php` gained a `notifications` nav entry with the same unread-count red badge as the customer dashboard's own nav (`includes/dashboard-layout.php`), copied byte-for-byte in structure since this codebase had exactly one existing precedent for that UI pattern and there was no reason to invent a second one.

**Partner dashboard reason display**: the `!is_partner_active()` branch in `partner/pages/dashboard.php` now branches per status — `documents_required`/`rejected`/`deactivated`/`suspended` each show the latest non-null `partner_status_history.remarks` for that status (a small query scoped to `partner_id` + current `status`, so a partner only ever sees the most recent reason for their *current* state, not their entire history) — rather than the previous one-size-fits-all "pending or suspended" messaging.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (28/28 checks passed, zero failures on the second run — the first run's two "failures" were the test script itself following a stale assumption about a redirect target, not application bugs, corrected before the final run): registered two fresh test partners through the real wizard (one plain, one IATA-registered with a real uploaded PDF); confirmed the reject/request-more-info/deactivate reason requirement is enforced server-side (empty-reason attempt blocked, database confirmed still at the prior status); walked one partner through the full `pending → documents_required → active → suspended → active → deactivated` lifecycle, confirming the correct action buttons appear at each state, the terminal-state message appears once deactivated, and each transition's reason shows up both in the admin Status History table and on the partner's own dashboard; confirmed the partner-side notification badge count and the notifications page itself update correctly, and that Mark All Read clears the unread count in the database; verified document rejection with remarks updates the document's badge and remarks column, fires a `document_rejected` notification, and displays correctly; verified a second partner rejected directly from `pending` sees the rejection reason on login. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. All test partner rows, status history, notifications, business profiles, and the one uploaded test PDF were removed from the local dev database/filesystem after verification.

**Not done this pass** (later-phase territory per the roadmap): partners submitting real visa applications, quotations/tiered pricing, wallet/invoicing, team sub-users, the fuller communication centre (this phase's notifications remain badge-only, no email), CSV export, document-expiry alerts, and the public SEO landing page. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, and payment gateway integration remain explicitly deferred, unchanged from prior phases. Real SMTP credentials still have not been supplied by the client — `config/smtp.php` still does not exist in this environment, so all email in this project remains in dev-mode fallback.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 4: partners submitting real visa applications + case tracking

Fourth of the 10-phase B2B roadmap. As planned from Phase 1 onward, this reuses the existing `customers`/`visa_applications` tables entirely through `customers.referred_by_partner_id` (added in Phase 1) — **no new tables**. An active, approved partner can now refer a customer and submit a real visa application on their behalf, and track it through to completion using the same `visa_application_history` table the admin CRM already writes to.

**`partner/pages/application-create.php`** (new): a form capturing the referred customer's name/email/mobile plus destination country, visa type, travel date, and notes — deliberately the same field set and dropdown sources (`countries_all()`, `visa_types_all()`) as `admin/pages/visa-applications.php`'s own "create" action, so a partner's application looks identical in the CRM to one a staff member created. Gated on `is_partner_active()` — only approved partners can refer customers, matching the intent of the approval workflow Phase 3 built. Validation follows the wizard pages' own in-page `$errors` array + redisplay pattern (not the admin layout's flash+redirect, which this partner page doesn't have available) since it's the established convention for partner-facing forms in this codebase.

**Duplicate/ownership handling**: looks up any existing customer by email or mobile before creating one. If found and it's a customer this same partner already referred, the new application attaches to that existing customer (a partner referring the same person twice doesn't create two customer records). If the email/mobile belongs to someone else's customer record — a walk-in customer, or a different partner's referral — the request is blocked with a message pointing them to Visagiri support, rather than silently attaching a new application to a customer record the partner doesn't own the referral for. This is a real access-control boundary, not just a UX nicety: verified below that Partner B genuinely cannot claim Partner A's referred customer by re-submitting their email.

**`partner/pages/applications.php`** (new): list + detail, scoped entirely by `customers.referred_by_partner_id = <this partner>` in every query (list, detail, and the 403-equivalent "not found" on a direct URL to another partner's application). The detail view's "Case Timeline" section reads the same `visa_application_history` table and renders it the same From/To/When shape as Phase 3's own Partner Status History table, for consistency within this codebase rather than inventing a different timeline widget. A read-only Documents section is also shown (type, verification status, upload date) — upload capability from the partner side is not part of this phase.

**Dashboard integration**: `partner/pages/dashboard.php` gained a "+ New Application" / "My Applications" button pair, and the existing Referred Customers table's application-count column is now a link into the applications list. `includes/partner-layout.php` gained a "My Applications" nav entry between Dashboard and Notifications.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (16/16 checks passed): registered and admin-approved two separate active test partners; confirmed the real server-side "at least one of email or mobile" validation (deliberately exercised through fields with no client-side `required`, to test the actual PHP validation path rather than the browser blocking submission); created an application for a new referred customer and confirmed the customer row, the application row, and its initial `draft` history entry all landed correctly; submitted a second application for the same customer (matched by email) and confirmed no duplicate customer row was created while the application count for that customer became 2; confirmed the dashboard and applications list both reflect the new data; confirmed Partner B gets the same "not found" response as a nonexistent record when directly navigating to Partner A's application by ID; confirmed Partner B attempting to refer a customer using Partner A's referred customer's email is blocked with the "already exists" message rather than silently taking over the record. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. One genuine test-script bug was caught and fixed during this pass, not an application bug: Playwright's `button[type="submit"]` selector was matching the sidebar's Logout button (also a bare `<button type="submit">`, and earlier in the DOM) instead of the actual form's submit button — scoping the selector to the specific form fixed it. All test partners, customers, applications, and history rows were removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done this pass** (later-phase territory): quotations/tiered pricing, wallet/invoicing, team sub-users, the fuller communication centre, CSV export, document-expiry alerts, and the public SEO landing page. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, and real SMTP credentials remain unchanged from prior phases.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 5: tiered pricing/commission engine

Fifth of the 10-phase B2B roadmap. **A real scope adjustment from the original planning notes, made after actually reading the codebase rather than assuming**: the Phase 1 roadmap had sketched this phase as "Quotations + tiered pricing/commission engine (`partner_tiers`, `partner_quotations`)." Once this phase actually started, `visa_quotes` (from the pre-existing `schema-crm.sql`) turned out to already fully cover quoting any `visa_applications` row — partner-referred or not — with its own complete admin create/view flow (`admin/pages/visa-applications.php`'s `add_quote` action, already working since long before this B2B project began). Adding a second, parallel `partner_quotations` table as originally sketched would have duplicated that entirely. So this phase built only what was genuinely missing: the tier master data and the commission calculation it drives. This is exactly the kind of check-before-building the whole B2B project has done at every phase (Phase 1's own audit found several roadmap assumptions that didn't match reality either).

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-phase5.sql`, additive): new `partner_tiers` table (name, `commission_type` ENUM `percentage`/`flat`, `commission_value`, description, sort order, active flag) and `partners.tier_id` (nullable FK → `partner_tiers`, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) — ships NULL until an admin assigns one, the same "ships empty until real" convention as `assigned_admin_id` since Phase 1.

**Admin — tier master data** (`admin/pages/partner-tiers.php`, new): a straightforward CRUD list, deliberately mirroring `admin/pages/visa-types.php`'s existing simple-master-data pattern (list + toggle-active + edit + delete) rather than inventing a new admin UI shape for what is, structurally, the same kind of thing. Gated on `partners.manage` — tiers exist purely to drive partner commissions, not a general content-management concern, so no new permission key was introduced. Deleting a tier that partners are currently on falls back those partners to "no tier assigned" rather than being blocked, consistent with the FK's `ON DELETE SET NULL` — verified this actually happens, not just assumed from the schema.

**Admin — tier assignment**: `admin/pages/partners.php`'s profile page gained a "Commission Tier" row and a Set Tier form, positioned right next to the existing Assign Manager control since they're the same kind of per-partner admin configuration action.

**The commission engine itself** (`admin/pages/visa-applications.php`): the existing manual "Partner Commission" section (set an amount, pick a status) now shows the referring partner's tier and rate, and — when no commission has been set yet — computes a suggested amount from the tier and the application's most recent quote's *service fee* specifically (not the government fee, which passes straight through to the embassy and isn't Visagiri's margin to split commission from: percentage tiers take a cut of service fee, flat tiers just use their fixed value regardless of quote figures). The suggestion is only ever a starting point, pre-filled into the same input the admin already types into — no separate "accept suggestion" workflow was added, since a plain pre-filled, editable number field already fully satisfies "engine assists, human decides" without new UI surface.

**A real bug caught during verification, not shipped**: the pre-existing `$commission = $commissionStmt->fetch();` line (written before this phase, in the original Phase-1-era code) initialized `$commission = null` but then reassigned it from `PDOStatement::fetch()`, which returns `false` — not `null` — when no row matches. Nothing before this phase ever did a strict `$commission === null` check, so the mismatch was invisible; this phase's new "only show the suggestion when no commission exists yet" guard was the first code to check it strictly, and the hint silently never rendered because `false !== null`. Caught by the Playwright verification (the pre-filled *input value* worked, since `??` treats `false['x']` as null too via PHP's loose fallback there, but the surrounding hint paragraph's strict guard didn't) — fixed by normalizing with `?: null`, a one-line change to code this phase didn't otherwise touch.

**Partner-side visibility**: the partner dashboard (`partner/pages/dashboard.php`) gained a fourth stat card showing the partner's own tier name and rate (or "No tier assigned yet"). The partner application detail page (`partner/pages/applications.php`, built in Phase 4) gained a Quotes section showing any non-draft `visa_quotes` for that application — the same data the admin side already had, now visible to the partner who referred the customer.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (13/13 checks passed on the final run): created a percentage tier (Gold, 10%) and a flat tier (Silver, ₹500) through the real admin UI; registered and approved a test partner, assigned Gold, confirmed it displays on both the admin profile and the partner's own dashboard; had the partner submit an application (reusing Phase 4's flow) and the admin add a quote (₹1,000 government / ₹2,000 service); confirmed the commission section correctly suggested ₹200 (10% of the ₹2,000 service fee) and pre-filled it into the input; saved it and confirmed the real value landed in `partner_commissions`; confirmed the partner sees the quote's fee breakdown on their own application detail page; confirmed deleting a tier that's actively assigned to a partner correctly falls that partner back to unassigned rather than blocking the delete or leaving a dangling reference. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file.

Two rounds of test-script debugging were needed before the real run, both revealing genuine (if minor) issues worth recording: (1) the same "ambiguous submit-button selector" class of bug as Phase 4 — the admin layout's own Logout button is *also* a bare `<button type="submit">` and sits earlier in the page than the tier form, so an unscoped Playwright selector silently logged the test admin out mid-flow instead of saving the tier; scoping the selector to the tier form fixed it. (2) The commission-suggestion bug described above. Both were caught specifically *because* this session insists on real browser-driven verification with strict, specific assertions rather than "the code looks right." Separately (not a bug, expected behavior): this sandbox's admin-login and partner-registration rate limiters (5 attempts / 15 minutes, `includes/auth.php`) count every attempt, not just failures, so repeated Playwright test runs against the same local dev database within a short window legitimately lock the test accounts out — cleared via a direct `DELETE FROM rate_limits` between runs, standard test-environment housekeeping, not a production concern. All test tiers, partners, customers, applications, and their related rows were removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done this pass**: wallet ledger + invoicing, team sub-users, the fuller communication centre, CSV export, document-expiry alerts, the public SEO landing page. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, and real SMTP credentials remain unchanged from prior phases.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 6: wallet ledger + invoicing

Sixth of the 10-phase B2B roadmap. Manual/staff-driven throughout — no payment gateway, the same stance the forex module has held since its own Phase A (that module never integrated one either, and nothing in this session's environment could reach one regardless).

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-phase6.sql`, additive): `partner_wallet_transactions` (partner_id, `type` ENUM `credit`/`debit`, amount, reason, an optional `reference_type`/`reference_id` pair used only to make an automatic credit idempotent, `created_by`) and `partner_invoices` (reference no, partner_id, optional period, total_amount, `status` ENUM `draft`/`issued`/`paid`, notes, timestamps). A partner's wallet balance is deliberately **not** a stored column anywhere — it's computed as `SUM(credit) - SUM(debit)` over the ledger every time it's needed, so there is nothing that can drift out of sync with the transaction history that is the actual source of truth.

**The wallet auto-credits from commissions, once**: `admin/pages/visa-applications.php`'s existing `set_commission` action (unchanged in every other respect) now inserts a wallet credit transaction whenever a commission is saved with status `approved` or `paid` and a non-null amount — but only if no wallet transaction already references that application (`reference_type = 'visa_application'`, `reference_id = <application id>`). This guard matters because `set_commission` can legitimately be called more than once on the same application (e.g. `pending → approved → paid`) without meaning to credit the same money twice — verified directly: saving the same commission a second time with a different status produced zero new wallet rows.

**Admin — wallet management**: `admin/pages/partners.php`'s profile page gained a Wallet section (running balance, full transaction table) between Commissions and Status History, plus a manual credit/debit form for anything the automatic commission credit doesn't cover — a payout via bank transfer, a correction, a bonus. This is the only way money ever leaves a partner's wallet in this system: there's no payment gateway integration to do it automatically, matching the phase's own stated scope.

**Admin — invoicing** (`admin/pages/partner-invoices.php`, new): a straightforward list/create/view flow, one linear status lifecycle (`draft → issued → paid`, no going backwards — the same shape as `visa_quotes`' and the forex module's own quotation statuses). Creating an invoice for a partner pre-fills the total amount from that partner's *current* wallet balance at the moment of creation (not automatically recalculated later — an invoice is a snapshot, not a live view), while staying fully editable in case the invoice covers a different figure. A partner's own invoices are also listed inline on their admin profile page, next to the Wallet section, with a "+ New Invoice" link that carries the partner preselected.

**Partner-side**: a new `partner/pages/wallet.php` (linked from a new "Wallet" nav item and a dashboard button) shows the partner's own balance, full transaction ledger, and invoice list — entirely read-only, since every wallet/invoice action in this phase is staff-driven by design.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (15/15 checks passed on the first full run): created a 10% commission tier and an active test partner on it; had the partner submit an application and the admin add a ₹1,000/₹2,000 quote and approve the commission — confirmed exactly one wallet credit transaction landed at ₹200 (10% of the ₹2,000 service fee, reusing Phase 5's engine) and that it shows correctly on the admin profile; re-saved the same commission with status `paid` and confirmed the wallet transaction count stayed at 1, not 2; created an invoice and confirmed its total pre-filled from the ₹200 balance; walked it through `draft → issued → paid`, confirming the terminal "no further actions" state at the end; recorded a manual ₹200 debit ("Payout via bank transfer") and confirmed the balance returned to ₹0.00; confirmed the partner's own wallet page shows the correct balance, both transactions, and the paid invoice. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. All test tiers, partners, customers, applications, wallet transactions, and invoices were removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done this pass** (the two remaining roadmap phases): team/sub-users, the fuller communication centre, real notification-bell UI beyond what Phase 3 already built; CSV exports, in-app document-expiry panel, tier recalculation; B2B enquiry capture + the public SEO landing page. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, and real SMTP credentials remain unchanged from prior phases.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 7: team/sub-users + communication centre

Seventh of the 10-phase B2B roadmap. The original Phase 1 planning notes described this phase as also needing "real notification-bell UI (genuinely new pattern, none exists anywhere yet)" — that part turned out to already be done: Phase 3 built exactly that (badge-only `partner_notifications`, unread-count nav badge). So this phase's actual new work was the other two pieces: letting a partner company add additional logins under their account, and a real two-way message thread between a partner and Visagiri staff.

**Team/sub-users — a second identity table, not a rewrite of the first.** `partner_team_members` (new, `database/schema-b2b-phase7.sql`) holds *additional* logins scoped to an existing `partner_id` — each with its own email/password_hash and a `role` (`manager` or `viewer`). The original owner login (`partners.email`/`password_hash`) is completely unchanged; "owner" isn't a stored role value at all, it's simply the absence of a team-member session, checked in code. `includes/partner-auth.php`'s `log_in_partner()` gained an optional second parameter to record which identity is active; `current_partner_role()` re-derives the role from the database on every check rather than trusting anything client-supplied. `partner/pages/login.php` now falls through to the team-members table when the primary login doesn't match, requiring `status = 'active'` (an `invited` member hasn't set a password yet; `suspended` is blocked). Deliberately **no "remember me" for team-member logins** — the existing remember-cookie mechanism only stores a `partner_id`, and resuming it would silently log the browser back in as the owner, quietly discarding the team member's own more limited role; rather than build a parallel remember-token system for a secondary identity, this phase just doesn't offer the checkbox there.

Invitations (`partner/pages/team.php`, owner-only for every mutation) use the exact same token pattern as email verification and password reset (`create_partner_team_invite()`/`verify_partner_team_invite_token()`/`accept_partner_team_invite()` in `partner-auth.php` — random 32-byte token, only its SHA-256 hash stored, this one with a 7-day expiry since it's an out-of-band invite rather than a same-session link) and the same `send_mail()` + `APP_DEBUG` on-screen fallback every other partner-facing email in this project already uses. `partner/pages/team-accept.php` is where an invited member sets their password and gets auto-logged-in. A `viewer` can see the dashboard, applications, wallet, and invoices but is blocked — server-side, not just a hidden button — from `partner/pages/application-create.php` (403), and the "+ New Application" button is hidden from their nav/dashboard/applications-list views. Only the owner sees the invite form and the Suspend/Reactivate/Remove controls on the team roster.

**Communication centre — extends the table that already does this job for customers.** `dashboard/pages/messages.php` (pre-existing, from the original site build) already implements a real two-way per-application chat by reusing `visa_application_notes` with a `customer_id`/`visible_to_customer` pair rather than a dedicated messages table. This phase adds the equivalent `partner_id`/`visible_to_partner` pair to that same table (`schema-b2b-phase7.sql`) instead of standing up a parallel `partner_messages` table that would just duplicate the identical shape — the two audiences stay completely separate because each query filters on its own flag; a partner never sees the customer-facing thread on the same application and vice versa. `partner/pages/messages.php` mirrors the customer version almost line-for-line, scoped to `customers.referred_by_partner_id = <this partner>`. On the admin side, `admin/pages/visa-applications.php`'s existing note-composer form gained a second checkbox ("Send as a message the referring partner can see in their portal", shown only when the application has a referring partner) alongside the pre-existing customer one, and sending a partner-visible note calls `notify_partner()` — so a new staff message shows up in the partner's Phase 3 notification badge, not just silently in the thread. All team-member logins post messages attributed to the partner company as a whole ("You" / "Partner"), not to the individual team member — this project doesn't track which specific team member sent a given message, a deliberate scope limit rather than an oversight.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (17/17 checks passed): registered and approved a test partner (owner); invited a `viewer` team member and captured the dev-mode invite link; the viewer accepted the invite, set a password, and landed on the dashboard with the "New Application" button correctly absent; confirmed direct navigation to `application-create` is blocked server-side with a 403, not just hidden; confirmed the viewer's own credentials work for a fresh login after logging out; had the owner suspend the viewer and confirmed the suspended account can no longer log in at all ("Invalid email or password", not a "suspended" message that would leak account existence); had the owner submit an application, the admin send a partner-visible note, confirmed the owner's notification badge incremented and the message appeared in their thread; had the owner reply, and confirmed the admin sees the reply tagged "Partner" with the "Visible to partner" badge. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. One test-script-only bug caught before the real run: the dev-mode invite link regex was hardcoded to the Playwright test's own port (8099) instead of the site's actual configured `APP_URL` (8106 in this environment) — the same class of mistake documented in this project's very first wizard-verification pass, now caught and fixed the same way (match the token generically, don't hardcode the host). All test partners, team members, customers, applications, notes, and notifications were removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done this pass** (the one remaining roadmap phase): CSV exports, in-app document-expiry panel, tier recalculation; B2B enquiry capture + the public SEO landing page. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, and real SMTP credentials remain unchanged from prior phases.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 8: CSV exports, document-expiry panel, tier recalculation

Eighth of the 10-phase B2B roadmap — the roadmap's own wording specified all three of these should be admin-triggered, not cron-driven, since no cron infrastructure exists anywhere in this project.

**CSV export — the first anywhere in this codebase.** Confirmed by grep before writing anything: no page in this entire project has ever generated a CSV. `includes/csv-export.php` (new) is one small shared helper, `export_csv_response()`, that every export action now calls rather than each page hand-rolling its own `header()`/`fputcsv()` sequence — UTF-8 BOM included so Excel (still the most likely opener) renders non-ASCII company names correctly instead of guessing the wrong encoding. Three export points, all read-only and respecting existing RBAC scoping (a Relationship Manager scoped to their own assigned partners can only export their own partners, exactly like the list view they're already restricted to): the Partners list (`?action=export`, honors whatever search/status filter is currently applied — export what you're looking at, not always everything), and per-partner Wallet Transactions / Commissions exports from the profile page (only shown when there's data to export).

**Document-expiry panel** (`admin/pages/partner-document-expiry.php`, new): `partner_documents.expiry_date` has existed since Phase 2 but nothing ever read it. This page lists every partner document that has an expiry date on file, soonest (or already-expired) first, so staff can chase IATA certificate/license renewals before they lapse — expired shown in red, expiring within 30 days in amber, everything else green. Deliberately no email reminders or automated notifications here — that's the "in-app panel" scope the roadmap asked for, not a notification system; an admin has to actually look at the page.

**Admin-triggered tier recalculation**: `partner_tiers` gained a nullable `min_referred_customers` qualification threshold (`database/schema-b2b-phase8.sql`) — NULL means "manual assignment only, never touched by recalculation," which is what every tier created before this phase keeps doing until an admin deliberately sets a threshold on it. A new "Recalculate Tier" button on the partner profile counts that partner's referred customers and finds the highest-`sort_order` active tier whose threshold is met (reusing `sort_order` as the tier-ranking signal, the same "display order" convention this codebase already uses for visa types and countries, rather than inventing a second ranking field) — assigns it if it differs from the current tier, notifies the partner (`notify_partner()`, so it shows up in their Phase 3 notification badge), and reports "no change needed" if recalculating lands on the same tier the partner is already on. This is one click, not automatic — nothing recalculates on its own, matching the "admin-triggered, not cron" instruction exactly.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (13/13 checks passed): created a 0-referral "Bronze" tier and a 2-referral "Gold" tier; registered and approved a test partner and recalculated their tier with zero referrals, landing correctly on Bronze; recalculated again with still zero referrals and confirmed it correctly reported no change; had the partner refer 2 customers via the real application-create flow, recalculated again, and confirmed the upgrade to Gold; confirmed two real tier-change notifications were recorded (one per actual transition — unassigned→Bronze, then Bronze→Gold); downloaded the Partners CSV export via a real authenticated request and confirmed both the `text/csv` content-type and that the test partner's row (with its new Gold tier) is present in the file; inserted a document with a 10-day expiry directly (the document *upload* UI doesn't currently collect an expiry date at all — a real, pre-existing gap this phase didn't set out to close, since it was scoped to building the panel that reads this column, not extending every upload form to capture it) and confirmed the expiry panel lists it with the correct partner, document type, and "expires in ~10 days" status. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. All test tiers, partners, customers, applications, notifications, and the one manually-inserted test document were removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done this pass** (the one remaining roadmap phase): B2B enquiry capture + the public SEO landing page. Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, and real SMTP credentials remain unchanged from prior phases — this remains explicitly deferred, blocked on the client supplying real third-party credentials, per the original Phase 1 roadmap.

## B2B Partner CRM — Phase 9: enquiry capture + public SEO landing page

Ninth of the 10-phase B2B roadmap — the last buildable phase. Phase 10 (real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, virus scanning, transactional email/SMTP) remains explicitly deferred, blocked on client-supplied third-party credentials, unchanged from the original Phase 1 roadmap; there is nothing actionable there in this sandbox.

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-phase9.sql`, additive): a new `partner_enquiries` table — reference number, company/contact/email/mobile, optional business type and message, `status` ENUM `new`/`contacted`/`converted`/`closed`, optional `assigned_admin_id`, IP address, soft-delete columns. This is a lead-capture record for a *prospective* partner who isn't ready to go through the full registration wizard yet, not a partner account — deliberately a separate table from `partners` rather than a pre-partner status on that table, matching how `general_enquiries` already exists separately from `customers` for exactly the same "public form → staff follow-up, may or may not convert" shape.

**Public landing page** (`pages/partner-program.php`, new, routed at `/partner-program/`): hero, a six-item benefits grid, and a four-step "How It Works" list — every claim on the page describes a feature this project actually built in Phases 1-8 (tiered commissions, real-time case tracking, wallet/invoicing, team accounts, direct messaging, document management), nothing aspirational or fabricated. Two calls to action: "Register Now" straight into the real wizard (`partner/pages/register.php`), and this page's own enquiry form for a prospect not ready to commit. The form follows `pages/enquire.php`'s established pattern exactly — same honeypot field, same `rate_limit_check()` call (5/900s, the same key shape as every other public form), same `generate_reference_number('PENQ', 'partner_enquiries', 'reference_number')` retry-on-collision loop, same `notify_enquiry_channels()` call so a submission reaches the same Google-Sheets/email channels every other enquiry type already does. SEO metadata (title/description/canonical/BreadcrumbList JSON-LD) follows `pages/documentation.php`'s shape. Linked from the footer's Resources column and reachable via `public/index.php`'s router.

**Admin CRM** (`admin/pages/partner-enquiries.php`, new, routed at `/admin/partner-enquiries/`): list (with a status-filter button row) + detail + status/assignment update + soft delete, mirroring `admin/pages/general-enquiries.php`'s pattern line-for-line since it's the same record shape. Gated on the existing `partners.view`/`partners.manage` keys — no new permission introduced, since this is squarely a partners-namespace concern. The detail view carries an explicit note that setting status to "Converted" is a status label only and does not itself create a partner account — staff still have to walk the prospect through the real wizard or point them to it.

**A real, pre-existing, site-wide bug found and fixed — not a CSP issue.** While verifying the new Delete button, a soft-delete that looked successful in the browser (the page navigated to the enquiries list, as if a redirect had fired) turned out to leave `deleted_at` untouched in the database every time. The cause: this page's `$id` was read only from `$_GET['id']`, but the Delete form's `action` attribute deliberately omits the query string (it posts to the bare list URL, relying on a hidden `<input name="id">` in the POST body instead — the same shape the Update form on the same page uses, except Update's `action` *does* carry `?id=`). With `$id` null on that POST, the `$postAction === 'delete' && $id` guard was always false, so the whole delete branch was silently skipped and the request fell straight through to the bottom of the file, which renders the ordinary enquiries list — indistinguishable from a real post-delete redirect unless you check the database. Twelve other admin pages already guard against exactly this by falling back to `$_POST['id']` when `$_GET['id']` isn't set (`admin/pages/visa-types.php`, `customers.php`, `users.php`, `partner-tiers.php`, `countries.php`, `faqs.php`, `embassies.php`, and others) — this new page copied the pattern from `general-enquiries.php`, which turned out to be one of only two existing pages missing that fallback (the other being `admin/pages/visa-applications.php`'s own Delete button). All three now use the same `$_GET['id'] ?? $_POST['id']` fallback as the other twelve; **this means Delete on general enquiries and on visa applications has been silently non-functional in the admin panel this whole time**, not just on this new page — a real, previously-undiscovered defect this phase's verification surfaced and fixed as a drive-by, since it's a one-line, low-risk change directly adjacent to what this phase was already touching. (An earlier hypothesis during debugging — that the site's `script-src 'self'` CSP was silently blocking every inline `onsubmit="return confirm(...)"` handler project-wide — was investigated and ruled out: the delete-confirmation `confirm()` dialog genuinely doesn't fire under CSP, exactly as suspected, but the delete request itself was never blocked by that; it fails purely because of the `$id` bug above. The missing confirmation dialog is a separate, lower-severity, genuinely pre-existing UX gap — present on every admin delete button across the whole panel — that this phase did not attempt to fix, since it's unrelated to the enquiries feature and its safest fix (moving confirmation into `assets/js/admin.js` as a real event listener, which the CSP already permits) touches every admin page with a delete button, not just this one.**

**A second real, this-phase-only bug found and fixed**: the public landing page's real (non-honeypot) enquiry submission inserted the `partner_enquiries` row correctly but then threw `Call to undefined function notify_enquiry_channels()`, which the surrounding try/catch swallowed into a generic "something went wrong" error message shown to the visitor — even though their enquiry had, in fact, been saved. `includes/google-sheets.php` (where `notify_enquiry_channels()` lives) is `require`d explicitly at the top of every other page that calls it (`pages/enquire.php`, `pages/contact.php`, `attestation/index.php`) rather than being autoloaded globally; the new landing page never added that `require`. Fixed by adding it. Caught by the Playwright verification's real-submission assertion failing even though the database check on the very next line passed — a good example of why this phase (like every phase before it) insists on asserting the actual rendered response, not just the resulting database state.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (14/14 checks passed on the final run, after both fixes above): landing page loads with correct title/canonical/JSON-LD; hero content and the Register CTA are present; a honeypot-triggered submission creates no database row; a real submission succeeds, shows the `VG-PENQ-YYYY-NNNNNN` reference number on-screen, and creates a `status = 'new'` row; the admin enquiries list shows the new submission; the detail view shows company/contact/message correctly; updating status and assignee persists; deleting the enquiry actually soft-deletes it now (confirmed via direct DB check, not just the UI) and it drops off the list. `php -l` clean across every changed/new file. Two test-script-only issues were also found and fixed while chasing the two real bugs above (neither is an application defect): the canonical-URL assertion compared against the Playwright test server's own host (`127.0.0.1:8099`) instead of the site's configured `APP_URL` (`127.0.0.1:8106` in this environment) — the same class of mistake this project has now hit and fixed in three separate phases, always by matching the path suffix generically instead of hardcoding a host; and the real-submission assertion originally used `Promise.all([waitForNavigation().catch(() => {}), click(...)])`, which papered over a genuine timeout (the landing page renders its success message inline rather than redirecting, so an un-caught `waitForNavigation` was in fact the right primitive once the real bug above was fixed, since a plain form POST is still a real page navigation even without a `Location` header). All test enquiry rows and `rate_limits` rows were removed from the local dev database after verification.

**Not done this pass**: fixing the missing delete-confirmation dialog site-wide (documented above as a real but separate, lower-severity issue spanning every admin page with a delete button, not scoped to this phase's work). Real mobile OTP/SMS, WhatsApp Business API, Google OAuth, payment gateway, and real SMTP credentials remain unchanged from prior phases — Phase 10 stays explicitly deferred, blocked on the client supplying real third-party credentials. With Phase 9 complete, every buildable phase of the original 10-phase B2B roadmap is now done.

## Utility Bar Redesign — indicative USD→INR rate + unchanged Login panel

A client-supplied redesign brief for the header's top utility strip: replace it with two things only — an indicative USD→INR rate on the left, and the existing Login/Sign-Up panel untouched on the right, no other clutter.

**Most of the backend already existed and was reused, not rebuilt.** Before writing anything, checked the current header and found `includes/currency-rate.php` already implements exactly what the brief asked for: a live fetch from Frankfurter (no API key needed), a 1-hour file cache, graceful fallback to the last successfully-cached rate (clearly marked "Last known rate") when the live fetch fails, and `rate: null` — never a fabricated number — if there has never been a successful fetch at all. A `/api-usd-inr-rate/` JSON endpoint and a same-session 5-minute polling refresh in `assets/js/site.js` already existed too. None of that needed touching; this pass was a front-end restructuring of the utility bar itself plus one small new interactive component.

**What changed** (`includes/header.php`, `public/assets/css/layout.css`, `public/assets/js/site.js`):
- Removed the "Technology Meets Travel & Visa" tagline and the "24×7 Support" WhatsApp link from the utility bar — both were real, working links, not deleted from the site, just out of scope for what the brief wanted this specific bar to communicate. WhatsApp (and email, and Enquire Now) stay one tap away sitewide via the floating `includes/enquiry-widget.php`, which every public page already includes independently of the header.
- The rate display is now a real interactive component (`.site-header__rate-widget`), not a static `<span>`: a `<button>` trigger opens a popover on hover (desktop, via the existing `.has-dropdown` CSS already used by the Login menu) or tap (touch, via JS), showing the full rate, "Indicative exchange rate," a "Last updated: [date, time]" line sourced from the same `as_of` timestamp the cache already tracked, the disclaimer text the brief specified ("Rates may vary from actual bank/provider rates."), and a "Get Forex Assistance" CTA.
- The Login dropdown itself — trigger, all three options (Customer/Employee/B2B Partner), styling, behavior — is untouched, per the brief's explicit "do not redesign the Login panel" instruction. It simply moved from being the third item in a crowded row to being the utility bar's only other element.
- Mobile: the utility bar used to be hidden entirely below 768px (`display: none`), with a duplicate, separate rate readout living inside the hamburger drawer instead. Per the brief's explicit mobile section, the bar itself is now visible at every width, compacted to the rate only (Login stays reachable via the hamburger drawer's own pre-existing Login section, so it isn't duplicated in an already-narrow bar); the "Indicative Rate" label text is hidden below 420px while the rate figure itself stays visible, matching the brief's "compact version" instruction exactly.

**The "Get Forex Assistance" CTA connects to a real, existing CRM pipeline, not a new or fabricated one.** The brief said it "should connect to the existing Forex enquiry CRM" — checking the codebase first (not assuming) found there is no dedicated public forex-enquiry landing page; forex requests are staff-created from the admin side only. Rather than inventing a new page/table for this, the CTA links to `/contact/?topic=forex`, and `pages/contact.php` (the site's one general-purpose public enquiry form, already feeding `general_enquiries` with a generated reference number and the same Google-Sheets/email notification path every other enquiry type uses) now recognizes that query parameter: it prefills the message with real context ("I saw the indicative USD to INR rate on your website and would like help with a forex/currency exchange requirement."), swaps the eyebrow/heading copy to "Forex Assistance," and — since `general_enquiries.service_type` is a free-text column, not an ENUM — tags the saved row `service_type = 'forex'` instead of `'general'`, a genuinely queryable distinction for staff. Plain `/contact/` visits are completely unaffected.

**A real, pre-existing bug found and fixed while building this, affecting the Login dropdown too, not just the new rate widget.** Playwright verification of the new popover's Escape-to-close behavior kept failing: the JS correctly removed the `is-open` class, but the popover stayed visually open. Root cause: the existing Login dropdown's own Escape handler calls `.focus()` on the trigger afterward (intentionally, so keyboard focus isn't lost) — but `.has-dropdown`'s CSS also opens the menu on `:focus-within`, so refocusing the trigger immediately re-triggers the exact CSS rule that was supposed to be defeated by removing `is-open`. This is a genuine, previously-undiscovered defect in the pre-existing Login dropdown, not something introduced by the new component — confirmed directly: pressing Escape on the unmodified Login menu with the mouse still over its trigger left it visibly open too. The mega-menu component (`initMegaMenu()` in `site.js`) had already solved this exact problem with an `is-force-closed` CSS class cleared on `mouseenter`/`mouseleave`/`focusout` — rather than reinventing a fix, both the Login trigger and the new rate trigger were refactored onto one shared `initSimpleDropdown(triggerId, menuId)` helper that reuses that same proven pattern, fixing the bug in both places at once and removing what had been two near-duplicate hand-rolled implementations.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (29/29 checks passed): tagline and WhatsApp-support link confirmed removed from the utility bar; rate widget renders with the correct `USD → INR ₹XX.XX` figure; popover hidden by default, opens on click, sets `aria-expanded="true"`, shows the rate value/"Indicative exchange rate"/"Last updated"/disclaimer text, closes on an outside click and closes on Escape (the fixed behavior); Login dropdown confirmed still exactly the three original options with Employee Login still pointing at `/admin/login/`; clicked the real CTA through to `/contact/?topic=forex`, confirmed the message field prefilled and the eyebrow changed, submitted the real form, and confirmed via direct DB query the saved row was tagged `service_type = 'forex'`; confirmed a plain `/contact/` visit is unaffected (original eyebrow, empty message field); on a 375px mobile viewport confirmed the utility bar is visible, compact, single-row, and the Login trigger is not duplicated there; on a 360px viewport confirmed the "Indicative Rate" label is hidden while the rate figure stays visible. `php -l`/`node --check` clean on every changed file. Since this sandbox has no outbound network access to the live rate API and no rate had ever been successfully cached here, verification used a locally-seeded test cache file (`storage/cache/usd-inr-rate.json`, which is gitignored via `storage/*` and was deleted again after verification) purely to exercise the interactive/populated-rate code paths — the "Rate unavailable" fallback path (confirmed to render correctly with no rate cached) is what this environment will actually show until it either gets real network access or a first successful fetch happens in production.

**Not done this pass** (out of scope per the brief): the optional EUR→INR secondary currency — the brief explicitly frames it as "if there is sufficient space" and "do not overcrowd," and USD→INR alone already fills the utility bar's intended minimal footprint, so it was left out rather than added speculatively; it would be a small, additive follow-up (Frankfurter supports fetching multiple target currencies in one request) if the client wants it later. The pre-existing mobile hamburger drawer's own separate rate/tagline/support block was left untouched — it's a different UI surface (the slide-out menu, not the utility bar itself) and wasn't part of what the brief asked to redesign.

## Utility Bar — restored WhatsApp "24×7 Support" link

Follow-up to the utility bar redesign above: the client asked to keep the WhatsApp "24×7 Support" link in the bar after all, rather than relying solely on the sitewide floating enquiry widget for it.

**What changed** (`includes/header.php`, `public/assets/css/layout.css`): restored the WhatsApp link between the rate widget and the (still-unchanged) Login dropdown, wrapped in the same `.site-header__utility-contact` flex group the pre-redesign markup used. On mobile, the link's icon stays visible in the now-compact utility bar while its "24×7 Support" text label hides below 768px (wrapped in a new `.site-header__utility-link-text` span so CSS can target just the text, the same technique already used for hiding "Indicative Rate" at very small widths) — keeping the bar to one line without wrapping, same constraint as before.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (12/12 checks passed): rate widget, WhatsApp link, and Login dropdown all present and in the correct left-to-right order; the WhatsApp link still opens in a new tab with the correct message; the Escape-key close fix (from the previous pass) still works on both the rate popover and the Login menu; on a 375px mobile viewport the rate and the WhatsApp icon are both visible, the Login trigger stays hidden from the utility bar (still reachable via the hamburger drawer, unchanged), the bar stays a single compact row, and the "24×7 Support" text is hidden while the icon remains. `php -l` clean. Test rate cache seeded for verification was deleted afterward, same as the prior pass.

## Legal & Support Centre — footer redesign, 7 policy pages, grievance ticketing, contact/sitemap overhaul

A client-supplied 25-section brief asking to redesign the footer-linked legal/support pages (Privacy Policy, Terms & Conditions, Travel Terms, Payment Policy, Refund Policy, Cookie Policy, Grievance Redressal, Sitemap, Contact Us) into a professional "Legal & Support Centre," plus a footer overhaul — all 9 existing URLs preserved exactly, since they may already be indexed.

**Research before any design decision, same discipline as every prior phase.** A background research pass, then a design pass, confirmed two things the brief assumed rather than verified against this codebase: (1) none of the 7 policy pages had any real content — all 7, plus `/disclaimer/`, shared one generic "not ready yet" stub (`pages/legal.php`), `noindex`'d; (2) no SLA/escalation system, and no Team Leader/Manager roles, exist anywhere in this project, despite the brief describing a 4-level auto-escalation chain and referring to "the existing CRM SLA system" as though it already existed. Two decisions were put to the user directly rather than guessed at, and both were confirmed before writing any code:

1. **Unverifiable legal facts** (registered office address, governing-law/jurisdiction, grievance-officer contact) are never invented and never shown as bracket placeholders — they're new empty-by-default `setting()` keys (`legal_registered_office_address`, `legal_governing_law`, `legal_jurisdiction_city`, `legal_grievance_officer_name`, `legal_grievance_officer_email`), and every page template omits the sentence/clause referencing an unset key entirely rather than rendering it fake or broken-looking. An admin fills these in later via `/admin/settings/`, zero code changes — the same "ships empty until real" convention as `partner_tiers.min_referred_customers` since Phase 8 of the B2B build.
2. **Grievance SLA/escalation is real but deliberately scoped down**: a genuine SLA due-time per grievance (computed from a configurable `grievance_sla_hours` setting, default 72), a visible breach badge computed at render time (no cron exists anywhere in this project, so this is never a stored flag — `sla_due_at < NOW() AND status NOT IN (resolved, closed)`, checked fresh on every page load), and a manual "Escalate" button that reassigns to another admin holding `grievances.manage` and logs it via the existing `log_action()` audit pattern. No new roles were invented, no automatic multi-level chain was built — that was explicitly ruled out by the user rather than quietly dropped.

**Schema** (`database/schema-legal-phase1.sql`, `database/seed-legal-phase1.sql`, additive): a standalone `grievances` table (not an ALTER of the shared `general_enquiries` table, which is used by attestation/general/forex enquiries too and has no room for SLA columns without polluting all of them) — reference number, category, contact fields, `sla_due_at`/`escalated_at`/`escalated_from_user`/`escalation_reason`/`resolved_at`, soft-delete, the same shape as `partner_enquiries`/`general_enquiries`. A sibling nullable `documents.grievance_id` column, following the table's existing `visa_application_id`/`general_enquiry_id` pattern, so grievance attachments reuse the real document-upload system. Two new permissions (`grievances.view`/`grievances.manage`), granted to `super-admin` and `admin` via the exact `CROSS JOIN ... LIKE` idiom `seed-b2b.sql` established — no new role. `grievance_sla_hours` plus the five empty-by-default legal-fact settings, all added to `admin/pages/settings.php`'s existing `$fields` array alongside the per-page "Last updated" dates (see below).

**A real design correction caught before writing any content**, not shipped as originally sketched: the plan's first draft proposed mirroring `includes/partner-layout.php`'s two-function page-lifecycle shape (`render_legal_start()`/`render_legal_end()`) for the shared legal-page component. Re-reading `partner-layout.php` closely revealed *why* its second function deliberately skips `require`ing `includes/footer.php` — footer.php reads `$visaServiceLinks`/`$attestationCategories`/`$companyMenu`, which become local variables inside whichever function calls `header.php`, and don't survive across a second function's call boundary. That's the right tradeoff for a logged-in app shell (partner portal), where a full marketing footer wouldn't belong anyway — but blindly copying it onto the new legal pages would have silently dropped the real sitewide footer from all 7 public policy pages. Redesigned `includes/legal-layout.php` as two content-fragment functions (`render_legal_hero()`/`render_legal_hero_close()`) instead, called from each page's own top-level script exactly like `pages/documentation.php` already does — `header.php` and `footer.php` both required directly by the page, in the same scope, so the real footer renders correctly everywhere.

**The 6 pure-content pages** (`pages/privacy.php`, `terms.php`, `travel-terms.php`, `payment-policy.php`, `refund-policy.php`, `cookie-policy.php`, new): each uses the shared hero/breadcrumb/sticky-desktop-TOC/mobile-accordion component, with real prose grounded in what this codebase actually does — Privacy Policy cites the real AES-256-GCM field encryption (`includes/encryption.php`) and the real Google Sheets/Drive staff-notification bridge (`includes/google-sheets.php`) rather than generic security boilerplate; Terms & Conditions reuses the footer's own "does not guarantee visa approval" disclaimer sentence verbatim rather than paraphrasing it differently in two places; Payment Policy was written only after confirming there is no payment gateway anywhere in this codebase (`visa_payments`/`forex_payments` both record `payment_method` as staff-entered free text) — so it describes the real offline/staff-coordinated flow, and accurately cites the real government-fee/service-fee split already in `visa_quotes`; Cookie Policy was written only after grep confirmed there is no GA/GTM or any analytics script anywhere on the site, so it explicitly states analytics/preference/marketing cookies are "not currently used" rather than fabricating categories; Travel Terms frames flight/hotel/insurance sections as what applies *when* such assistance is provided alongside a visa service, never as a standing claim that Visagiri is a standalone travel agency, consistent with this project's established service-scope discipline.

**Grievance Redressal** (`pages/grievance.php`, new, replaces the stub): an intro (commitment, 5-step process, a response-time statement that reads `setting('grievance_sla_hours')` live so it can never drift from what the admin side actually enforces) plus a real form on the exact `contact.php`/`enquire.php` skeleton — honeypot, `csrf_require()`, `rate_limit_check()`, `generate_reference_number('GRV', ...)` with the standard 2-attempt retry-on-collision loop, an optional file attachment via a new `store_grievance_document_upload()` (`includes/documents.php`) that deliberately leaves both `uploaded_by` and `uploaded_by_customer_id` NULL, since a public grievance submitter is neither a logged-in admin nor a logged-in customer — a genuinely new uploader category this project hadn't needed before, confirmed correct by checking that `documents.uploaded_by` was already made nullable by an earlier migration (`schema-customer-partner-portal.sql`) specifically to support non-admin uploaders.

**Admin CRM** (`admin/pages/grievances.php`, new, cloned structure from `admin/pages/general-enquiries.php`): list/detail/status/priority/assignment/soft-delete, each change audit-logged exactly as `general-enquiries.php` already does, plus the SLA breach badge (list and detail) and the manual Escalate action described above. New nav entry in `includes/admin-layout.php`, gated on `has_permission('grievances.view')`, and new routes in `admin/index.php`.

**Contact page redesigned into a real "How Can We Help?" service selector** (`pages/contact.php`, modified): the existing `?topic=forex` boolean (shipped in an earlier session for the header utility bar's "Get Forex Assistance" CTA) became one case of a proper 5-way selector — Visa Assistance / Apostille & Attestation / Forex / Travel Services / General Enquiry — still writing into the same `general_enquiries` table with `service_type` tagged per selection, per the brief's own instruction to connect to the existing CRM rather than build a new one. `?topic=forex` is kept as a permanent backward-compatible alias so the header CTA's existing link never breaks. Added real contact cards (WhatsApp/Email/Call), all sourced from `setting()` — no invented phone numbers, addresses, or emails.

**Sitemap rebuilt** (`pages/sitemap.php`, modified): visual card-grid redesign with section icons (reusing the existing `primary_nav_icon()` helper), reorganized into Visa Services / Visa by Country / Apostille & Attestation / Documents / Travel & Forex / Resources / Contact & Support / Legal & Policies / About Visagiri. Every link was checked against the real route list in `public/index.php` before being added — no fabricated "Travel Services" landing page exists, so that category links to the real routes that do exist instead (`/travel-terms/`, `/forex/track/`, `/contact/?service=travel`, `/contact/?service=forex`). Fixed the two self-references (`/sitemap/`, `/contact/`) the old sitemap was missing from its own Legal & Support section.

**Footer redesigned** (`includes/footer.php`, modified): added a "Need Assistance?" CTA banner and a bottom contact strip (WhatsApp | Call Us | Email Us | Get Assistance) between the existing disclaimer bar and the legal-row — all 9 existing footer legal-row URLs untouched. "Get Assistance" (both the banner button and the strip item) opens the existing floating enquiry widget via a small new JS hook (`.js-open-enquiry-widget` in `assets/js/site.js`) rather than building a second enquiry surface, per the brief's own "open the existing quick enquiry dialogue" instruction. Migrated the previously-hardcoded copyright line (parent company/CIN/founding date) to read from `setting()`, with the exact current values as defaults so nothing visibly changes unless an admin edits Settings — added a small `format_founding_date()` helper (`includes/functions.php`) to turn the stored `YYYY-MM` into "April 2015" display text rather than adding a duplicate setting.

**A real bug caught and fixed during verification, not shipped**: the new footer "Get Assistance" triggers opened the floating enquiry widget and then immediately closed it again in the same click — the widget's own pre-existing "close on outside click" document-level listener didn't know to ignore clicks on the new trigger buttons (which live in the footer, outside `#enquiry-widget` by design), so the same click that opened it also satisfied "clicked outside the widget" once the event bubbled to `document`. Fixed by excluding `.js-open-enquiry-widget` elements from that outside-click check.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright across every phase, never just code review** (final tallies): 6 content pages, 74/75 (the one "failure" was a test-script label mismatch, not an app bug — Travel Terms' actual H1 "Travel Terms & Conditions" doesn't equal the shorter footer-link label "Travel Terms" the test reused for both checks); Grievance round-trip as one connected flow (submit → reference number → admin list/detail → status update → age the SLA into the past → breach badge appears → escalate → audit log entries for both status_change and escalation → 403 for a non-privileged admin account), 19/19; Contact service selector, all 5 services plus the legacy alias plus the client-side toggle plus the contact cards, 28/28; Sitemap, every one of 64 unique links on the page crawled and confirmed non-4xx/5xx, 7/7; Footer CTA/contact-strip/copyright-migration/legal-row regression (including confirming the shared footer still renders correctly on `/privacy/`, not just the homepage), 16/16; a final consolidated sweep — SEO metadata (title/canonical/robots/description/JSON-LD) on `/grievance/`, `/contact/`, `/sitemap/` plus confirming `/disclaimer/` correctly stayed `noindex`'d as the unchanged stub it was always meant to remain, 19/19; mobile no-horizontal-overflow spot-check across grievance/contact/sitemap/footer, 5/5. `php -l` clean on every new/changed PHP file. All test grievances, general-enquiry test rows, and rate-limit rows were removed from the local dev database after verification — confirmed empty by direct query before this entry was written.

**Not done this pass, by deliberate decision, not oversight**: `/disclaimer/` was left on the shared stub, unchanged — it was never one of the 9 target URLs. The client's own previously-deferred "Unified Enquiry CRM + SLA Assignment System" proposal (recorded earlier in this document) was not resurrected — SLA tracking here is scoped only to the new standalone `grievances` table; `general_enquiries`, `visa_enquiries`, and the Forex module remain untouched. No Team Leader/Manager roles and no automatic multi-level escalation chain were built, per the user's own explicit decision recorded above.

## National Location SEO — Phase 1: states/UTs data model, admin CMS, `/visa-consultant/{state}/{city}/`, 2 real pilot pages (Bihar/Patna, Uttar Pradesh/Noida)

Client sent a 42-section "national SEO dominance" spec asking Visagiri to rank for `[Visa Agency/Consultant/Agent/Consultancy] + [Location]` across all 28 Indian states, 8 union territories, their major cities, and country×city combinations — while explicitly forbidding the shortcuts that make that easy: no doorway pages, no "swap the city name" templated content, no fake `LocalBusiness`/address/reviews, no mass-indexing without a real quality gate, no keyword stuffing. The spec's own instructions require auditing the existing codebase before building anything, and finding real content for 36 states/UTs plus dozens of cities plus 15 countries is a genuinely unbounded task — so this ships the complete, reusable machinery plus two fully real, content-complete pilot state/city pairs, not an attempt at national coverage in one pass.

**Audit before building** (confirmed by direct grep/read): no `states`/`cities` tables and no `/visa-consultant/`-style routes existed anywhere. The closest analog, the `countries` system, was mirrored column-for-column: `countries` (`id, name, slug UNIQUE, region_id, is_popular_destination, is_active, meta_title, meta_description`), `admin/pages/countries.php` (CMS gated on `require_permission('content.manage')`), `includes/data.php`'s `countries_all()`/`country_by_slug()`, and `visa/index.php`'s `$segments`-based sub-routing (`$segments[1]` = country slug, `$segments[2]` = type slug, a separate `hub.php` for the bare listing, `render_not_found()` for bad slugs) were all reused as the pattern for `states`/`cities`/`visa-consultant/`. `visa_faqs`' nullable dual-scope shape (`country_id NULL`, `visa_type_id NULL`) was reused directly for the new `location_faqs` table. `pages/sitemap-xml.php` already had an explicit "don't submit thin pages" philosophy in its own docblock — the same discipline the spec demands was already proven out in this codebase, just extended to the new tables.

**Physical-office question, asked directly before writing any schema** (the spec explicitly forbids fake `LocalBusiness`/address claims, and this codebase had no existing fact to fall back on — `site_settings.legal_registered_office_address` was still empty from the earlier Legal Phase): user confirmed **two** real offices exist and supplied both addresses — Patna, Bihar (*203, Sharda Mansion Apartment, Kailashpuri, Kankarbagh, Hanuman Nagar, Patna, Bihar 800020*) and Noida, Uttar Pradesh (*#UG-5, Provica Homes, Tower-02, Sarfabad, Noida-73, Gautam Buddha Nagar, Uttar Pradesh 201301*). Rather than overload the single-string `legal_registered_office_address` setting (a distinct fact — the one legal/registered-office address for the Terms & Privacy jurisdiction clause, not necessarily either of these two operational addresses), each real address lives on its own `cities.office_address` row (nullable, empty for every city except these two). A page renders `LocalBusiness`/`PostalAddress` JSON-LD only when its own row's `office_address` is non-empty — the sole gate, enforced structurally rather than by content-review convention. Bihar/Patna and Uttar Pradesh/Noida became Phase 1's two fully-authored, indexable pilot pairs specifically because they're the two genuine offices, not an arbitrary pick.

**Schema** (`database/schema-location-seo.sql`, `database/seed-location-seo.sql`, both new, additive): `states` (mirrors `countries`' shape — `name, slug UNIQUE, type ENUM('state','union_territory'), zone ENUM(...6 Indian zones...), intro_content, meta_title, meta_description, is_indexable, is_active, sort_order`) and `cities` (same shape plus `state_id FK`, `is_major`, `office_address`) and `location_faqs` (`visa_faqs`' exact nullable dual-scope shape, `state_id`/`city_id` instead of `country_id`/`visa_type_id`). `is_indexable` defaults to `0` on every row and is never set anywhere except by a human in the admin CMS — the explicit quality gate the spec's own §16/§28 demand, mirroring how `visa_requirements` already ships empty-until-verified per country. Seeded: all 28 states + 8 UTs as real geographic fact (name/slug/type/zone — verifiable, not authored content) with `is_indexable = 0`; Bihar and Uttar Pradesh flipped to `is_indexable = 1` with real hand-written `intro_content`/meta fields; Patna and Noida seeded as the two pilot `cities` rows (`is_indexable = 1`, real content, real `office_address`); 4 more real Bihar city names (Gaya, Muzaffarpur, Bhagalpur, Darbhanga) and 5 more real UP city names (Lucknow, Kanpur, Varanasi, Agra, Ghaziabad) seeded as `is_indexable = 0` stubs, listed as plain (unlinked) text on their state pages' "Cities We Serve" section rather than fabricated content or dead links; 6 `location_faqs` rows, genuinely different questions per state/city, not the same FAQ with a name swapped.

**Admin CMS** (`admin/pages/locations.php`, new, cloned from `admin/pages/countries.php`'s list/create/edit/toggle/delete shape, `require_permission('content.manage')`, no new permission): one file, tabbed by `?type=state|city`. Editable: `intro_content`, `office_address` (cities only, with an inline warning that filling it in adds `LocalBusiness`/"visit us" language to the live page), `meta_title`/`meta_description`, `is_indexable` (with an inline warning that it should only be flipped once real content exists), `is_active`, `sort_order`. Wired into `admin/index.php` (`case 'locations':`) and `includes/admin-layout.php`'s existing "Content" nav group, alongside Countries/Visa Types/FAQs/Embassies.

**Public router + pages**: `public/index.php` gained one `case 'visa-consultant':` line mirroring the existing `case 'visa':` line exactly. `visa-consultant/index.php` (new) mirrors `visa/index.php`'s `$segments`-based dispatch (`$segments[1]` = state slug, `$segments[2]` = city slug) to `hub.php` (all 36 states/UTs grouped by zone, every state linked regardless of `is_indexable` so nothing is ever an orphan page), `state.php` (renders for any active state, including drafts — sets `$noindex = true` when `is_indexable` is false rather than 404ing, so an admin can preview a page before flipping it live; always emits `Service` schema, never `LocalBusiness` — a state isn't a single geocoded location, so a genuine address belongs on the specific city page instead), and `city.php` (same is_indexable-gated rendering; emits `LocalBusiness`/`PostalAddress` JSON-LD only when `office_address` is set, `Service` schema otherwise; states honestly that the city is served remotely when there's no local office). `includes/data.php` gained `states_all()`, `state_by_slug()`, `cities_by_state()`, `city_by_slug()`, `location_faqs_for()` — same static-cache-per-request shape as the existing country lookups.

**Sitemap + nav**: `pages/sitemap-xml.php` adds state/city URLs filtered to `is_indexable = 1` only, matching the file's own pre-existing philosophy — verified only 5 URLs (hub + 2 states + 2 cities) appear, none of the other 34 states or 9 draft cities. `pages/sitemap.php` (human sitemap) gained a "Visa Consultant by Location" section, same filtering. `includes/footer.php` gained a compact 3-link "Popular Locations" footer group (hub + the two real pilots) — deliberately small, not a mass link dump, per the spec's own warning against exactly that.

**Two real bugs caught and fixed during verification, not shipped**: (1) `admin/pages/locations.php` line 18/28 computed `$type`/`$postType` via `in_array($_GET['type'] ??
