'state', ...) ? $_GET['type'] : 'state'` — the ternary's true branch re-read `$_GET['type']`/`$_POST['type']` directly instead of the already-coalesced value, firing an "Undefined array key" warning on any request without `type` in that specific superglobal (e.g. every POST, since the form's `action` has no query string). Fixed by coalescing once into a local variable and branching on that. (2) `bin/package-cpanel.sh`'s printed post-deploy database-import instructions were still stale from the original 4-pair list (schema/seed, crm, hrms, forex) — already caught and fixed once this session after the live production directory-listing incident (see below) — now extended again with the new `schema-location-seo.sql`/`seed-location-seo.sql` pair so the printed instructions stay accurate for future re-deploys.

**A live production incident, unrelated to this feature but handled in this same session**: the user reported `visagiri.com` serving a raw Apache directory listing of the entire application root (`admin/`, `config/`, `database/`, `auth/`, etc.) instead of the site — the live document root was pointed at the raw repo checkout instead of `public/`, with no front controller at that path and directory indexing left on. Not fixable from this sandbox (no hosting/cPanel access) — flagged to the user with the root cause and the two correct fixes (point the docroot at `public/`, or deploy the flattened `bin/package-cpanel.sh` package instead of the raw repo), and `public/.htaccess` got `Options -Indexes` added as defense-in-depth regardless of which fix they apply. Once the docroot was corrected, a second live incident (PDO access-denied, then a literal `<placeholder>` string including the angle brackets pasted into `config/database.php`'s `username` value) was talked through and resolved directly with the user — both real production credential/config edits happened on their end, not through any tool call in this session.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright/curl against the real local dev DB, never just code review**: all 8 route combinations (hub, 2 indexable states, 2 indexable cities, 1 draft state 200s-but-noindexed, 2 bad-slug 404s) return the correct status; Patna and Noida confirmed `index, follow` with real `LocalBusiness`/`PostalAddress` JSON-LD containing the exact real addresses; a draft state (Goa) confirmed `noindex, nofollow`; FAQ scoping confirmed correct and distinct between a state page (2 state-scoped FAQs) and its city page (2 different city-scoped FAQs) from the same 6-row seed; sitemap.xml confirmed to contain exactly the 5 expected indexable URLs and no others, and confirmed still-valid XML (247 total URLs); admin CMS create → edit (toggle `is_indexable`, confirmed via direct DB query since a test-script regex window was too tight to catch it in rendered HTML) → delete round-trip against a disposable test city, all confirmed via direct DB queries after each step; 403 confirmed for a non-`content.manage` admin (`hr.admin`, temporary password swap reverted immediately after, hash confirmed restored byte-for-byte); footer "Popular Locations" group confirmed rendering correctly on `/privacy/` (not just the homepage). `php -l` clean on all 13 new/changed PHP files. All test data (the disposable city, its audit-log rows, `rate_limits`) removed and confirmed empty via direct query before this entry was written.

**Not done this pass, by deliberate decision, not oversight**: only 2 of 36 states/UTs and 2 of 11 seeded cities are indexable — the other 34 states and 9 cities exist as real geographic rows (safe to seed) but stay `noindex`'d until real, unique content is authored for each, which is future work, not automatable without producing exactly the thin/templated pages the spec forbids. Country×city combination pages, the SEO dashboard, the keyword-to-URL mapping document, and GA4/GTM analytics wiring (no analytics of any kind exists anywhere in this codebase) were explicitly out of scope for this phase and were not started.

## National Location SEO — Phase 2: 8 more states + primary cities (Delhi, Maharashtra/Mumbai, Karnataka/Bengaluru, West Bengal/Kolkata, Telangana/Hyderabad, Tamil Nadu/Chennai, Gujarat/Ahmedabad, Rajasthan/Jaipur)

Direct continuation of Phase 1 using the exact same machinery — no PHP code changed this phase at all, only `database/seed-location-seo-phase2.sql` (new, additive). Asked the user what Phase 2 should cover (more states with real content vs. country×city pages vs. an SEO dashboard); they chose more states, following the established pattern.

**Content, not code**: each of the 8 states got genuinely distinct, hand-written `intro_content` — Delhi framed around most embassies/consulates being based there, Mumbai around finance/trading, Bengaluru around IT/tech and onsite-assignment work visas, Kolkata around historic trade/education links, Hyderabad around IT/pharma, Chennai around IT/manufacturing/healthcare, Ahmedabad around its trading community and diaspora ties, Jaipur around tourism — real, generic, uncontroversial facts about each region's economic character, never a fabricated statistic and never the same paragraph with the city name swapped. One primary, fully-authored, indexable city page per state (skipping Delhi, since as a UT it already functions as a single city-state and a separate "New Delhi" page would just duplicate the state page — a real duplicate-content risk avoided rather than built and cleaned up later); 2-3 more real city names per state seeded as non-indexable stub rows (Pune/Nagpur/Nashik, Mysuru/Mangaluru, Siliguri/Durgapur, Warangal, Coimbatore/Madurai, Surat/Vadodara, Jodhpur/Udaipur) for the "Cities We Serve" list, exactly matching Phase 1's Bihar/Uttar Pradesh pattern. 9 new `location_faqs` rows, each a genuinely different question tied to that state or city's own framing (embassy-appointment prep for Delhi, business visas for Mumbai, IT work visas for Bengaluru, etc.), not a templated FAQ repeated with the name changed.

**None of these 7 new cities has a real Visagiri office** — `office_address` stays `NULL` on every one of them, so every new city page emits `Service` schema and states plainly that Visagiri's offices are in Patna and Noida and that the city is served remotely. Only Patna and Noida (Phase 1) carry `LocalBusiness`/`PostalAddress` schema, and that remains true after this phase — verified directly by grepping the rendered HTML of all 8 new pages for `LocalBusiness` and confirming zero matches.

**Verified end-to-end**: all 8 new state pages return 200 with `index, follow` (confirmed via curl) and zero `LocalBusiness` schema; FAQ scoping re-confirmed correct and distinct on a new pair (Delhi state: 2 state-scoped FAQs; Mumbai city: 1 city-scoped FAQ; Maharashtra state itself has 0 FAQs, correctly, since none were seeded at that level); sitemap.xml grew from 5 to the correct 20 location URLs (1 hub + 10 indexable states + 9 indexable cities) and remains valid XML (262 total URLs sitewide); the human sitemap page's dynamic "Visa Consultant by Location" section picked up all 10 states/9 cities automatically with no code change, confirming the Phase 1 machinery is genuinely reusable rather than needing per-batch wiring. `php -l` was not needed this phase (no PHP files touched) but re-run anyway on every location-related file as a regression check — clean.

**Not done this pass**: 26 states/UTs and 2 seeded cities (Delhi's own stub-tier neighbors aside) remain `noindex`'d drafts, same reasoning as Phase 1 — real content takes real authoring time, not a bulk operation. Country×city pages, the SEO dashboard, and analytics wiring remain out of scope, per the user's own Phase 2 scope choice.

## National Location SEO — Phase 4: 9 more states (Chandigarh, Himachal Pradesh/Shimla, Jammu and Kashmir/Srinagar, Uttarakhand/Dehradun, Puducherry, Jharkhand/Ranchi, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, Chhattisgarh/Raipur, Tripura) — 27 of 36 now live

User said "Start phase 4" — same continuation, no new questions needed. Zero PHP changed again; only `database/seed-location-seo-phase4.sql` (new, additive). This batch worked through most of the remaining North-zone states/UTs plus a mix from South/East/West/Central, leaving mainly the smallest-population Northeast states and a few island/hill UTs (Ladakh, Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep, Arunachal Pradesh, Manipur, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Sikkim) for a future phase — deliberately not forced into this batch, since writing genuinely differentiated, non-thin content for very small population entities takes more care, not less, and rushing it risks producing exactly the kind of thin/templated pages this whole build has been designed to avoid.

**Real differentiating angle per state, same discipline as every prior phase**: Chandigarh framed around sharing Punjab's well-documented emigration culture (a genuinely accurate, well-known fact, not an invented one); Himachal Pradesh around tourism plus growing UK/Canada/Australia student-visa interest; Jammu and Kashmir kept strictly neutral and apolitical, purely about visa-consultancy service availability, no territorial commentary; Uttarakhand around Dehradun's real, well-known education-institution density; Puducherry around its genuine, uncontroversial French colonial history (safe angle for France/Schengen-visa framing); Jharkhand and Chhattisgarh both around their real steel/mining/industrial economies (Jamshedpur/Ranchi and Bhilai/Raipur are genuinely well-documented industrial hubs, not fabricated); Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu around its real manufacturing base and Daman/Diu's genuine Portuguese colonial history; Tripura kept modest and honest, no invented specifics.

**City-page decision this phase, made explicitly rather than defaulted**: only the 5 states with a real, separately-known capital/hub city distinct from the state name (Himachal Pradesh→Shimla, J&K→Srinagar, Uttarakhand→Dehradun, Jharkhand→Ranchi, Chhattisgarh→Raipur) got a dedicated city page. Chandigarh and Puducherry are single-city UTs already fully represented by their own state-level page (same reasoning as Delhi in Phase 2); Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu likewise has no second town with enough distinct material; Tripura has no clearly documented second city strong enough to avoid essentially repeating the state page. Skipping these rather than padding them out avoids fabricated specificity and near-duplicate content.

**No new offices**: `office_address` stays `NULL` on all 5 new city rows — confirmed by checking every one of the 9 new page renders for `LocalBusiness` and finding zero matches, including the long UT slug `dadra-and-nagar-haveli-and-daman-and-diu` (verified it doesn't break routing or the sitemap).

**Verified end-to-end**: all 9 new pages return 200 with `index, follow` and zero `LocalBusiness` schema; sitemap.xml grew from 36 to the correct 50 location URLs (1 hub + 27 indexable states + 22 indexable cities) and remains valid XML (292 total URLs sitewide); FAQ scoping re-confirmed correct on two new state-level pairs (Chandigarh, Puducherry), each showing exactly its own distinct FAQ. `php -l` re-run on every location-related PHP file (none touched) — clean. Local dev DB now has 27 of 36 states/UTs indexable, 22 cities.

**Not done this pass**: the 9 remaining states/UTs (mostly the smallest-population Northeast states plus Ladakh, Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep) stay `noindex`'d drafts, deliberately deferred rather than rushed. Country×city pages, the SEO dashboard, and analytics wiring remain untouched.

## National Location SEO — Phase 5 (final batch): Ladakh, Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep, Arunachal Pradesh/Itanagar, Manipur/Imphal, Meghalaya/Shillong, Mizoram/Aizawl, Nagaland/Kohima, Sikkim/Gangtok — **all 36 states/UTs now indexable**

Completes the state/UT layer of the location-SEO build started in Phase 1. Deliberately saved for last and handled separately from Phases 2-4: these 9 are the smallest-population entities in the seed (Lakshadweep alone is under 65,000 people), so the risk of drifting into thin or templated content was highest here, not lowest. Each state's `intro_content` is anchored to one specific, well-documented, uncontroversial real fact — never a restated "growing demand" claim used interchangeably across all nine, and never a business-volume claim this build has no basis to make. Ladakh (Himalayan adventure tourism and Buddhist monastic heritage), Andaman and Nicobar Islands (island tourism, the historic Cellular Jail), Lakshadweep (India's smallest UT — kept the most modest of all nine, explicitly framing service as fully remote given the population and distance from the mainland rather than implying local demand), Arunachal Pradesh (largest Northeastern state by area), Manipur (nationally known for producing many of India's top international athletes — real, well-documented, not invented), Meghalaya (its high-rainfall geography and distinct matrilineal culture), Mizoram (one of India's highest state literacy rates), Nagaland (its distinct tribal culture and the real, well-known annual Hornbill Festival), Sikkim (India's first fully organic-farming state, achieved in 2016 — genuinely verifiable, not a guess).

**City-page decision, same explicit reasoning as Phase 4**: the 3 union territories in this batch (Ladakh, Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep) get no separate city page — each is a single administrative hub already fully represented by its own page, and a "Leh" or "Port Blair" page today would just restate the UT page's own content. The 6 full states each get one real primary city (their capital: Itanagar, Imphal, Shillong, Aizawl, Kohima, Gangtok), matching the standard pattern used for every other state in this build.

**No new offices, same as every prior phase**: `office_address` stays `NULL` on all 6 new city rows, verified by checking every one of the 9 new page renders for `LocalBusiness` and finding zero matches.

**Verified end-to-end**: all 9 new pages return 200 with `index, follow` and zero `LocalBusiness` schema; the hub page's state list confirmed to contain exactly 36 unique state links (was previously incomplete); sitemap.xml grew from 50 to the correct, final 65 location URLs (1 hub + 36 indexable states + 28 indexable cities) and remains valid XML (307 total URLs sitewide); FAQ scoping re-confirmed correct on the two most content-sensitive pages in this batch (Lakshadweep's own remote-service FAQ, Imphal's sports-visa FAQ), each showing exactly its own distinct question. `php -l` re-run on every location-related PHP file (none touched this phase either) — clean.

**State of the location-SEO build after 5 phases**: all 36 states/UTs are indexable with genuinely distinct, hand-written content; 28 cities are indexable (2 with real `LocalBusiness`/`PostalAddress` schema — Patna and Noida, the two confirmed real offices — and 26 with honest "served remotely" `Service` schema); 38 location-specific FAQs, none of them a templated repeat. Zero PHP code was added in Phases 2-5 — the entire expansion from 2 to 36 states was data authored against the machinery Phase 1 built once. Country×city combination pages, the SEO dashboard, the keyword-to-URL mapping document, and GA4/GTM analytics wiring remain the explicitly out-of-scope items from the original 42-section spec that were never started in this build.

## Utility bar redesign #2: removed the USD→INR rate widget entirely

The header utility bar's rate widget (built earlier this session, then restored with WhatsApp support alongside it) is gone at the user's explicit request, confirmed via two clarifying questions ("visual style only" vs "different content/features" vs "mobile only", then which specific content change) — the answer was a straight removal, not a restyle.

Removed the whole feature, not just its visible markup, since none of the supporting machinery had any other consumer once the widget was gone:
- `includes/header.php`: the desktop `.site-header__rate-widget` dropdown block and its `$usdInrRate = get_usd_inr_rate();` computation, plus a second copy in the mobile hamburger menu's `.site-header__mobile-rate` block that a first grep pass missed and a follow-up sweep caught.
- `public/assets/js/site.js`: the `rate-menu-trigger` dropdown init and the whole 5-minute polling `setInterval` that kept the widget's numbers fresh — dead code the moment its DOM targets stopped existing, removed rather than left inert.
- `public/index.php`: the `case 'api-usd-inr-rate':` route and its `require_once` of `includes/currency-rate.php` — the AJAX endpoint existed solely to feed that polling interval.
- Deleted outright (confirmed via grep that nothing else in the codebase called `get_usd_inr_rate()` or referenced the route): `pages/api-usd-inr-rate.php`, `includes/currency-rate.php`.
- `includes/mail.php`: two docblock comments cited `currency-rate.php` as a design-pattern precedent; edited to drop the now-dead file reference rather than leave a comment pointing at nothing.
- `public/assets/css/layout.css`: removed every `.site-header__rate*` and `.site-header__mobile-rate*` rule; changed `.site-header__utility-bar`'s `justify-content` from `space-between` to `flex-end` now that WhatsApp+Login is the bar's only remaining content (space-between with one child would have left it awkwardly pinned to the left edge); fixed a stale mobile-layout comment that still described "rate + WhatsApp support, one line."

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright**: desktop utility bar renders right-aligned with just WhatsApp + Login, confirmed via screenshot; Login dropdown still opens and lists all three options correctly; mobile utility bar shows only the WhatsApp icon on one line; mobile hamburger menu confirmed to go straight from the tagline to the 24×7 Support button with no leftover rate box. `php -l` clean on every touched file. A full-codebase grep for `site-header__rate`, `usdInrRate`, `get_usd_inr_rate`, `api-usd-inr-rate`, and `currency-rate` came back completely empty after the fix — zero stray references anywhere.

## Utility bar: added a left-side "technology-driven company" tagline

The left side of the utility bar sat empty after the rate-widget removal above. Asked the user what to put there via two rounds of options (a functional inline tracker vs. a stated tech/security fact vs. a row of quick links); they picked a real security fact stated plainly.

Checked `includes/encryption.php` before writing any copy: it genuinely uses `openssl_encrypt(..., 'aes-256-gcm', ...)` on sensitive fields (passport numbers on `customers`/`visa_applicants`, per its own docblock). Chose the wording carefully — "AES-256 Encrypted Data Protection" rather than "Document Handling" or "Document Encryption" — because the encryption is applied to specific sensitive database fields, not to uploaded document files themselves, and this project's standing rule is to state only what's literally true.

`includes/header.php`: added a `<span class="site-header__utility-tagline">` (small lock icon + the text above) as the first child of `.site-header__utility-bar`, before the existing WhatsApp/Login group.
`public/assets/css/layout.css`: reverted `.site-header__utility-bar`'s `justify-content` from `flex-end` back to `space-between` (now that there are two groups again), added `.site-header__utility-tagline` styling matching the existing utility-link visual weight, and hid it below 900px (a new breakpoint, tighter than the existing 768px one) so it never crowds the WhatsApp+Login group on tablet widths — verified all three states (desktop showing both sides, 850px hiding the tagline only, 390px mobile) via Playwright screenshots.

## Header: replaced the search icon with "Track Application", made application tracking 3-factor

The main nav's search icon (linked to `/countries/`, between the "Company" mega-menu and "Enquire Now") is replaced with a "Track Application" text link to the existing `/track-visa/` page, per the user's direct request. Reused `.site-header__text-link` — a CSS class that already existed in `layout.css` but had no consumer anywhere in `header.php`, apparently left over from an earlier design iteration — rather than inventing a new one. Removed `.site-header__icon-btn` entirely once confirmed (via grep) that nothing else referenced it.

The user also specified exactly what the tracker should require: Application Number, Mobile No/Email ID, and Passport Number — three factors, not the two (`reference` + `mobile`) `pages/track-visa.php` previously checked. Implemented as a single query requiring all three to match simultaneously, same non-enumerating pattern as the original (`/forex/track/` and the old two-factor version): any mismatch on any field shows the identical generic "couldn't find" message, never a field-specific hint.

**Passport verification never touches the encrypted column directly.** `customers.passport_number_encrypted` is AES-256-GCM (non-deterministic — encrypting the same plaintext twice produces different ciphertext), so it can't be used in a `WHERE` clause. Used the existing `customers.passport_number_hash` (a keyed HMAC via `searchable_hash()`, already used the same way by every admin passport-search feature — `admin/pages/customers.php`, `admin/pages/visa-applications.php`, `admin/pages/forex-requests.php`) instead — confirmed this was the established pattern before writing a single line, rather than inventing a new comparison method.

**A real bug caught and fixed during verification**: the first version bound `:contact` twice in one query (`c.mobile = :contact OR c.email = :contact`) so the mobile-or-email field could match either column. PDO's non-emulated prepared statements (this project's standing configuration) don't support reusing one named placeholder twice — this threw `PDOException: SQLSTATE[HY093] Invalid parameter number` as soon as the form was submitted with real data, only caught because the verification step submitted an actual form rather than stopping at `php -l`. Fixed by using two distinct placeholders (`:contact1`, `:contact2`) both bound to the same value.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright/curl against a real test customer+application** (created via the app's own `encrypt_value()`/`searchable_hash()`/`generate_reference_number()` functions, not raw guessed SQL): header shows "Track Application" and no longer has the old search-icon link, confirmed via screenshot; all-three-correct succeeds via mobile lookup; all-three-correct succeeds via email lookup instead of mobile (confirming the OR condition actually works after the placeholder fix); wrong passport, wrong contact, and wrong reference each independently produce the identical generic not-found message with zero application-status leakage — 7/7 checks. `php -l` clean. Test customer and application rows removed and confirmed deleted via direct query before this entry was written.

## Resources Hub Phase 1: "Visa Updates" tab replaced with a real "Resources" mega-menu + `/resources/` hub

The client sent a 36-section spec asking to re-architect the header's "Visa Updates" tab into a premium "Resources" mega-menu with 6 columns, a full `/resources/` hub with search/filters, a "Last Verified" system, a Resource CMS, and homepage/footer integration. Audited first, per the spec's own requirement: a full `CREATE TABLE` grep across all 29 `database/*.sql` files for `blog|article|post|guide|resource|content` returned nothing — there is no backing data model anywhere for blog/article/guide/resource content, and no "Tools" (calculator/checker) infrastructure exists at all. Building the spec's search/filter engine, Resource CMS, or per-category content against zero real data would mean fabricating exactly the doorway/thin content this project has refused to ship since Phase 15. Scoped Phase 1 to what's genuinely real: a nav restructure plus a hub page that link only to pages that already exist, with the harder pieces named as explicitly deferred rather than silently dropped or faked.

**Header (`includes/header.php`, `public/assets/js/site.js`)**: removed "Visa Updates" (which pointed at the honestly-empty `/blog/`) from `$navLinks`, and added a "Resources" mega-menu in its place — structurally cloned from the existing Attestation mega-menu (`.mega-menu__columns` of `.mega-menu__col` link lists + a bottom `.mega-menu__cta` bar), reusing the `primary_nav_icon('resources')` icon that already existed but, until now, was only ever used in the footer's Resources heading. Four columns, every link pointing at a page that is genuinely live today: Guides & Documents (Documentation Assistance, Document Templates, Document Checklists), Visa Information (Visa Types, Countries We Serve, Embassy Directory), Tools (Track Your Application, Check Visa Status, Visa Tools), Updates & Help (Visa Updates & News, FAQs, Submit an Enquiry). The two still-stub destinations (`/document-checklists/`, the new `/resources/tools/`) are labeled "(Coming Soon)" in the menu itself rather than hidden or presented as finished. Added the matching mobile `<details>` accordion (same subgroup pattern as Attestation's mobile block) and one new `initMegaMenu('resources-mega-trigger', 'resources-mega-menu')` call in `site.js` — zero new JS logic needed, the existing factory handles it.

**New `/resources/` hub (`pages/resources.php`)**: real page, indexed, `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD (same pattern as `pages/faq.php`/`pages/documentation.php`), four category cards (`.card.service-card`, the same class `pages/home.php`'s "Visa Services" section already uses) mirroring the mega-menu's four columns, each linking to the same real destinations. **New `/resources/tools/`** (`pages/resources-tools.php`): an honest "Coming Soon" page — states plainly that interactive tools (fee calculators, eligibility checkers) don't exist yet, links to the two real tools that do (`/track-visa/`, `/visa-status/`), `$noindex = true` since there's nothing here yet worth ranking. Both routed via one new `case 'resources':` block in `public/index.php` (segment-dispatches to the tools sub-page).

**Two real pre-existing bugs fixed along the way**: `pages/sitemap-xml.php`'s `$staticUrls` was missing `/documentation/` and `/document-templates/` — both real, indexed pages that had simply never been added; now included, alongside the new `/resources/`. `includes/footer.php`'s Resources column had "Travel Guides", "Visa Updates", and "Blog" as three separate list items all pointing at the identical `/blog/` href — deduped to one real "Visa Updates" link, and the column rebuilt to mirror the mega-menu's real link set (Resource Hub, Documentation Assistance, Document Templates, Visa Information, Visa FAQs, Track Application, Visa Updates, Submit a Visa Enquiry). "Become a B2B Partner" (`/partner-program/`) didn't fit any Resources category, so it moved to the Company footer column instead of staying miscategorized. Added `/resources/` to the human sitemap's existing "Resources" section too.

**Homepage** (`pages/home.php`): added a new "Explore Visa Resources" section (same `.card-grid` pattern as the rest of the homepage) between the existing "Latest Visa Updates" section and the FAQ section, linking to five of the real Resources pages plus an "Explore All Resources" CTA. The existing "Latest Visa Updates" section (always-empty by deliberate prior design, per its own docblock) was left completely untouched.

**Explicitly deferred, not silently dropped** (documented in the plan the user approved before this build): blog/article schema + CMS + authored articles (no `blog_posts` table exists — authoring real articles is a content project, not a code change); real interactive tools; the resource search/filter engine and Country/Visa-Type/Topic filters (no Resource entity exists to filter over); per-category `/resources/{category}/` index routes (deferred specifically to avoid the thin/duplicate-URL trap the client's own spec forbids — the single hub with real anchor sections covers this honestly); the "Last Verified"/"Source" metadata system and the "Important" info-box component (belong to a future resource-page template once real per-resource content exists); a Resource CMS admin entity (nothing new to manage yet — every linked page already has its own CMS or is static).

**Verified end-to-end**: `php -l` clean on all 8 touched/new files. Playwright: desktop hover on the Resources trigger opens the mega-menu and all 12 real links plus the CTA resolve to the correct URLs (confirmed via `$$eval` over the rendered `<a>` hrefs); mobile hamburger menu opens the Resources accordion showing the same four grouped subheadings, matching the Attestation/Countries accordions' existing visual pattern exactly; `/resources/` returns 200 with `index, follow` and renders all four category cards; `/resources/tools/` returns 200 with `noindex, nofollow`; `sitemap.xml` now contains `/documentation/`, `/document-templates/`, and `/resources/`; homepage confirmed to have exactly one `/blog/` link inside the footer's Resources column (previously three) plus the expected one each in the desktop/mobile mega-menu copies.

## B2B Travel Partner Portal — Phase B5: admin verification center

The client's B2B Travel Partner Portal spec called for a full admin-side application review + KYC verification workflow. Phases B1-B3 (schema, public landing page, the 6-step signup wizard with dual-channel OTP, a minimal dashboard shell) shipped earlier as a deliberately standalone system alongside the existing referral-partner CRM (`partners`/`admin/pages/partners.php`), sharing only low-level infrastructure. This phase gives admins the missing other half: a way to actually see and act on the applications that wizard produces.

**`admin/pages/b2b-partners.php`** (new, routed at `/admin/b2b-partners/`, gated on `b2b_travel_partners.view`/`.manage`): rebuilt on `admin/pages/partners.php`'s proven list+detail+status-transition pattern rather than inventing a new one. List view: search (business name/email/reference/phone/city) + status filter + pagination, submitted/under-review applications sorted first. Detail view: full business/legal info (GSTIN, PAN, IATA, TIDS, Udyam), all contact users and their verification state, selected services/countries, KYC document table with per-document Verify/Reject + remarks, billing/banking details (decrypted via `decrypt_value()`, gated separately behind `b2b_travel_partners.billing.view` — seeded to `super-admin` only, not `admin`, same restriction as the schema always intended), and full status history.

**Status workflow**, built on the existing `b2b_change_status()` helper (writes `b2b_partners.status` + a `b2b_status_history` row together): `start_review` (submitted → under_review), `documents_required`, `reject`, `suspend`, `blacklist`, `close` all require a non-empty reason — mirroring `partners.php`'s `$reasonRequiredActions` gate exactly, since these are consequential, partner-visible decisions, not one-click toggles. `reactivate` stays reason-optional (existing convention). Rejecting also writes `b2b_partners.rejection_reason`, which `b2b_change_status()` deliberately does not touch on its own.

**A real server-side business rule on Approve, not just a hidden button** — the same principle the client's spec insisted on for the original partner system ("never trust a hidden button as the real security/business-rule boundary") applies here too, adapted to what's actually true of this schema: unlike the old system (where email verification happens *after* signup and gates approval), B2B signup verifies both email and mobile via OTP *before* the account row even exists, so that check is structurally already guaranteed and would be a no-op gate here. The real open question at approval time is KYC completeness, so Approve is blocked server-side — re-checked on every POST, not just disabled in the UI — unless at least one document has been uploaded and every uploaded document's `verification_status` is `verified` (none still `uploaded`/`under_review`/`rejected`). Approving sets `approved_at`, clears any prior `rejection_reason`, transitions to `active`, and emails the partner's `partner_admin` contact.

**`admin/pages/b2b-partner-document-download.php`** (new): authenticated download of a `b2b_partner_documents` row, mirroring `partner-document-download.php`'s pattern against the separate B2B table/storage path.

**Wiring**: `case 'b2b-partners':` / `case 'b2b-partner-document-download':` added to `admin/index.php`; a `B2B Travel Partners` nav link with a pending-count badge added to `includes/admin-layout.php`, gated on `b2b_travel_partners.view`. The dashboard's existing "B2B Partners" tile (added during the earlier single-window dashboard redesign) was relabeled **"Referral Partners"** — it always pointed at the old commission/referral system (`/admin/partners/`) and the shared name was misleading now that a second, unrelated B2B system exists; a new **"B2B Travel Partner Portal"** tile was added alongside it pointing at `/admin/b2b-partners/`, carrying its own pending-applications badge.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB: signed up a fresh test partner (Skyline Voyages) through the full public wizard (business info → OTP-verified contact → KYC upload → services/countries → billing skip → declaration → submit), confirmed the auto `submitted → under_review` transition fired on submission (already wired in Phase B3's declaration step), logged in as admin using a throwaway bcrypt hash set only on the local DB, confirmed both new dashboard tiles render, opened the partner's detail page, verified the uploaded KYC document, clicked Approve & Activate, and confirmed the partner's own dashboard immediately reflected "Your partner account is active." Direct DB check confirmed `b2b_status_history` recorded both transitions (`submitted→under_review`, `under_review→active`) and `b2b_partners.approved_at` was set. The real admin password hash was restored and all test partner/user/document/OTP/status-history rows and `rate_limits` entries were deleted from the local dev DB afterward. `php -l` clean on all 5 touched/new files.

**Not done this pass, explicitly next**: Phase B4 (real KPI cards on the partner-side dashboard — meaningful once B6's visa enquiry entities exist to count), B6 (visa enquiry submission flow inside the partner dashboard), B7 (quotations/invoices/payments), B8 (messaging/notifications/support tickets), B9 (multi-user role boundaries, performance analytics, admin audit-log views) — all per the client's original phased B2B Travel Partner Portal spec, proceeding automatically per instruction.

## B2B Travel Partner Portal — Phases B4 + B6: visa enquiry flow + real dashboard KPI cards

Built together rather than in the originally-proposed B4-then-B6 order: B4's KPI cards ("Total Enquiries," "New," "In Progress," etc.) have nothing real to count until the enquiry entity from B6 exists, so building B4 first would have meant either faking numbers or shipping empty cards and revisiting the same file twice. Reordered, documented here rather than silently.

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-portal-b6.sql`, additive): `b2b_visa_enquiries` (reference number, `b2b_partner_id`, `created_by_user_id`, destination country FK, visa type, purpose/intended travel date, applicant count, `status` ENUM `new`/`in_progress`/`documents_pending`/`submitted`/`payment_pending`/`completed`/`rejected`/`cancelled`, `assigned_admin_id`); `b2b_enquiry_applicants` (one-to-many, passport number encrypted via the existing `encrypt_value()` helper — same treatment as PAN/banking data elsewhere); `b2b_enquiry_documents` (KYC-style upload table scoped to the enquiry rather than per-applicant — the same simplification `b2b_partner_documents`/`partner_documents` already make for onboarding KYC, so a multi-applicant case shares one document set in this phase); `b2b_enquiry_status_history` (mirrors `b2b_status_history`'s shape, but records either `changed_by_admin` or `changed_by_partner_user` since both sides can trigger a transition here). No new RBAC permissions — enquiry management reuses `b2b_travel_partners.view`/`.manage`, the same sub-resource relationship `partners.view` already has with `partner_enquiries`.

**`includes/b2b-enquiry.php`** (new): `B2B_ENQUIRY_STATUS_LABELS`, `B2B_ENQUIRY_DOCUMENT_TYPES` (7 real types + Other), `B2B_APPLICANT_RELATIONSHIPS`, `b2b_generate_enquiry_reference()` (`VG-B2BENQ-YYYY-NNNNNN`), `b2b_change_enquiry_status()`, `store_b2b_enquiry_document()` — same shapes as their `b2b_partner.php` counterparts, kept in a separate file since this is a distinct entity family.

**Partner-side flow**: `b2b/pages/enquiry-create.php` (`/b2b/enquiry-create/`) is one page, not a session-backed multi-step wizard like signup — Travel Details, Visa Requirements, and a dynamically-repeatable Applicants section (`public/assets/js/b2b-enquiry-applicants.js`: clones a `<template>` row, renumbers `name` attributes, hides Remove on the last row) all submit together in one transaction, since every field here is real required data for one atomic record rather than genuinely separate steps worth persisting individually. Gated server-side: a partner whose account isn't yet `active` is redirected back to the dashboard with an explanation, not shown the form. `b2b/pages/enquiries.php` (`/b2b/enquiries/`) is list+detail in one file (query-string `?action=view&id=`, the same pattern `partner/pages/applications.php` already established) scoped to the logged-in partner's own `b2b_partner_id`; the detail view supports document upload and a partner-initiated Cancel while the enquiry is still open.

**Admin-side flow**: `admin/pages/b2b-enquiries.php` (new, `/admin/b2b-enquiries/`), same list+detail+status-transition shape as `admin/pages/b2b-partners.php`: `start_review`, `documents_required` (reason required), `submit` (marks submitted to embassy), `payment_pending`, `complete`, `reject` (reason required), `cancel` (reason required), plus per-document Verify/Reject and relationship-manager assignment. `admin/pages/b2b-enquiry-document-download.php` mirrors the partner-document download pattern against the enquiry-scoped table. The partner's own enquiries (last 10, with status) are now listed directly on their `admin/pages/b2b-partners.php` detail page with a link into the full filtered list — the admin never has to leave a partner's profile to see what they've submitted.

**`b2b/pages/dashboard.php`** rebuilt from the Phase B1-B3 placeholder into the real KPI dashboard the spec described, but only once a partner is `active` (before that, there's nothing real to show): a `GROUP BY status` query over the partner's own enquiries drives 8 stat cards (Total, New, In Progress, Documents Pending, Submitted, Payment Pending, Completed, Rejected), plus "+ New Visa Enquiry" and "View All Enquiries" quick-action buttons. The existing KYC document counters stay as their own section below.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB, extending the same signup-to-approval flow proven in Phase B5: signed up a fresh partner (Northwind Travel Co), confirmed `/b2b/enquiry-create/` redirects back to the dashboard with an explanatory error while the partner is still `submitted` (the active-only gate firing correctly, not just disabled in the UI); had admin verify the KYC document and approve; confirmed the dashboard now shows the enquiry KPI section; created a real enquiry with two applicants (added the second via the dynamic "+ Add Applicant" button) and a purpose/travel date, landing on the detail page with both applicants listed; uploaded a passport-copy document from the partner side; as admin, walked the enquiry through `new → in_progress → submitted → completed`, verifying the uploaded document along the way; confirmed each transition rendered correctly and, via direct DB check, that `b2b_enquiry_status_history` recorded all four transitions with the right admin actor and that both applicants (including the encrypted passport number field) and the verified document persisted correctly. All test partner/enquiry/applicant/document/status-history rows and `rate_limits` entries were removed from the local dev DB afterward, and the real admin password hash was restored. `php -l` clean on all 10 touched/new PHP files; `node --check` clean on the new JS file.

**A test-script-only finding, not an application defect**: the verification script's first drafts re-submitted the admin and B2B partner login forms after already being authenticated in the same browser session — both login pages correctly redirect an already-authenticated user straight to their dashboard (the same guard `admin/pages/login.php` and `b2b/pages/login.php` already had), so the redundant re-login attempts timed out waiting for a login field that was never going to render. Fixed by relying on the existing session instead of re-authenticating — worth noting only because it confirms the admin and B2B portal sessions coexist in the same PHP session store without interfering with each other, which is the same shared-session design already documented for the admin/customer/partner login systems elsewhere in this codebase.

**Not done this pass, explicitly next**: B7 (quotations, invoices, payment tracking tied to these enquiries — `payment_pending` exists as a status today but nothing yet produces or tracks an actual amount), B8 (partner↔Visagiri messaging per enquiry, notification centre, support tickets), B9 (multi-user role boundaries for `partner_manager`/`partner_agent`/`partner_finance` within the dashboard — right now any logged-in partner user can create/cancel enquiries regardless of role, partner performance analytics, admin B2B audit-log views).

## B2B Travel Partner Portal — Phase B7: quotations, invoices, manual payment tracking

Attaches real financial documents to a B2B visa enquiry: a quotation the admin prices and sends, which the partner can accept or decline, and an invoice issued once a quotation is accepted, tracked to manual payment — no payment gateway integration exists or was requested anywhere in this project (the same documented decision behind `schema-crm.sql`'s `visa_payments`), so "payment received" is staff recording what actually came in, never a fake "Pay Now" button.

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-portal-b7.sql`, additive): `b2b_enquiry_quotations` (reference number, `b2b_visa_enquiry_id`, `government_fee`/`service_fee`/`other_charges`/`total_amount`, `status` ENUM `draft`/`sent`/`accepted`/`rejected`, `sent_at`/`responded_at`/`response_note`) — same three-line-item shape `visa_quotes` already uses elsewhere in the project. `b2b_enquiry_invoices` (reference number, enquiry + partner FKs, optional `quotation_id` link, `amount`, `status` ENUM `issued`/`paid`/`cancelled`, `payment_method`/`payment_reference` for the manual record, `issued_at`/`paid_at`).

**`includes/b2b-billing.php`** (new): status label constants and `b2b_generate_quotation_reference()`/`b2b_generate_invoice_reference()` (`VG-B2BQT-...`/`VG-B2BINV-...`) via the same shared reference-number generator every other entity in this project uses.

**Admin side** (`admin/pages/b2b-enquiries.php` extended): a quotation form (three fee fields + notes) creates a `draft`, only visible to admins until an explicit "Send to Partner" action moves it to `sent`. An invoice can be raised either from an accepted quotation (pre-fills the amount via a `<select>`) or as a manual amount; issuing one automatically transitions the enquiry to `payment_pending` via the existing `b2b_change_enquiry_status()` helper, so the KPI card built in Phase B4/B6 immediately reflects it. "Mark Paid" requires a payment method (free text — Bank Transfer, UPI, Cheque, etc.) and records an optional reference/UTR, matching the manual-collection pattern already established for visa payments elsewhere in the CRM.

**Partner side** (`b2b/pages/enquiries.php` extended): the quotations query explicitly excludes `draft` — a partner only ever sees a quotation once an admin has actually sent it, never an internal pricing draft. A `sent` quotation gets Accept/Decline buttons (state re-checked server-side against `status = 'sent'`, not just hidden once acted on). Invoices are listed with their status; an `issued` (unpaid) invoice shows an honest note that online payment isn't connected yet and Visagiri's finance team will reach out — the same "don't fake what isn't built" discipline used for the mobile OTP fallback and the Resources Hub's "Tools" page.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB, extending the same partner-signup-to-active flow: created and approved a fresh partner (Coral Bay Journeys), created a real enquiry, had admin start review then create a ₹7,500 quotation (₹5,000 govt. fee + ₹2,000 service fee + ₹500 other) and send it, confirmed the partner's enquiry page shows the total and Accept/Decline buttons, accepted it as the partner, confirmed the admin side then offers "Create Invoice from Quotation," issued the invoice (confirming the enquiry status flipped to Payment Pending), marked it paid with a bank-transfer reference, and confirmed both the admin and partner views show the invoice as Paid with the recorded method/reference. Direct DB check confirmed the quotation's `status = accepted`, the invoice's `status = paid` with the correct `payment_method`/`payment_reference`, and the enquiry's `status = payment_pending` (case completion is a separate, deliberate admin action from payment collection — not automatically inferred from a paid invoice). All test partner/enquiry/quotation/invoice rows and `rate_limits` entries were removed afterward and the real admin password hash restored. `php -l` clean on all 5 touched/new PHP files.

**Not done this pass, explicitly next**: B8 (partner↔Visagiri messaging per enquiry, notification centre, support tickets), B9 (multi-user role boundaries for `partner_manager`/`partner_agent`/`partner_finance` — right now any logged-in partner user can accept/decline quotations and create/cancel enquiries regardless of role; partner performance analytics; admin B2B audit-log views).

## B2B Travel Partner Portal — Phase B8: enquiry messaging, partner notification centre, support tickets

Gives the B2B portal the communication layer the earlier phases were missing — a message thread on each enquiry, a real notification centre (previously nothing surfaced the events Phases B5-B7 were already generating), and a support ticket system independent of any specific enquiry.

**A gap this phase had to fix first**: the B2B portal had no shared navigation shell. Phase B1-B3 only ever needed 2 real pages (a landing wizard and a bare dashboard), so each page just called `includes/header.php`/`footer.php` directly with a manual "back" link between pages. With 6+ real pages now (dashboard, enquiries, enquiry-create, and now notifications/support), that stops scaling — a notification centre nobody can navigate to is dead weight. Added `includes/b2b-partner-layout.php` (`render_b2b_partner_start()`/`render_b2b_partner_end()`), mirroring `includes/partner-layout.php`'s sidebar-nav pattern for the old referral-partner system exactly (same `.dashboard-shell`/`.sidebar-nav` CSS, already in `components.css`, nothing new to write), and retrofitted `dashboard.php`, `enquiries.php`, and `enquiry-create.php` onto it.

**Database** (`database/schema-b2b-portal-b8.sql`, additive): `b2b_enquiry_messages` (one thread per enquiry — `sender_admin_id`/`sender_partner_user_id`, exactly one set per row, same split `b2b_enquiry_status_history` already uses); `b2b_partner_notifications` (identical shape to the old system's `partner_notifications`); `b2b_support_tickets` + `b2b_support_ticket_messages` (a ticket is its own entity, not tied to an enquiry, since "I can't log in" or "how do I update billing" aren't enquiry-scoped questions). No new RBAC permissions — support tickets and enquiry messaging reuse the existing `b2b_travel_partners.view`/`.manage` keys, same sub-resource relationship the rest of this module already has.

**`includes/b2b-notify.php`** (new): `notify_b2b_partner()` (same shape as `notify_partner()`), `b2b_generate_ticket_reference()` (`VG-B2BTKT-...`), and the ticket category/priority/status label constants. Wired into every partner-facing event that previously only sent an email or updated silently: partner account status changes (`admin/pages/b2b-partners.php`), and — centralized inside `b2b_change_enquiry_status()` itself so every call site gets it automatically rather than depending on each admin action remembering to notify — enquiry status transitions, plus explicit calls for document verification, quotation-sent, invoice-paid, enquiry messages, and ticket replies.

**Enquiry messaging**: a thread section added to both `b2b/pages/enquiries.php`'s and `admin/pages/b2b-enquiries.php`'s detail views, writing to `b2b_enquiry_messages` and notifying the other side. Deliberately no read-receipt tracking — matches the old system's `visa_application_notes`-based messages, which also don't track per-message read state, just an unread notification count.

**`b2b/pages/notifications.php`** (new, `/b2b/notifications/`): opening the page marks everything read (same convention as the old partner notifications page) — no per-item mark-as-read UI needed since there's nothing to leave unread once you've opened the list.

**Support tickets**: `b2b/pages/support.php` (list + create + detail-with-thread) and `admin/pages/b2b-support-tickets.php` (list + detail + reply + status/assignment, mirroring the other B2B admin pages' pattern). A partner reply on a `resolved` ticket automatically reopens it to `open`; an admin reply on an `open` ticket automatically advances it to `in_progress` — status reflects who's waiting on whom without a separate manual step for the common case, while a closed ticket is display-only (the UI just says to open a new one).

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB: signed up and approved a fresh partner (Horizon Travel Solutions), confirmed the new sidebar nav renders on the dashboard with Notifications/Support links, confirmed the approval notification appears in `/b2b/notifications/`, created a real enquiry, posted a partner message and confirmed it's visible on the admin side, posted an admin reply and confirmed it's visible on the partner side plus generates a "New message on..." notification, created a support ticket with a real category/priority/initial message, confirmed it appears in the admin ticket list scoped to the right partner, replied as admin (confirming the ticket auto-advanced to In Progress), manually set it to Resolved, and confirmed the partner sees both the reply and the resolved status. Direct DB check confirmed message counts, notification counts, and the ticket's final `status = resolved`. All test partner/enquiry/message/notification/ticket rows and `rate_limits` entries were removed afterward and the real admin password hash restored. `php -l` clean on all 13 touched/new PHP files.

**Not done this pass, explicitly next**: B9 (multi-user role boundaries — right now any logged-in `b2b_partner_users` row, regardless of `role`, can create/cancel enquiries, accept/reject quotations, and post messages/tickets with no distinction between `partner_admin`/`partner_manager`/`partner_agent`/`partner_finance`; partner performance analytics; admin-side B2B audit-log views beyond the existing generic `audit_logs` table these actions already write to via `log_action()`).

## B2B Travel Partner Portal — Phase B9: multi-user role boundaries, performance analytics, admin audit-log discoverability

The final phase of the B2B Travel Partner Portal build. `current_b2b_partner_can_manage_team()` and `current_b2b_partner_can_view_billing()` were both written back in Phase B1 but never actually called anywhere — with only one user (the signup-time `partner_admin`) ever able to exist, role boundaries had nothing to bound. This phase makes the four `B2B_ROLES` (`partner_admin`/`partner_manager`/`partner_agent`/`partner_finance`) real by giving a partner org a way to have more than one user, and wiring the role checks that were already sitting there unused.

**Team invitations, reusing existing schema rather than adding new tables**: unlike the old referral-partner system (which invites into a separate `partner_team_members` table with its own status/token columns because a member there isn't a first-class login until accepted), `b2b_partner_users` already models every team member as a full row from day one — so `b2b_create_user_invite()` (`includes/b2b-partner-auth.php`) just inserts the row immediately with an unusable random `password_hash` and repurposes the already-present `reset_token_hash`/`reset_token_expires_at` columns as the accept-invite token (same random-32-byte/SHA-256-hashed/7-day-expiry shape as `create_partner_password_reset_token()`). The account is inert — can't log in — until the invitee visits `/b2b/team-accept/?token=...` and sets a real password, at which point `b2b_complete_invite()` clears the token and logs them straight in. No new database table, no new status enum value — a row with `reset_token_hash IS NOT NULL` displays as "Invited" in the UI even though its underlying `status` column already reads `active`.

**`b2b/pages/team.php`** (new, `/b2b/team/`): roster + invite form, gated by `current_b2b_partner_can_manage_team()` exactly like the old system's `current_partner_can_manage_team()` — only `partner_admin` can invite, suspend, or remove; every other role sees a read-only table. Self-suspend/self-remove are explicitly blocked server-side (an admin can't lock themselves out).

**Role boundaries wired into existing actions** (`current_b2b_partner_can_manage_enquiries()`: `partner_admin`/`partner_manager`/`partner_agent`; `current_b2b_partner_can_view_billing()`: `partner_admin`/`partner_finance`, unchanged from B1): enquiry creation and cancellation now require the former — checked server-side on `enquiry-create.php` and the `cancel` POST handler, not just hidden client-side, and the "+ New Enquiry" CTAs are hidden for roles that can't use them rather than left dangling. Quotation Accept/Decline now require the latter — a `partner_agent` can still see the price on a sent quotation (transparency within the org) but can't commit to it; the buttons are replaced with an explanatory note for everyone else.

**Performance analytics** (`b2b/pages/dashboard.php`): a completion rate (`completed / (total − cancelled)`, undefined and shown as `—` rather than `0%` when there's nothing decided yet) and an average-enquiries-per-month figure computed from the partner's real `MIN(created_at)` across their own enquiries — not an assumed reporting window. The section only renders once at least one enquiry exists, so a brand-new active partner doesn't see a hollow "0% completion" card. The dashboard's Partner ID line also now shows the logged-in user's own role, so a `partner_finance` user immediately understands why they don't see the enquiry-creation button.

**Admin-side audit-log discoverability**: `admin/pages/audit-log.php` already existed as a fully generic filterable viewer (`SELECT DISTINCT module FROM audit_logs`) — every `log_action()` call this entire B2B module has made since Phase B5 (`b2b_partners`, `b2b_partner_documents`, `b2b_visa_enquiries`, `b2b_enquiry_documents`, `b2b_enquiry_quotations`, `b2b_enquiry_invoices`, `b2b_support_tickets`) was already showing up there with zero code changes needed. This phase's actual work was adding a "View in Audit Log" link, pre-filtered by module, from both `admin/pages/b2b-partners.php`'s and `admin/pages/b2b-enquiries.php`'s detail views — closing the discoverability gap rather than the functionality gap, which didn't exist.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB: signed up and approved a fresh partner (Summit Voyages), confirmed the dashboard shows "Your Role: Partner Admin," invited a `partner_finance` team member from `/b2b/team/` (captured the dev-mode invite link shown on-screen since no SMTP is configured locally — the same honest fallback pattern used everywhere else in this project), accepted the invite in a separate browser context and confirmed the new user lands logged-in on the dashboard showing "Your Role: Partner Finance," confirmed the finance user does not see the "+ New Visa Enquiry" button and is server-side redirected with an explanatory error when hitting `/b2b/enquiry-create/` directly, confirmed the finance user can still see the team roster but not the invite form, had the partner_admin create and complete a real enquiry, confirmed the dashboard's Performance section then shows a 100% completion rate, and confirmed both `/admin/audit-log/?module=b2b_partners` and `?module=b2b_visa_enquiries` return real entries from this session's actions. Direct DB check confirmed the invited user's `reset_token_hash` cleared to `NULL` after acceptance and the audit log rows exist with the expected module/action values. All test partner/user/enquiry rows and `rate_limits` entries were removed afterward and the real admin password hash restored. `php -l` clean on all 10 touched/new PHP files.

**With Phase B9 complete, all nine phases (B1-B9) of the client's B2B Travel Partner Portal spec are now built**: standalone onboarding wizard with OTP verification (B1-B3), admin verification center (B5), real dashboard KPIs and the visa enquiry flow (B4/B6), quotations/invoices with manual payment tracking (B7), enquiry messaging/notification centre/support tickets (B8), and multi-user role boundaries/performance analytics/audit-log discoverability (B9) — running alongside, and deliberately not merged into, the pre-existing commission/referral partner system, per the explicit "Full rebuild as specified, accepting the duplication" decision made at the start of this build.

## Admin sidebar reduced to a single Dashboard link — the module dashboard is now the real command center

Per explicit client feedback: the sidebar still listed every module flatly (Sales CRM, Customers, Partners, Partner Tiers/Invoices/Document Expiry/Enquiries, Finance, B2B Travel Partners/Enquiries/Support Tickets, Enquiries, Visa Enquiries (Legacy), Visa Applications, General Enquiries, Grievances, plus collapsible Forex/Content/System groups) even after the single-window dashboard redesign gave every one of those modules its own tile — so the sidebar and the "command center" were duplicating the same navigation. Fixed by cutting the sidebar down to just the Dashboard link; the dashboard's tile grid is now the only place all modules are listed from.

**`includes/admin-layout.php`**: the sidebar `<nav>` now renders exactly one link. The removed group-expand logic (`$navGroups`/`$isGroupOpen`, the collapsible Forex/Content/System `<button>` sections) is gone with it — there's no sidebar tree left to expand.

**The real work: making sure nothing became unreachable.** A tile only gets you to one page per module (e.g. the B2B Partners tile only opens `/admin/b2b-partners/`) — without the sidebar, pages like Partner Tiers, Partner Invoices, B2B Visa Enquiries, B2B Support Tickets, Exchange Rates, Country Rules, FEMA/Audit Records, Visa Types, FAQs, Embassies, Audit Log, Settings, Mail Log, etc. would have had no link pointing at them anywhere in the UI. Added a new `admin_subnav(string $groupKey, string $activeNav)` helper (`includes/admin-layout.php`) — a small pill-tab strip (reusing the existing `.admin-toolbar`/`.btn-outline`/`.btn-primary` classes, no new CSS) listing every page in a module family with the current page highlighted, permission-gated exactly like the old sidebar links were. Six groups (`partners`, `b2b`, `leads`, `forex`, `content`, `system`) cover every page the old sidebar used to link to. Called immediately after `admin_header_start()` on all 27 files across those six families (54 call sites total, including create/edit/detail views, not just the list pages) — so every existing admin page keeps its co-navigation, it's just scoped to that page's own module family instead of listing all twelve families on every page. `visa-requirements.php` (which has always used `'countries'` as its own `$activeNav` for the sidebar-highlight, not `'visa-requirements'`) gets its subnav called with the correct `'visa-requirements'` key explicitly, so it highlights itself correctly rather than piggy-backing on Countries' highlight.

**Verified via Playwright** against the local dev DB: confirmed the sidebar renders exactly one link (`["Dashboard"]`) on the dashboard and on every one of six spot-checked hub pages (`/admin/partners/`, `/admin/b2b-partners/`, `/admin/countries/`, `/admin/users/`, `/admin/forex-dashboard/`, `/admin/sales-crm/`), and that each of those pages now renders its own subnav tab strip. `php -l` clean on all 28 touched files (27 page files + `includes/admin-layout.php`). Screenshots delivered to the client for review.

## Roles & Permissions management — Super Admin now controls per-role module access from the UI

Per explicit client rule: Super Admin must have full rights to add/remove admin users and control their access to each service/module "as per requirements" — not be limited to just viewing or resetting dashboard tiles. Users could already be created, edited, assigned a role, and suspended/reactivated (`admin/pages/users.php` — this codebase never hard-deletes any record, so "remove" has always meant reversible suspension, confirmed as the intended meaning). What was missing: the 47 real permissions (`permissions` table) and which of the 14 real roles held which of them were only ever set by seed SQL — there was no screen for Super Admin to actually change what a role could access.

**New `admin/pages/roles.php`** (`/admin/roles/`), gated on `users.manage` — the same permission `users.php` already restricts to Super Admin only:
- **List view**: every role, its user count, and how many of the 47 permissions it holds.
- **Create**: name + optional description; the slug is derived via the existing `slugify()` helper. A new role starts with zero permissions — nothing is granted by default.
- **Edit Permissions**: all permissions grouped by module (derived from the `module.action` shape every `permission_key` already follows, e.g. `forex.rates.manage` groups under Forex — no new taxonomy invented, just read off the existing keys) with a "Select all" toggle per group (`public/assets/js/admin-role-permissions.js`). Saving replaces the role's `role_permissions` rows inside one transaction.
- **Super Admin is permanently protected**: the list shows it with a "Full Access" badge and no Edit link; hitting the edit URL directly redirects with an explanatory error; the save handler independently re-checks the role id server-side before writing anything. This is deliberate — Super Admin's entire purpose is to be the one role guaranteed full access no matter what happens on this screen, so there is no path (UI or direct POST) that could ever accidentally lock every admin out of their own system.

**Wiring**: `case 'roles':` added to `admin/index.php`; `roles` added to the existing `system` subnav group (`includes/admin-layout.php`, the same per-module tab strip introduced for the sidebar-trim change) alongside Users, Audit Log, Settings, Mail Log, and Recycle Bin; the System dashboard tile's description updated to mention it.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB: confirmed the Super Admin role shows as protected and its edit URL redirects with an error even when hit directly; created a new "Test Sales Executive" role, granted it exactly `customers.view`, `customers.manage`, and `enquiries.view` via the checkbox UI, and confirmed all three (and only those three) persisted after save; created a real admin user on that role and logged in as them in a separate browser context; confirmed their dashboard shows only the tiles their granted permissions unlock (Sales CRM, Customers, and HRMS — HRMS is intentionally gated only on being logged in at all, per its existing documented shared-session design, not a role permission) and that Forex/System/B2B tiles are genuinely absent (checked by tile `href`, not just visible text, since Sales CRM's own description copy happens to mention "Forex" as one of the lead sources it aggregates); confirmed a direct hit on `/admin/forex-dashboard/` returns a real 403 with "You do not have permission..." for this user, while `/admin/customers/` returns 200. All test role/user rows and `rate_limits` entries were removed afterward and the real admin password hash restored. `php -l` clean on all 4 touched/new PHP files; `node --check` clean on the new JS file.

## cPanel deployment package — bin/package-cpanel.sh brought current

Client requested a ready-to-upload zip for shared cPanel hosting. `bin/package-cpanel.sh` (the pre-existing flattening/packaging script) had drifted out of date against the SQL files added since it was last touched — its printed import-order instructions were missing `schema-enquiry-v2.sql`/`seed-enquiry-v2.sql`, `seed-recycle-bin.sql`, and the entire B2B Travel Partner Portal SQL set (`schema-b2b-portal.sql`/`seed-b2b-portal.sql`/`schema-b2b-portal-b6/b7/b8.sql`).

**Fixed by empirical verification, not guesswork**: imported all 34 `database/*.sql` files against a fresh throwaway `visagiri_pkgtest` database in the corrected order, confirmed zero import errors on the first attempt, then diffed table/role/permission counts against the live dev database (94 tables, 15 roles, 47 permissions — exact match in both) before editing the script's printed instructions and dropping the test database.

**Also fixed a real packaging gap**: the rsync exclude list only excluded bare `*.log` files, so local-dev test uploads under `storage/documents/` (including four leftover enquiry PDFs and ~20 test images) would have shipped inside the production zip. Added excludes for `storage/documents/*`, `storage/cache/*`, `storage/forex-documents/*`, `storage/hrms-documents/*` — `storage/.gitkeep` still ships so the directory exists on the server; the app's own upload functions already self-create their subdirectories on first real use.

**Verified the produced zip directly** (`visagiri-cpanel-20260907.zip`, 992K): unzipped and confirmed `index.php`'s `$root = __DIR__;` patch applied, `public/`'s contents landed at the package root with no leftover `public/` folder, `config/database.php`/`config/encryption.php` are byte-identical to their `.example.php` templates (`CHANGE-ME` placeholders, no real secrets), `storage/` contains only `.gitkeep`, and the new `admin/pages/roles.php` (Roles & Permissions) is present. The script's own internal `php -l` pass over every packaged file completed without aborting.

## Admin/HR-admin credentials regenerated again (client requested, plaintext still not recoverable from a hash)

Client asked to be given the admin panel credentials. As with the earlier regeneration (see the "Admin credentials regenerated" entry above), the plaintext for the password behind the hash currently in `database/seed.sql`/`database/seed-hrms.sql` was only ever shared once, directly in chat, at the time it was originally generated — bcrypt hashes are one-way, so there was no way to recover it from the repo. Generated two brand-new random passwords (one for `admin`, one for `hr.admin`), hashed both with PHP's `password_hash()`, and updated both seed files' hashes.

**Verified end-to-end**, not an offline check: started the local MariaDB service (had stopped across a container restart earlier in this session) and confirmed the local dev database was intact; updated both accounts' `password_hash` in the live dev DB to the new values; ran a real Playwright login through `/admin/login/` and `/hrms/login/` with each new username/password pair against the actual rate-limiting/CSRF/session code path, landing on `/admin/dashboard/` and `/hrms/dashboard/` respectively for both. Client was given the new plaintext credentials directly in chat, plus `UPDATE admin_users SET password_hash = ...` SQL statements to run against their live database via phpMyAdmin — editing this repo's seed file has no effect on an already-deployed database, and the `visagiri-cpanel-*.zip` already delivered still carries the previous (now-superseded, plaintext-unknown) hash baked into its copy of `database/seed.sql`, so the direct `UPDATE` is the only path that reaches a database already provisioned from that zip.

## Production fatal error on the public enquiry form: `finfo_open()` undefined on the client's cPanel host

Client reported a fatal error on their live site (`/home/tripgation/public_html/visagiri.com/`) breaking `/enquire/`: `Call to undefined function finfo_open()` in `includes/documents.php:63`, inside `validate_document_upload()`. Their production PHP build ships without the `fileinfo` extension compiled in or enabled — not something this codebase controls, and not guaranteed to be the same on every shared-hosting PHP build even though it usually is.

**Every upload path in the app depended on this one extension.** A grep across the codebase found the identical `finfo_open(FILEINFO_MIME_TYPE)` / `finfo_file()` pattern at 15 call sites across 7 files (`includes/documents.php` ×5, `includes/hrms.php`, `includes/enquiry.php`, `includes/forex.php`, `includes/b2b-partner.php`, `includes/b2b-enquiry.php`) — every single upload flow in the app (visa/general enquiries, HR candidate documents, forex requests, B2B partner documents, B2B visa enquiries) would fatal the same way on this host, not just the one form the client happened to hit first.

**Fix**: added one shared `detect_file_mime_type(string $path): ?string` helper (`includes/functions.php`, loaded before every file that needs it via `public/index.php`'s require order). It prefers `finfo_open()` when available — unchanged behavior on every host that has it — and falls back to checking the file's leading bytes against the magic numbers for exactly the five types this app ever accepts (PDF, JPEG, PNG, DOC, DOCX) when `finfo_open` doesn't exist, rather than hard-failing every upload on hosts without it. Replaced all 15 call sites with this one helper, matching this project's zero-dependency, no-Composer approach already established elsewhere (the hand-rolled SMTP client, etc.) rather than reaching for a library.

**Verified against the client's exact failure condition**, not just a local check: ran `php -d disable_functions=finfo_open,finfo_file,finfo_close` (making `function_exists('finfo_open')` false and calling it fatal, exactly reproducing the reported server condition) and confirmed `detect_file_mime_type()` correctly identifies all five allowed types by magic bytes and returns `null` for a renamed `.exe` (so the existing "content doesn't match its extension" rejection still fires — the security property finfo existed for isn't weakened by the fallback). Then, with `finfo` still disabled, drove a real HTTP submission through the actual `/enquire/` form (fresh CSRF token, full multipart POST, a real uploaded PDF) against the local dev database and confirmed: no fatal error, a real 302 redirect to the success confirmation, and the stored `enquiry_documents.mime_type` correctly reads `application/pdf` (not a default/octet-stream fallback) — the fallback path genuinely reaches and correctly annotates the database row, it doesn't merely avoid crashing. `php -l` clean on all 7 changed files. Test enquiry rows and uploaded test files removed from the local dev DB/filesystem afterward.

## Admin dashboard redesign — Phase 1 (real analytics, no emoji icons, self-service profile)

Client sent an 84-section spec for a full "premium enterprise CRM" dashboard rebuild (notification bell, IP/geolocation login audit, customer reminder automation, SLA workflow engine, full sidebar redesign, global search, team workload analytics, etc.). Given the size, and that most of the client's session immediately before this had been spent finding and fixing gaps in their live database's migration history, two decisions were confirmed with the client before writing any code: (1) keep the just-built single-Dashboard-link command center exactly as-is — the spec's own Section 13 asked for a full multi-group sidebar tree, which is a direct reversal of the client's own earlier explicit instruction to trim the sidebar; (2) don't add any *new* database tables in this pass, since doing so on top of a database whose exact state had just been the subject of several rounds of live-crash debugging would compound the risk rather than reduce it. Phase 1 therefore scopes to what can be built truthfully from data that already exists — real KPIs, real enquiry analytics, real activity — and explicitly does not attempt notifications, login audit/geolocation, reminders, or workflow automation, all of which need new schema and were deferred rather than faked.

**What shipped, all backed by real queries against the existing `enquiries`/`audit_logs`/`admin_users` tables — nothing hardcoded**:
- **KPI row** expanded from 4 to 6 real metrics (New Leads Today, Active Enquiries, Resolved Today, Documents Pending, Customers, Active B2B Partners).
- **Enquiry Status Distribution**: a real breakdown of the `enquiries` table's actual status vocabulary (`enquiry_internal_statuses()`, already defined in `includes/enquiry.php` — not a new taxonomy invented for this), rendered as plain CSS bars (no charting library, matching this project's zero-dependency stance everywhere else). Shows "No enquiry data available" rather than a wall of zeros when there's nothing to show.
- **Today's Work Queue**: five real buckets (Urgent Priority, Pending Customer Action, Documents Pending, Internal Processing, Resolved Today) derived from the `enquiries` table's real `priority`/`status` columns — not fabricated categories.
- **Recent Enquiries table** and **Recent Activity feed**: the latest real rows from `enquiries` and `audit_logs` respectively, both tables that already existed and were already being written to.
- **Role-scoped**: a `visa-consultant` (or any role gated by `current_admin_scoped_to_assigned()`) sees only their own enquiries across every one of these widgets, matching the scoping `admin/pages/enquiries.php`'s own list view already enforces — the dashboard must not leak a wider view than the page it links to.
- **Module tile icons**: replaced all 11 emoji icons with inline stroke-SVG icons (new `admin_module_icon()` in `includes/functions.php`), matching the exact visual language (20×20 viewBox, `currentColor` stroke, 1.5 width) already established by `company_nav_icon()`/`primary_nav_icon()` on the public site, so the admin panel now reads as one deliberate icon system rather than emoji in the admin panel and hand-drawn line icons everywhere else.
- **Topbar profile dropdown** replaces the plain "name + Logout" block: avatar-initials trigger, role label, links to a new self-service **My Profile** page (`admin/pages/profile.php` — edit own name/email, change own password, gated to the logged-in admin's own `admin_users` row only, no new schema) and to Settings (permission-gated), plus Logout. Click-to-toggle JS in `admin.js`, closes on outside-click or Escape.
- **Cleanup**: removed the dead `.admin-sidebar__group`/`.admin-sidebar__subgroup` CSS and JS toggle logic left over from the earlier sidebar-trim work — that markup no longer exists in `admin-layout.php`, so the rules were orphaned.

**Explicitly deferred, named rather than silently dropped**: notification bell + real-time alerts, login audit with IP/geolocation tracking, customer reminder system with email/SMS/WhatsApp templates, SLA/workflow automation engine, global cross-entity search, staff workload/performance analytics beyond what's directly computable today. All of these need new tables (`notifications`, `login_audits`, `reminders`, etc.) or new integrations (geolocation API, SMS/WhatsApp provider — neither exists in this project, confirmed by earlier phases' own audits) that weren't going to be built and verified responsibly in the same pass as a live production database recovery.

**Verified via Playwright** against the local dev DB: real admin login, confirmed zero emoji characters render anywhere on the dashboard, confirmed all four new panels (Status Distribution, Work Queue, Recent Enquiries, Recent Activity) render with real data, confirmed the profile dropdown opens/closes and the Profile page loads with real account data. Screenshot-verified at both desktop (1440px) and mobile (390px) — caught and fixed a real mobile issue in the process: the Recent Enquiries table was squeezing into the viewport instead of scrolling horizontally (`.admin-table` had no `min-width` inside its scroll wrapper); fixed and confirmed via a DOM-level check (table renders at its intended 720px+ width inside a horizontally-scrollable, page-width-respecting container) that the fix is real, not just visually similar. `php -l` clean on all 5 changed/new PHP files; `node --check` clean on `admin.js`. Screenshots delivered to the client.

## Admin dashboard redesign — Phase 2 (notification bell)

First of the items explicitly deferred out of Phase 1. One new table (`admin_notifications`), following the same one-row-per-recipient fan-out shape this codebase already uses for `customer_notifications`/`partner_notifications`/`b2b_partner_notifications` — no shared/broadcast-plus-read-receipts design invented for this.

**What shipped**:
- **Schema** (`database/schema-admin-notifications.sql`, idempotent — `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`): `admin_notifications` (id, admin_user_id, type, title, body, link, is_read, read_at, created_at), FK to `admin_users` with `ON DELETE CASCADE`.
- **`includes/admin-notifications.php`** — new helper library: `create_admin_notification()` (single recipient), `notify_admins_by_permission()` (fans out to every active admin whose role holds a given permission key, via the existing `role_permissions`/`permissions` join — no new RBAC concept), `admin_unread_notification_count()`, `admin_recent_notifications()`, `mark_admin_notification_read()` (ownership-scoped — a notification can only be marked read by the admin it belongs to), `mark_all_admin_notifications_read()`. `notify_admins_by_permission()` is wrapped in try/catch + `error_log()` so a notification failure can never block the real business action that triggered it (matches the client's own spec requirement that notification delivery "must not fail the enquiry").
- **Hooked into new-enquiry creation** (`pages/enquire.php`): after a public enquiry is saved, every admin holding `enquiries.view` gets a notification with the customer name, service type, and a direct link to the enquiry.
- **Bell UI** (`includes/admin-layout.php` + `admin.css`): topbar bell icon with an unread-count badge, dropdown listing the 6 most recent notifications (each its own `POST` form — CSRF-protected, no GET-triggered mutation), "Mark all as read", and a link to the full Notification Center. Badge count refreshes every 45s via a small `fetch()` poll of a new JSON endpoint (`/admin/notifications-unread-count/`) — plain polling, not WebSocket/SSE, consistent with this app having no persistent-connection infrastructure anywhere else.
- **Notification Center** (`admin/pages/notifications.php`, `/admin/notifications/`): full list, filterable by All/Unread/Read, click-to-open-and-mark-read (server-side POST-then-redirect to the notification's linked record), "Mark all as read".
