**Bug found and fixed during verification**: `.admin-bell__badge { display: flex; ... }` in `admin.css` unconditionally set `display: flex`, which — per CSS cascade rules — overrides the browser's built-in `[hidden] { display: none }` UA rule regardless of source order, since author styles always beat UA styles at equal specificity. The practical effect: even when the server correctly rendered the badge with the `hidden` attribute (0 unread), the badge stayed visibly on screen. Fixed with an explicit `.admin-bell__badge[hidden] { display: none; }` rule (higher specificity than the plain class selector, so it wins regardless of order). Found via a Playwright assertion that failed after a real mark-as-read action, not by inspection — confirmed independently via `curl` with a real session cookie that the server-rendered HTML and the JSON endpoint both correctly reported 0/hidden, isolating the bug to CSS rather than the PHP/SQL logic.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB, driven through the real UI (no direct DB inserts): submitted a real public enquiry through the actual 5-step `/enquire/` wizard (including a mandatory document upload) → confirmed a notification was created and visible in the bell dropdown with the correct title and customer name → confirmed the unread-count JSON endpoint matched the badge → clicked the notification → confirmed it navigated to the correct `/admin/enquiries/?id=N` detail page and marked the notification read server-side → confirmed the badge correctly disappeared afterward (post-CSS-fix) → confirmed the Notification Center page listed it and that the Read/Unread filters worked correctly. `php -l` clean on all 9 changed/new PHP files; `node --check` clean on `admin.js`; `php -l` clean on `admin.css`. All test enquiry rows, their uploaded documents (DB rows and on-disk files), and their notification rows were removed from the local dev DB/filesystem afterward. Screenshots delivered to the client. The new `admin_notifications` table has not yet been applied to the client's live database — `database/schema-admin-notifications.sql` needs to be run there via phpMyAdmin's Import tab before this feature will work in production.

## Admin dashboard redesign — Phase 3 (login audit + reminder automation)

Second and third of the items deferred out of Phase 1. Both were scoped after a research pass across the existing codebase to see what was already there before writing anything new — the honest finding: IP capture already existed three times over (`login_attempts`, `user_sessions`, `audit_logs`), but geolocation and reminder scheduling had zero prior infrastructure.

**Login Audit** (`admin/pages/login-audit.php`, `/admin/login-audit/`, gated on the existing `audit.view` permission):
- Lists every login attempt from `login_attempts` (already populated on every sign-in, success or fail, since the original auth build) — username, result badge, IP, location, and user agent, filterable by username, paginated.
- **"New IP" flag**: a single grouped query (`MIN(attempted_at)` per username+IP over successful logins) rather than a per-row subquery, so a row is flagged the one time it's genuinely a user's first successful login from that address.
- **Active Sessions panel** (Super Admin only, gated on `users.manage`): every currently-live `user_sessions` row with a **Revoke** button that force-logs-out that session (`expired_at = NOW()`). Fixed a real pre-existing bug in the process: `user_sessions.last_activity_at` was set once at login and never updated again, so every session looked "active" forever regardless of actual use. `require_admin_login()` (`includes/auth.php`) now touches it on every authenticated request, and the Active Sessions query only shows sessions active in the last 24h (PHP's own session garbage collection reclaims session data well before that point, so an older row is a stale leftover from a closed browser tab, not a live session). ~87 such stale rows accumulated across this project's entire testing history were cleared from the local dev DB as part of verifying the fix — none of that carries over to a fresh production database anyway.
- **Geolocation, scoped honestly**: no geolocation infrastructure existed anywhere in this codebase before this — no API key, no outbound HTTP client, and this sandbox (like the client's shared host, per `includes/mail.php`'s own long-standing caveat about SMTP) may have no outbound network access at all. Built `includes/geolocation.php` — a single short-timeout (2s), no-API-key request to ip-api.com's free endpoint, results cached per-IP in a new `ip_geolocation_cache` table so a given address is only ever looked up once. Critically, **resolution is never automatic**: a page load here is always just an indexed DB query; each unresolved IP gets its own small "Resolve" button an admin clicks deliberately, so a login-audit page view can never stack N outbound HTTP timeouts onto one page render. Private/loopback/LAN addresses (`FILTER_FLAG_NO_PRIV_RANGE`/`NO_RES_RANGE`) are recognized and labeled "Local / Private Network" without any network call. Like `send_mail()`, this can never throw — a failed or unreachable lookup degrades to "Unknown," cached so it isn't retried on every view.

**Reminder Automation** (`includes/reminders.php`, new `reminders` table, `admin/pages/reminders.php` at `/admin/reminders/`, gated on new `reminders.manage` permission granted to Super Admin/Admin/Visa Consultant — the same role set `enquiries.view`/`manage` already has):
- Reminders can be created standalone or from a specific enquiry (a new "Reminders" panel added to the enquiry detail view in `admin/pages/enquiries.php`), each with a title, optional note, due date, and an assigned admin.
- **Deliberately admin-triggered, not cron-driven** — the same decision this project already made twice before (see the document-expiry and tier-recalculation entries above): no cron infrastructure exists anywhere in this codebase, and cPanel Cron Jobs availability on the client's actual hosting plan has never been confirmed. `notify_due_reminders()` fans out an `admin_notifications` row (reusing the Phase 2 bell system, not a new notification channel) the first time a reminder becomes due — guarded by a `notified_at` column so it never re-notifies on a later page load, and it's cheap enough (a handful of indexed queries) to call opportunistically on every dashboard load, which is exactly what `admin/pages/dashboard.php` now does. A new "Reminders Due" panel on the dashboard shows live Overdue/Due Today/Upcoming counts, scoped to the logged-in admin's own reminders for a `visa-consultant`-style scoped role, matching every other dashboard widget's existing scoping rule.
- `/admin/reminders/` lists everything with Overdue/Due Today/Upcoming/Mine/All/Done filter tabs, a creation form, and Mark Done / +1 Day snooze actions per row (snoozing clears `notified_at` so a re-snoozed reminder correctly notifies again when its new date arrives).
- `bin/check-reminders.php` — an optional CLI entrypoint calling the exact same `notify_due_reminders()` function, for a client whose cPanel plan does offer Cron Jobs and wants reminders to notify even on a day nobody opens the dashboard. Nothing depends on it; the feature works fully without it.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB: a real failed login attempt followed by a real successful one, both correctly appearing in the Login Audit list with the right result badges; the Active Sessions panel showing genuinely live sessions with a working Revoke button; clicking "Resolve" on an unresolved IP correctly identified `127.0.0.1` as a private address (cached, no network call attempted) without any error, confirming the graceful-degradation path since this sandbox has no outbound network access to actually test the live ip-api.com fetch; a reminder created from a real existing enquiry's detail page correctly appearing there, in the dashboard's Reminders Due widget, in the bell notification dropdown, and in the Notification Center — reloading the dashboard a second time correctly did **not** create a duplicate notification (verified via the unread-count endpoint), confirming the `notified_at` idempotency guard actually works, not just in theory; marking it done correctly moved it from the "Due Today" tab to "Done." `php -l` clean on all 11 changed/new PHP files. Test reminders and their notification rows were removed from the local dev DB afterward; the login/session audit trail itself was left intact, consistent with not purging genuine audit history. The new `ip_geolocation_cache` and `reminders` tables (plus the `reminders.manage` permission) have not yet been applied to the client's live database — `database/schema-login-audit.sql` and `database/schema-reminders.sql` need to be run there via phpMyAdmin's Import tab.

## Public site redesign — Phase 1 (brand & technical-trust foundation)

Client sent a 50-section "COMPLETE redesign and content/SEO transformation" spec positioning Visagiri as a premium India-based visa consultancy, asking for a full IA rebuild, hundreds of new SEO landing pages, and a technical-SEO/schema overhaul. Given the size, this was scoped like every other large client spec this session: audited first (three research passes across nav/footer/routes/SEO/content), a phased plan written and approved before any code, and Phase 1 limited to what the audit found was genuinely missing or broken — not a wholesale rebuild of what already works.

**Audit found most of the spec already built** from earlier phases: a live "Resources" mega-menu, a real 6-column footer, `/countries/`, `/attestation/`, `/visa-type/`, `/resources/`, all 7 legal pages, and 28 real indexed city/state SEO pages already exist. The homepage already has a real hero + DB-driven visa-search widget (destination/visa-type/nationality/date), popular destinations, services, "How It Works", an honest "Why Visagiri" (no fabricated stats), and a real FAQ section. Phase 1 therefore closes the specific real gaps the audit found:

- **Removed "A Unit of Tripgation Pvt Ltd" from the public footer** (`includes/footer.php` — brand blurb and copyright bar) — the spec's single most-repeated instruction, marked "IMPORTANT" / "VERY IMPORTANT" / "FINAL CHECK" across three separate sections. The disclosure itself is untouched and still correct on `/about/`, `/leadership/`, `/terms/`, and `/privacy/` (the spec explicitly wants it kept there, just not repeated in the footer/nav). CIN and founding-date facts stay in the footer — legal registration disclosure, not brand promotion.
- **Removed a genuinely dead homepage section** — `pages/home.php`'s "Latest Visa Updates" was permanently hardcoded to an empty array (no blog content management exists), always rendering "No visa updates have been published yet." directly above a real, populated "Explore Visa Resources" section that already does that job. One redundant empty section removed, nothing lost.
- **Rebuilt `/blog/`'s empty state** into an honest explainer (what the section is for, why nothing's published yet) with real links to Documentation, Document Templates, FAQs, Visa Process, and the Resource Hub — instead of a bare "No Updates Published Yet." Still `noindex` — no articles were fabricated.
- **Added a shared `breadcrumb_schema()` helper** (`includes/functions.php`) replacing what was previously a hand-rolled `BreadcrumbList` array literal copy-pasted per page, and used it to close a real coverage gap: 11 real, indexed hub/content pages (`countries/index.php`, `careers/index.php`, `about.php`, `contact.php`, `faq.php`, `document-templates.php` ×2 branches, `embassy-directory.php`, `process.php`, `leadership.php`, `our-team.php`, `track-visa.php`) had no `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD at all despite being the same tier as pages that already did.
- **Elevated two stub pages** (`pages/affiliations.php`, `pages/corporate-services.php`) off the generic `render_scaffold_page()` "This page isn't ready yet" copy onto real, honest, brand-quality content — Affiliations explains real accreditations will be published once verified (no invented bodies/logos), Corporate Services describes real corporate/group visa support as an extension of the existing consultancy process, linking to `/contact/` and the real B2B portal. Both stay `noindex` — still not the spec's full envisioned content, just no longer dev-scaffold text.
- **Built a real `/disclaimer/` page** (`pages/legal.php`) — the last of the 8 Legal & Support Centre pages still a stub. Not new legal copy: the exact, already-vetted disclaimer text was already live sitewide in the footer disclaimer bar; this just gives it its own indexable page like its 7 siblings.
- **Found and fixed a related real gap while there**: none of the 7 existing legal pages (Privacy, Terms, Travel Terms, Payment Policy, Refund Policy, Cookie Policy, Grievance) were in the XML sitemap at all, only the human-readable one. Added all 7 plus the new Disclaimer page to `pages/sitemap-xml.php`.
- **Enriched `/visa-process/`** into the fuller "Visa Consultancy & Application Management Services" page the spec's §12 asked for — same URL (avoiding the duplicate-URL trap the spec itself warns against), new H1, a "What's Included" section naming only real, already-built capabilities (document review, application tracking at `/track-visa/`), and a link to the new Corporate Services page for group/team coordination. Deliberately did **not** add refusal/reapplication guidance or biometrics-appointment specifics — no verified process detail exists for either, and inventing one would be exactly the "unsupported claim" problem the spec forbids.

**Explicitly deferred, named rather than silently dropped** (all require either real sourced content this session doesn't have, or a larger separately-scoped effort): destination-from-India landing pages (`/visa/usa-from-india/` etc. — risks the duplicate-content trap the spec itself warns against, given `/visa/{country}/` pages already exist and already target an India audience); real embassy/consulate/VAC directory data (schema exists, zero real rows — `pages/embassy-directory.php`'s honest "Not yet published" empty state is left as-is); FAQ expansion (only 5 general FAQs exist despite schema supporting country/visa-type-specific ones); the remaining 4/15 target India city pages (Delhi, Pune, Chandigarh, Lucknow) still not indexable; a real Document Checklists page (`enquiry_document_requirements` has only 1 seeded rule — too sparse for genuine per-combination content); blog articles, real Visa Tools, leadership bios beyond the 2 confirmed, accreditation data, analytics/tracking event wiring, and a formal redirect map.

**Verified via Playwright** against the local dev server: confirmed "Tripgation" no longer appears anywhere on the homepage while still correctly appearing on `/about/`, `/leadership/`, and `/terms/`; confirmed the homepage no longer renders the dead "Latest Visa Updates" section; confirmed `/blog/` shows the new explainer with working real links, still noindexed; spot-checked 5 of the 11 breadcrumb pages for valid `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD with the correct name chain; confirmed `/affiliations/` and `/corporate-services/` render real copy (not scaffold text) while staying noindexed; confirmed `/disclaimer/` renders indexable with the correct disclaimer text and now appears in `sitemap.xml` alongside its 7 siblings; confirmed `/visa-process/` renders the enriched content with working links to `/track-visa/` and the new Corporate Services page. `php -l` clean on all 19 changed/new files. No test data to clean up — every change here is real, permanent content, not verification scaffolding.

## Public site redesign — Phase 2 (FAQ expansion + final 2 India city pages)

Continuing the deferred list from Phase 1, scoped to what could be built honestly without any new fabricated facts.

**Real gap found in the prior audit before writing anything**: Phase 1's own deferred list said 4 of the 15 target India cities (Delhi, Pune, Chandigarh, Lucknow) were still not indexable. Checking the live database directly (not re-trusting the earlier grep-based audit, which only read the initial seed file and missed a later `UPDATE` in `seed-location-seo-phase2.sql`) showed **Delhi and Chandigarh are already indexable** — only **Pune** and **Lucknow** were genuinely still gaps. Scoped the work to those 2 real remaining cities instead of 4.

- **`database/seed-location-seo-phase6.sql`**: real content for Pune (Maharashtra) and Lucknow (Uttar Pradesh) — both already had city rows and indexable parent states from Phase 2, just no page content. Same honest pattern as every prior location-SEO phase: no Visagiri office in either city (real offices are Patna, Bihar and Noida, Uttar Pradesh only), so both explicitly say "served remotely"; characterizations are limited to well-established, defensible facts (Pune's IT/education/manufacturing base, Lucknow's role as UP's administrative and education capital) — no invented statistics. 2 location FAQs per city, idempotent via `NOT EXISTS` guards.
- **`database/seed-general-faqs-phase2.sql`**: 10 new general FAQs (`visa_faqs`, `country_id`/`visa_type_id` both NULL) covering ground the 5 existing ones didn't — apostille vs. embassy attestation, tourist vs. business visa, group/family/corporate applications, remote consultation, travel insurance, reapplication after refusal, and the government-vs-service-fee distinction. Every answer only restates facts already established elsewhere on the site (attestation pages, the new Corporate Services page, `/visa-process/`) — no specific fees, processing-day counts, or country-specific claims invented. Idempotent via `NOT EXISTS` guards on the question text (`visa_faqs` has no unique key).

**Bug found and fixed during verification**: the first import of both seed files via `mysql -u root visagiri < file.sql` corrupted every em-dash into mojibake (`â€"`) — the local `mysql` client's `character_set_client` defaults to `latin1`, so it misread the files' real UTF-8 bytes. Caught via a direct page screenshot, not assumed. Fixed by deleting the corrupted rows and re-importing with `mysql --default-character-set=utf8mb4`; verified the fix at the byte level (`HEX()` on the affected column showed the correct 3-byte UTF-8 em-dash sequence, `E2 80 94`, instead of the corrupted 6-byte mojibake sequence) before re-checking every page. Both seed files' own SQL source was confirmed clean UTF-8 throughout — this was purely an import-command issue, not a file-encoding one. Flagging for the client: phpMyAdmin's Import tab defaults to UTF-8 and should be unaffected, but it's worth confirming the "Character set of the file" setting reads UTF-8 before importing either of these two files.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB: confirmed all 15 general FAQs (5 original + 10 new) render on `/faq/` with correct `FAQPage` JSON-LD (`mainEntity` count = 15); confirmed `/visa-consultant/maharashtra/pune/` and `/visa-consultant/uttar-pradesh/lucknow/` both return 200 with the new real content, the honest "no physical office" FAQ, and no em-dash corruption anywhere on either page; confirmed both cities now appear in `sitemap.xml` (fully dynamic, driven by `is_indexable` — no code changes were needed for sitemap inclusion). Both seed files re-run cleanly with zero duplicate rows on a second import (idempotency verified directly, not assumed). `php -l` not applicable (SQL-only changes this round).

## Admin CRM: enquiry SLA/workflow automation + global search

Resumed after the two public-redesign phases — the last of the items deferred out of the original 84-section dashboard spec (notification bell and reminders shipped in Phases 2-3). Both were researched before writing code: the existing `grievances` SLA/escalation implementation (`admin/pages/grievances.php`, `database/schema-legal-phase1.sql`) is the direct template — render-time-computed breach (never a stored flag, no cron anywhere in this project), manual escalate-to-eligible-admin with an audit-logged reason — and every existing per-page search box (`enquiries.php`, `customers.php`, `partners.php`, `b2b-partners.php`) already uses the same `LIKE '%term%'` pattern global search reuses, since no full-text search infrastructure exists anywhere in the codebase.

**SLA + workflow automation** (`database/schema-enquiry-sla.sql`, `includes/enquiry.php`, `admin/pages/enquiries.php`):
- `enquiries` gains `sla_due_at`, `escalated_at`, `escalated_from_user`, `escalation_reason`, `sla_breach_notified_at` — same shape as `grievances`' existing columns.
- Unlike grievances' single flat SLA-hours setting, an enquiry's SLA window is **priority-driven** (`enquiry_sla_hours_for_priority()`, 4 new admin-editable `site_settings`: urgent/high/normal/low, defaulting to 4/24/72/120 hours) and **auto-recomputed whenever priority changes** — the "workflow" behavior the original spec asked for: escalating an enquiry's priority to Urgent from the update form automatically retargets its own deadline from that moment, it isn't a one-time value set only at creation. Set on creation too (`pages/enquire.php`, using the 'normal' priority window every new enquiry starts at).
- `notify_breached_enquiry_slas()` is the automatic half — fans out an `admin_notifications` row (reusing the Phase 2 bell system, not a new channel) the first time an enquiry breaches its SLA, guarded by `sla_breach_notified_at` so it fires exactly once per breach, called opportunistically from `admin/pages/dashboard.php` on every load — same "admin-triggered, not cron" pattern as `notify_due_reminders()` (Phase 3), for the same reason: no cron infrastructure exists anywhere in this project and cPanel Cron Jobs availability on the client's host has never been confirmed.
- Manual **Escalate** action on the enquiry detail page reassigns to any admin holding `enquiries.manage`, requires a reason, audit-logged via `log_action('escalation', ...)` — identical shape to grievances' escalation, not a new pattern invented for this.
- List page: SLA badge column (Breached/On track) + an "SLA Breached" filter chip; dashboard gets a new "Enquiry SLA" panel showing the live breached count, linking straight to the filtered list.

**Global search** (`admin/pages/search.php`, `/admin/search/`, plus a search box in the admin topbar — `includes/admin-layout.php`):
- Searches enquiries, customers, referral partners, and B2B partners in one box — each entity only searched (and only shown) if the logged-in admin already holds that entity's own `.view` permission, so this can never surface a record a role couldn't otherwise open directly; it's a shortcut into the existing list/detail pages, not a new access path.
- Results capped at 8 per entity with a "View all matches" link into that entity's own already-existing filtered list view when there are more — this stays a fast, always-fresh render on every page load, not a paginated search index.

**Bug found and fixed during verification**: the topbar search form had no visible submit control — just an SVG icon and an input, relying on implicit Enter-to-submit. Caught by a Playwright test that tried to click a search button and found none. Fixed by wrapping the icon in a real `<button type="submit">`, giving the box a proper accessible affordance rather than depending on undiscoverable keyboard-only behavior.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB, driven through the real UI: submitted a real enquiry through the actual `/enquire/` wizard and confirmed `sla_due_at` was set correctly at creation; changed its priority to Urgent from the admin update form and confirmed the SLA deadline recomputed to the new priority's window; used the Escalate form and confirmed the reassignment, audit log entry, and "Escalated:" line on the detail page; backdated the SLA due date directly in the database to force a real breach, then confirmed the dashboard's new "Enquiry SLA" panel showed the correct count, the notification bell showed a real "SLA Breached" alert, and — critically — reloading the dashboard a second time did **not** create a duplicate notification (checked via the unread-count endpoint), confirming the idempotency guard works in practice, not just in theory; confirmed the "SLA Breached" filter on the enquiries list correctly surfaced the breached enquiry. Global search: confirmed it found the test enquiry by name from both `/admin/search/` directly and the topbar search box, correctly grouped under an "Enquiries" section with a working View link. `php -l` clean on all 8 changed/new PHP files. Test enquiries, their uploaded documents (DB rows and on-disk files), and their notification rows were removed from the local dev DB/filesystem afterward. The new `enquiries` SLA columns and `site_settings` seed rows have not yet been applied to the client's live database — `database/schema-enquiry-sla.sql` needs to be run there via phpMyAdmin's Import tab.

## PDF receipts: branded letterhead + General Enquiry & Forex coverage

Client asked whether a customer can download their enquiry number as a PDF after submitting Visa, Forex, Apostille, or General enquiries, and to add it wherever missing, with the Visagiri logo placed centrally. Researched first rather than assuming: **Visa and Apostille enquiries already had a full working PDF receipt system** (`includes/pdf.php`'s `build_enquiry_pdf()`, reference+tracking-token gated download at `/enquire/pdf/`) — its only real gap was a small, left-corner logo. **General Enquiries (`pages/contact.php`'s Visa/Attestation/Forex/Travel/General selector) and Forex Requests had no PDF capability at all.** Forex also has a structural difference from the other three flows: every `forex_requests` row is staff-created via the admin panel (`admin/pages/forex-requests.php`) — there is no public self-submission form, so there's no "submission success" moment to hand a customer a fresh token the way Visa/Apostille/General enquiries get one.

Mid-task, the client supplied the real official letterhead artwork (`public/assets/images/pdf-letterhead-bg.jpg`, converted from their PNG — logo + "Your Journey Our Expertise" tagline top, a centered translucent VG watermark with a world-map/flight-path graphic, and a navy "YOUR GLOBAL VISA PARTNER" contact-info bar at the bottom) — this superseded the initial plan to just re-center the existing small corner logo. All three PDF types now use this exact letterhead as a single full-page background image, with receipt content laid out in the safe zone between the header and footer bars (`pdf_draw_letterhead_background()` / `pdf_start_page()`, new `PDF_CONTENT_TOP_Y`/`PDF_CONTENT_BOTTOM_Y` constants in `includes/pdf.php`) — this directly answers "keep logo of Visagiri in central," since the centered VG mark is now the letterhead's own watermark rather than a small corner logo being artificially centered.

- **`database/schema-enquiry-pdf-downloads.sql`**: `general_enquiries` gains `tracking_token` (same non-enumerating reference+token download gate the Visa/Apostille system already uses) and `pdf_path`; `forex_requests` gains only `pdf_path` — no token column, since there's no submission moment to hand one out.
- **`includes/pdf.php`**: `build_general_enquiry_pdf()` and `build_forex_request_pdf()` added alongside the existing `build_enquiry_pdf()` (whose corner-logo header was refactored onto the same shared `pdf_draw_letterhead_background()`/`pdf_start_page()` helpers, so all three — and any future continuation page from a long document list — render identically branded). Forex's builder only surfaces the same customer-safe fields `/forex/track/` itself already shows (reference, currency, amount, status, travel dates) — no internal staff remarks, quotation margins, or compliance notes.
- **`pages/contact.php`**: generates a `tracking_token` (`generate_tracking_token()`) alongside the existing reference number at submission, best-effort generates the PDF right then (a PDF failure never blocks the enquiry itself — the download route regenerates on demand if `pdf_path` is empty or the file's gone missing), and now shows the reference number plus a "Download PDF Receipt" link in the success alert. New gated route `pages/general-enquiry-pdf-download.php` at `/contact/pdf/`.
- **`forex/track/index.php`**: since forex has no token to hand out, the download button reuses the page's own already-verified reference+mobile lookup — a hidden-field POST form (not a GET link, so the customer's mobile number never lands in a bookmarkable URL or server access log) to a new `pages/forex-pdf-download.php` at `/forex/track/pdf/`, which re-runs the identical `forex_requests JOIN customers` reference+mobile query before generating/streaming the PDF.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the local dev DB, driven through the real UI: submitted a real enquiry through `/contact/`, confirmed the success alert showed the real reference number and a working Download PDF Receipt link, downloaded it, and rendered it with `pdftoppm`/`pdftotext` — confirmed the letterhead (logo, tagline, centered VG watermark, navy footer bar) and every field (reference, tracking number, service type, status, name, email, phone, message, disclaimer) rendered correctly in the safe content zone; logged into the admin panel and created a real forex request via `/admin/forex-requests/`, then logged out and tracked it as a real customer would via `/forex/track/` with the same reference + mobile number, confirmed the new Download PDF Receipt form appeared on the success card, downloaded it, and confirmed it rendered correctly with the same letterhead and only customer-safe fields; deleted an existing Visa/Apostille enquiry's stale pre-refactor PDF file and re-downloaded it via `/enquire/pdf/` to force on-demand regeneration, confirming it now renders on the identical new letterhead rather than the old left-corner logo. Two selector bugs were caught and fixed during this verification, not assumed away: a generic `button[type="submit"]` selector in the test script matched the admin topbar's search button instead of the intended form (both pages have more than one submit button) — fixed by scoping every test click to its specific `form[action="..."]`. `php -l` clean on all 5 changed/new PHP files. Test general enquiries (`VG-GEN-2026-000001`/`000002`), the test forex request (`VG-FX-2026-000001`) and its auto-created customer record, and all generated test PDF files were removed from the local dev DB/filesystem afterward; the real existing Visa/Apostille enquiry's regenerated PDF was left in place since it's genuine data, not test scaffolding. The new `database/schema-enquiry-pdf-downloads.sql` and the new `public/assets/images/pdf-letterhead-bg.jpg` letterhead asset have not yet been applied to the client's live site — the schema file needs to be run via phpMyAdmin's Import tab, and the asset just needs to be part of the next file deployment.

## Sitewide "Enquire Now" dialog (service picker + in-place multi-step forms)

Client shared two reference screenshots of a modal-based enquiry flow — a dialog with a service-picker grid, transitioning in place to a multi-step form ("next window appears on the same screen") — and asked for this behavior on the site. Before building, two real scope questions were raised with the client via the same "no invented services" discipline used all session: the reference mockup showed 8 service tiles including ones Visagiri has no real backing form for (Corporate Travel, Flights, Hotels, Car Rentals), and used a purple/pink/red palette that doesn't match the site's navy/gold brand anywhere else. Client confirmed: 4 real tiles only (Visa Services, Apostille & Attestation, Forex, General Enquiry — the existing catalog, Travel Services folded into General), and the site's own navy/gold brand rather than the mockup's colors.

**Today's actual behavior, audited before building**: "Enquire Now" (header CTA, mobile menu, floating widget) was a plain link straight to the full-page `/enquire/` wizard (Visa/Apostille only, multi-step with file upload); Forex and General Enquiry only existed via `/contact/`'s own simpler service-selector page. Neither was a dialog, and picking a service always meant a full page navigation. The two existing, already-working, already-tested submission systems (the `/enquire/` wizard and `/contact/`'s form) were kept as the real backend — this was a UI/UX wrapper around them, not a rebuilt submission pipeline, so nothing about validation, storage, PDF generation, or email/notification logic changed.

- **`includes/enquiry-wizard-fields.php`** (new): the Visa/Apostille wizard's actual form markup (steps 1-4, file uploads, review) extracted out of `pages/enquire.php` into a reusable partial — a pure refactor, zero behavior change to the standalone page, done specifically so the modal could lazy-load the identical markup instead of duplicating ~230 lines of form fields that would drift out of sync over time.
- **`pages/enquire-wizard-fragment.php`** (new, routed at `/enquire/wizard/`): renders just that partial with no header/footer chrome, for the modal to `fetch()` the first time a visitor picks Visa or Apostille — keeping every other page load free of the countries/visa-types queries this wizard needs, rather than loading them sitewide.
- **`pages/enquire.php` / `pages/contact.php`**: both POST handlers gained a small AJAX branch — when the request carries an `X-Requested-With: fetch` header (set only by the modal's JS), a successful submission returns JSON (`reference`, `tracking_token`, `pdf_url`, and — Visa/Apostille only — `track_url`) instead of doing `redirect()`/rendering the full page; a failed one returns JSON errors instead of re-rendering. The standalone pages' existing behavior for a normal browser POST is completely unchanged — this is an additive branch, not a rewrite, so direct navigation to `/enquire/` or `/contact/` (no-JS, search engines, bookmarks) still works exactly as before.
- **`includes/enquiry-modal.php`** (new, included once from `footer.php` so every "Enquire Now" entry point shares one dialog instance): the picker (4 tiles), a small static form for Forex/General (name/email/phone/message, mirroring `/contact/`'s own fields), an empty container the Visa/Apostille wizard fragment loads into, and a generic success panel.
- **`public/assets/js/enquiry-wizard.js`** (refactored) / **`enquiry-modal.js`** (new): the wizard's step-switching logic was rewritten from a page-load-time IIFE into a reusable `VisagiriEnquiryWizard.init(root, options)` function, scoped entirely to `root` via `root.querySelector(...)` rather than `document.getElementById(...)` — so it can run correctly against either the standalone page's wizard or a second copy lazy-loaded into the modal without the two ever cross-matching, even sharing the same element ids (a real risk if a visitor opens the modal while already on `/enquire/`; `querySelector` scoped to a root only searches that root's descendants, so this is safe by construction, not by convention). When given `data-ajax-submit`, the wizard's submit now goes through `fetch()` with `FormData` (file uploads included) instead of a normal browser POST, and calls back into `enquiry-modal.js` to render the success panel in place. The modal's own simple Forex/General form does the same against `/contact/`. Net effect: the picker → form transition, filling in every field, and the final submission are all in-place DOM updates — no page navigation happens anywhere in the flow, only the standalone pages (still there as a fallback) do a real POST.
- **`public/assets/css/enquiry-modal.css`** (new, added to `header.php`'s sitewide stylesheet list): navy gradient header band per step (matching the mockup's structure, `--visa-blue-dark`/`--visa-gold` tokens instead of its purple/pink/red), a 2-column tile grid collapsing to 1 column under 640px, focus-trapped dialog with backdrop/Escape/close-button dismissal.
- Wired `data-open-enquiry-modal` onto all 4 real "Enquire Now" entry points (`includes/header.php`'s desktop and mobile CTAs, `includes/enquiry-widget.php`'s floating-widget icon) — JS intercepts the click and opens the modal; the `href="/enquire/"` stays in the markup unchanged as the no-JS fallback.

**This feature was built, verified, and lost to an environment reset mid-session, then rebuilt from scratch and re-verified — worth recording plainly.** Partway through the first verification pass, this session's container was reprovisioned: the working tree was replaced with an unrelated cached repository state, and the local MariaDB instance/config files were gone entirely. The already-pushed commit history (through the PDF-letterhead work) was intact on GitHub and safely recovered via `git fetch` + `git reset --hard origin/...`, but this feature's files had never been committed yet, so they had to be re-authored from the same source captured earlier in the conversation, and the local dev database rebuilt from scratch (MariaDB installed fresh, `database/schema*.sql`/`seed*.sql` re-applied, `config/database.php`/`config/encryption.php` regenerated — both gitignored by design, never recoverable from git, which is exactly why they're gitignored real secrets rather than checked-in values).

**Re-verifying from scratch caught a real bug the first pass never exercised**: the modal originally passed an explicit (but empty) `onError` callback into `VisagiriEnquiryWizard.init()`, intending "enquiry-wizard.js already renders its own inline errors" — but passing *any* function there, even a no-op, skips that library's own default `showFormErrors()` fallback, so every server-side validation error was being silently swallowed instead of shown to the visitor. The first verification pass never caught this because its test happened to pick a visa type with no mandatory-document rule attached, so the error path was simply never exercised. This rebuild's fresh seed data inserted visa types in a different order, so the same test script picked "Business Visa" (which does have a real mandatory-document rule) and immediately surfaced the bug via a hung submit button instead of a visible error. Fixed by removing the empty `onError` override entirely, letting the library's default error rendering take over — confirmed by deliberately re-triggering a validation failure and watching the real error message appear inline above the review step, with the modal staying open and every filled field intact so the visitor can fix it and resubmit.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** against the rebuilt local dev DB, driven through the real UI: clicked the header's real "Enquire Now" button from the homepage and confirmed the modal opened with all 4 tiles, no page navigation; General Enquiry tile → filled and submitted the simple form → success panel rendered in place with the real reference number and a working PDF link, URL never changed; closed and reopened the modal, Forex tile → same simple form relabeled correctly ("Forex" eyebrow) → submitted → success, in place; closed and reopened again, Visa Services tile → the real multi-step wizard fragment loaded via fetch (confirmed via the network — no full navigation), filled all 4 steps including a real file upload for the passport document, submitted → success panel showed both the reference number *and* tracking number plus working PDF-download and Track-Enquiry links, still on the same URL throughout; confirmed no browser console/page errors across the whole run; separately re-triggered the mandatory-document validation error on purpose and confirmed it now renders inline in the modal instead of being swallowed. Confirmed `/enquire/` and `/contact/` still return 200 and render their normal standalone forms with exactly one `#enquiry-form` in the DOM (no id collision) when visited directly. `php -l` clean on all 9 changed/new PHP files; both JS files pass `node -c`. All test rows this created in `general_enquiries`/`enquiries` (plus uploaded documents/status-history) and their generated PDF files were removed from the rebuilt local dev DB/filesystem afterward, and rate-limit counters reset. Nothing here needs a live-site database migration — no schema changed, only PHP/JS/CSS files.

## Production bug: PDF letterhead silently missing on the flattened cPanel deployment

The client deployed the flattened cPanel package (`bin/package-cpanel.sh`) built earlier this session and forwarded a real customer's enquiry receipt PDF — every field was correct, but the letterhead (logo, watermark, navy footer bar) was entirely absent, just bare white space where it should have been.

**Root cause**: `pdf_draw_letterhead_background()` (`includes/pdf.php`) hardcoded the two-folder dev layout's path — `__DIR__ . '/../public/assets/images/pdf-letterhead-bg.jpg'` — with no fallback. The flattened cPanel package moves everything under `public/` up to sit directly beside `includes/`, so on that layout the real file lives at `<root>/assets/images/pdf-letterhead-bg.jpg`, one directory level shallower than the hardcoded path expects. `is_file()` on the wrong path just returns `false` with no error or warning — exactly why this shipped silently instead of throwing something visible in a server log. `includes/functions.php`'s own `asset_url()` already solves this exact problem (checking both `public/assets/...` and `assets/...` and using whichever exists) for every `<link>`/`<script>`/`<img>` tag on the site; `pdf_draw_letterhead_background()` was the one place in the codebase that built a raw filesystem path instead of going through that helper, and it never got the same dual-layout treatment.

**Fix** (`includes/pdf.php`): the function now checks both candidate paths, same order and pattern as `asset_url()`, and uses whichever one actually exists on disk. Verified by literally reproducing both deployment shapes in temp directories — a `public/assets/...` two-folder layout and a flattened `assets/...` layout, each with only `includes/pdf.php` and a copy of the real letterhead JPEG — and calling `pdf_draw_letterhead_background()` directly against both; confirmed the output PDF embeds the JPEG (`DCTDecode` present, ~125KB) in both cases, where before the fix the flattened case produced a PDF with no embedded image at all (matching the ~4KB bare-text PDF the client actually received). A full grep across the codebase for the same hardcoded-`public/`-path pattern found exactly one other spot (`asset_url()` itself, which already handles both layouts correctly) — no other function has this bug.

**A fresh deployment zip was rebuilt and delivered** with this fix included. The client's live site needs the corrected `includes/pdf.php` re-uploaded (the rest of the previously-deployed files don't need to change). One thing this fix does **not** do on its own: the download routes' on-demand regeneration (`pages/enquiry-pdf-download.php` and its General/Forex siblings) only regenerates when `pdf_path` is missing or the file itself is gone — a broken-but-present PDF from before this fix will keep being served as-is on repeat downloads, since the file exists and the check never re-examines its actual contents. Every *new* submission from the moment the fix is deployed generates a correct PDF automatically; any enquiries submitted before that (like the one the client forwarded) will keep serving their already-generated broken copy until the stored file is deleted from `storage/documents/enquiry-pdfs/`, `storage/documents/general-enquiry-pdfs/`, or `storage/documents/forex-pdfs/` (matched by reference number) — deleting it is enough, since the next download request then finds no file and regenerates it correctly through the exact same on-demand path.

## New "Why Visagiri" page + About page redesign (bespoke "vintage travel document" visual treatment)

Client asked to retire `/about/#why-visagiri` as a destination and replace it with a dedicated `/why-visagiri/` page built from a supplied creative design — a distinct Newsreader/IBM Plex Google-Fonts-driven "vintage travel document" aesthetic (parchment/navy/brass palette, a circular wax-seal-style stamp, a stats strip, numbered "register" entries) — then followed up with a second, matching design for `/about/` itself (hero, statement, stats, story, a condensed "why" strip linking out to the new page, a "People & Trust" directory, CTA, and legal disclaimer/CIN line).

Both designs were adapted rather than pasted verbatim: the supplied About mockup hardcoded "10" visa categories, which is factually wrong (the real seeded count is 9 — Tourist, Business, Student, Work, Family Visit, Transit, Medical, Conference, Sports); every number on both pages (visa-type count, country count, founding year) is now sourced live via `visa_types_all()`/`countries_all()`/`setting('company_founding_date', ...)` rather than hardcoded, so neither page can drift stale or repeat that error. The About page's "People & Trust" directory links (Leadership, Our Team, Careers, Become a Partner, Affiliations & Accreditations, Contact Us) were checked against `public/index.php`'s real routes before being added — all six already exist. The CIN shown in the new disclaimer line reuses the existing real `setting('company_cin', 'U63030UP2020PTC128661')`, the same value already shown in the footer, not a new claim.

- **`pages/why-visagiri.php`** (new, routed at `/why-visagiri/` via a new `case 'why-visagiri':` in `public/index.php`): hero with a "SINCE APR `<year>`" seal, a 4-stat strip, an "Our Story" section (same facts as `/about/#our-story`, retold at this design's pace), an "Our Approach" intro line, and 5 stamped register entries (EX/DA/TP/QR/HS) that elaborate on the exact same five items `why_visagiri_features()` (`includes/functions.php`) already returns for the homepage's condensed version — longer copy, not new claims — ending in the standard `whatsapp_enquiry_href()` CTA.
- **`public/assets/css/why-visagiri.css`** (new): the page's full bespoke stylesheet, scoped entirely under `.why-visagiri-page` (including its own `--wv-`-prefixed custom properties) so it can load sitewide via `header.php`'s shared stylesheet array — matching this project's established convention (every page-specific CSS file, e.g. `home.css`/`visa.css`/`countries.css`, already loads unconditionally on every page and relies on its own wrapper scope rather than conditional loading) — without leaking styles onto any other page.
- **`pages/about.php`** (rewritten): the old `#why-visagiri` `<section>` (using `why_visagiri_features()` in the site's default navy/gold design) is gone; the page now uses the same bespoke visual treatment as the new page, with a condensed "why" strip (reusing `why_visagiri_features()`'s existing short titles/descriptions as-is) that links out to `/why-visagiri/` for the full version, plus the new "People & Trust" directory and legal disclaimer/CIN line. The `id="our-story"` anchor was kept on the story section, so the existing `/about/#our-story` link in `header.php`'s Company mega-menu still resolves correctly.
- **`public/assets/css/about.css`** (new): same pattern as `why-visagiri.css`, scoped under `.about-page` with its own `--ap-`-prefixed custom properties, added alongside `why-visagiri` in `header.php`'s sitewide stylesheet array.
- **`includes/header.php`**: both existing references to `/about/#why-visagiri` (the Company mega-menu's "Why Us" entry and the mega-menu's "Explore Our Approach →" featured link) now point to `/why-visagiri/`; `'why-visagiri'` and `'about'` added to the sitewide CSS array.
- **`includes/security.php`**: the CSP's `style-src` gained `https://fonts.googleapis.com` and a new `font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com` directive, scoped specifically to let these two pages' Google Fonts `@import` load — every other page still only uses the system font stack, so this doesn't widen what any other page can load.

**Verified via Playwright** against the local dev DB: both `/why-visagiri/` and `/about/` return 200; confirmed the rendered stat values are the real sourced figures (`2015`, `208+`, `9`, `24×7` — not the mockup's incorrect `10`) on both pages; confirmed the About page's six directory links resolve to the real routes (`/leadership/`, `/our-team/`, `/careers/`, `/partners/`, `/affiliations/`, `/contact/`) and its "Read the full page →" link points to `/why-visagiri/`; confirmed `/about/#our-story` still scrolls to the right section; took full-page desktop (1440px) and mobile (390px) screenshots of both pages confirming the scoped CSS renders correctly with no bleed into the shared navy/gold header/footer. `php -l` clean on all changed/new PHP files. The sandbox's outbound network proxy blocked the actual Google Fonts file fetch during this verification pass (`net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`, not a CSP violation — a CSP block logs differently), so both pages rendered on their system-font fallback locally; the CSP headers themselves were reviewed to confirm `fonts.googleapis.com`/`fonts.gstatic.com` are correctly allowed, and the real fonts will load once deployed to the live site's normal internet access.

**Correction, same session, next task**: the "9, not the mockup's 10" claim above was wrong, and the error was mine, not the mockup's. `database/seed.sql` alone does seed only 9 visa types, but `database/schema-visa-portal.sql` — a real, later migration, already present in the repo and in the documented cPanel import order — adds a genuine 10th type, **Dependent Visa**, shifting Family Visit/Transit/Medical/Conference/Sports down in sort order to make room for it. The local dev DB used for that verification pass had never had `schema-visa-portal.sql` applied correctly (its `INSERT` had apparently landed as an `UPDATE` overwriting row id 1, turning "Tourist Visa" into a second "Dependent Visa" and silently losing Tourist entirely, with none of the accompanying sort-order shifts applied) — an artifact of this session's mid-conversation container-reset rebuild, not a production data issue. Fixed the local DB directly: restored id 1 to Tourist Visa exactly as `seed.sql` defines it, applied the sort-order shifts, inserted the real Dependent Visa row, and backfilled `country_visa_types` for it across all 208 countries — exactly what `schema-visa-portal.sql` itself does, run by hand since it had never actually taken effect locally. The real, current visa-type count is **10** (Tourist, Business, Student, Work, Dependent, Family Visit, Transit, Medical, Conference, Sports), not 9. This doesn't change any code from the entry above — both `pages/why-visagiri.php` and (at the time) `pages/about.php` already sourced the count live via `visa_types_all()` rather than hardcoding it, so they self-corrected the moment the underlying data was fixed; only this document's narrative was wrong, and only this paragraph corrects it. No client-facing content anywhere in the codebase hardcoded "9" or omitted Dependent Visa.

## About page reverted to the site's standard design; leadership/team/affiliations/partners pages get expanded copy

Client uploaded 8 reference HTML mockups (`about.html`, `our-story.html`, `leadership.html`, `our-team.html`, `careers.html`, `partners.html`, `affiliations.html`, `contact.html`) in a plain blue/Inter "standard business site" style, asking to integrate them "as per website standard text size color." Two real conflicts were surfaced to the client before touching anything, rather than guessed at:

1. **About page**: the previous entry above had just given `/about/` a bespoke "vintage parchment" treatment at the client's own explicit request one turn earlier, and this upload's `about.html` used the plain, simple styling instead — a direct contradiction. Client's answer: **revert `/about/` to the site's plain standard design** (matching Leadership/Partners), built from this upload's content instead of the vintage one.
2. **Careers & Contact**: the uploaded `careers.html`/`contact.html` are static placeholder content ("no open positions," 3 contact cards) — far simpler than the real `careers/index.php` (a full HRMS-backed job listing + application system) and `pages/contact.php` (a real 5-way enquiry router saving to the database) already live on the site. Replacing either with the upload's static content would have deleted working functionality. Client's answer: **leave both alone entirely** — only integrate the other 6 pages.

**`pages/about.php`** (rewritten again, vintage version fully removed): now uses the same standard component classes as every other Company page (`.section`/`.container`/`.section-heading`/`.card-grid`/`.accordion-item`, matching `pages/leadership.php`/`pages/partners.php`/`pages/faq.php`), with content merged from the upload's `about.html` and `our-story.html`: Who We Are, a 4-stat "at a glance" strip, What We Do (visa-category list now built from `visa_types_all()` with a small inline oxford-comma join rather than hardcoded, so it can't repeat the 9-vs-10 mistake above), an expanded Our Story (folding in `our-story.html`'s three extra paragraphs — the pre-2015 paperwork/phone-call framing, the tech-plus-consultant combination, and "what hasn't changed" — under the same `id="our-story"` anchor the header's mega-menu already links to), a "Visa management, reimagined" teaser linking out to `/why-visagiri/` (still its own distinct bespoke page, untouched — that design choice was separately requested and stands), a People & Trust directory of links, a 3-item FAQ block with `FAQPage` structured data, and the existing final WhatsApp CTA. `public/assets/css/about.css` (the vintage stylesheet) and `'about'` in `header.php`'s sitewide CSS array were both removed since nothing references them anymore; `why-visagiri.css`/`'why-visagiri'` were left in place.
- **`pages/leadership.php`**: added the upload's two extra sections ("How leadership shapes Visagiri," "Connect with the wider team" with a related-links row), same `.section`/`.card` pattern already on the page.
- **`pages/our-team.php`**: added "How the team works together" plus an `.alert-info` note box (replacing the old single Contact-Us button with a proper "Talk to the team" related-links row), same pattern.
- **`pages/affiliations.php`**: added the upload's second explanatory paragraph and a two-link related row (About Us, Contact Us).
- **`pages/partners.php`**: added a 2-item FAQ accordion section (the upload's third FAQ item — "not ready to register yet?" — was skipped as redundant, since the page's own enquiry-form section immediately below already covers exactly that).

**Verified via Playwright** against the fixed local dev DB: all five touched routes (`/about/`, `/leadership/`, `/our-team/`, `/affiliations/`, `/partners/`) return 200 with zero page errors; confirmed About's stat cards now read `2015`/`208+`/`10`/`24×7`, its "What we do" paragraph lists all 10 real categories by name (pulled live, not hardcoded), the `#our-story` anchor still exists, and its FAQ renders 3 accordion items; confirmed Partners' new FAQ renders 2 accordion items. `php -l` clean on every changed file.

## Country visa content system + Singapore country/visa-type pages

Client requested a full "country-wise standalone visa service architecture" — a Singapore hub page plus 10 child visa-type pages (`/visa/singapore/tourist`, `/business`, `/students`, `/family`, `/medical`, `/transit`, `/conference`, `/employment`, `/dependent`, `/official`), reusable across future countries, with real researched content (not templated find-replace), local Patna/Bihar SEO, FAQs, schema markup, and technical SEO — explicitly warning against thin/duplicate programmatic pages and fabricated facts (fees, processing times, "government approved" claims).

**Architecture decision, checked with the client before building**: the site already has a data-driven `/visa/{country}/{type}/` route (`visa/index.php`) backed by `visa_requirements`/`visa_faqs` tables, with an honest "requirements not yet verified" fallback — but it renders a thin spec-sheet, not the rich editorial content requested. Two real forks were surfaced rather than guessed at: (1) extend the existing DB-backed system vs. build a parallel content system — client chose **extend existing**, matching the project's own "don't hardcode, don't duplicate" discipline; (2) the requested URLs used `/students`, `/employment`, `/official`, which don't match the real global `visa_types` catalog (`student`, `work`, no `official` category at all) — client chose **use the real catalog + redirect the mismatched slugs**, and separately confirmed merging "employment" into the existing "work" category rather than adding a new one. `/official` (diplomatic/government travel) was not built — Visagiri doesn't offer this as a real service, and inventing a global visa type no other country would use wasn't justified by one client request.

- **`database/schema-country-content.sql`** (new): adds a `country_content` table (hub-level rich content — overview, who-needs-a-visa, Patna-specific SEO section, services-across-India section, common mistakes/refusal factors, source URL, last-reviewed date) and extends `visa_requirements` with `overview`, `who_should_apply`, `common_mistakes`, `local_notes`, `fee_notes` columns, plus widening `processing_time`/`validity_period`/`stay_duration` from their original short VARCHAR sizes (sized for "5-7 business days", not full explanatory sentences). Ships empty for every country except Singapore — every other country keeps rendering the existing honest fallback.
- **`includes/data.php`**: added `visa_types_for_country()` (the hub previously listed *every* global visa type regardless of `country_visa_types.is_available`, ignoring that column entirely — a real pre-existing gap, not something this task introduced, but one that mattered now that Singapore needed a type actually hidden); `fetch_country_content()`; `fetch_country_faqs()`. **Fixed a real bug** in the pre-existing `fetch_relevant_faqs()`: its `country_id = X OR visa_type_id = Y` logic would have leaked *every* Singapore page's FAQs onto *every other* Singapore page (via the country match alone) and *every* country's Tourist-visa FAQs onto every other country's Tourist page (via the type match alone) — harmless while the table was empty, but a real cross-contamination bug the moment real per-country-per-type FAQs existed, which they now do. Changed to an exact country+type match (plus the general/untagged rows), added as its own function for the hub's country-only (type `NULL`) FAQ set.
- **`visa/index.php`**: added a slug-alias redirect (`students`→`student`, `employment`→`work`, 301, site-wide not Singapore-specific) before any DB lookup. Hub block: rich sections (overview, who-needs-a-visa, delay/refusal reasons, Patna section, services-across-India, Why Choose Visagiri via the existing `why_visagiri_features()`, country-level FAQ, last-reviewed/source line) all render conditionally — a country with no `country_content` row still gets exactly the previous generic page, nothing regresses. Visa-type block: added overview/who-should-apply prose, a hardcoded (not per-row) processing-time disclaimer sentence matching the client's own requested wording, a fees section that separates government fee/service-fee/notes and never fabricates a figure, common-mistakes/local-notes cards, a Why Choose Visagiri section, a "why-should-it-link-to-siblings" internal-linking block to the country's *other available* visa types (fixed mid-build: this list initially used the global catalog, which still showed a Sports Visa link Singapore doesn't offer — caught via screenshot review, fixed to use the same availability-aware `visa_types_for_country()` as the hub), and page title/H1 that upgrade to the requested SEO pattern (`"{Country} {Type} Consultant in India"`) only once real content exists for that pair, not for every thin fallback page.
- **`pages/sitemap-xml.php`**: previously excluded every `/visa/{country}/{type}/` leaf page outright (avoiding submitting ~1,600 mostly-thin pages). Added `published_visa_requirement_slugs()` (`includes/data.php`) and now includes exactly the pairs with a real `visa_requirements` row — currently the 9 real Singapore pages, growing automatically as more countries get real content, without ever mass-submitting the unpublished ones.
- **`database/seed-country-content-singapore.sql`** / **`database/seed-faqs-singapore.sql`** (new): real Singapore content for all 9 real visa categories that apply there (Tourist, Business, Student, Family Visit, Medical, Transit, Conference, Work, Dependent — Sports marked unavailable for Singapore via `country_visa_types`, since Visagiri doesn't offer a distinct Singapore sports-visa service) plus the hub page, and 69 FAQs (20 shown on the hub including the 5 pre-existing general ones, 11 on each type page). Content is grounded in Singapore's real ICA (Immigration & Checkpoints Authority) visit-pass framework and Ministry of Manpower (MOM) work-pass framework — researched via web search this session since ICA's own site returned `EGRESS_BLOCKED` when fetched directly from this sandbox, so facts are corroborated across multiple independent sources rather than a single primary citation. Deliberately conservative about anything time-sensitive or unverifiable: **no government or service fee figures were entered** (`government_fee`/`service_fee` left `NULL` on every row) — third-party sources cited a specific current fee, but without a primary-source ICA fetch to confirm it, presenting it as fact felt like exactly the "invented government fee" the client explicitly ruled out; `fee_notes` explains this and directs applicants to get a current quote instead. Processing times use qualified language ("commonly quoted as...", "MOM does not publish a fixed turnaround...") rather than guaranteed numbers, matching the client's own requested disclaimer wording. The Work Visa and Dependent Visa pages are explicit that these are Ministry of Manpower Employment Pass / Dependant's Pass processes — a different authority and application channel from ICA's visit-pass system — rather than loosely calling every Singapore work-related trip a "work visa" the way some third-party aggregators do.
- No claim anywhere states or implies Visagiri is ICA-authorised, embassy-authorised, government-approved, or able to guarantee approval — every category page's process description says applications go "through an ICA-authorised Singapore visa agent" (referring to the channel, not asserting Visagiri itself holds that authorisation) and every fees/processing-time section carries the "decisions are made by the relevant authority" framing already established sitewide.

**Verified via Playwright** against the local dev DB: all 10 target URLs return 200 (`/visa/singapore/`, `/tourist/`, `/business/`, `/student/`, `/family/`, `/medical/`, `/transit/`, `/conference/`, `/work/`, `/dependent/`); confirmed `/students/`→`/student/` and `/employment/`→`/work/` 301 redirects; confirmed the hub shows exactly 9 visa-category cards (Sports correctly hidden) and 20 FAQ accordion items; confirmed the Tourist page's H1/title upgraded to "Singapore Tourist Visa Consultant in India" / matching SEO title, its 8-item document checklist, 9-step numbered application process, and 11 FAQ items; confirmed breadcrumb, Service, and FAQPage structured data are present; confirmed no other country's `/visa/{country}/` page changed behavior (still renders the pre-existing generic fallback). `php -l` clean on every changed/new PHP file. Full-page desktop screenshots taken of the hub, Tourist, and Work pages.

**Not done in this pass, flagged rather than silently skipped**: the other 8 real visa-type pages beyond Tourist/Business (Student, Family, Medical, Transit, Conference, Work, Dependent) have real content and FAQs but weren't individually screenshot-reviewed the way Tourist/Work were — spot-checked via the automated data assertions instead. The `/visa-consultant/{city}/` local-landing-page architecture the brief also requested (Patna, Bihar, Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata, ...) was **not built this pass** — the Singapore hub's own "Visa Consultant in Patna" section covers the client's most specific ask (Patna/Bihar local SEO for Singapore), but the broader multi-city landing-page system is a separate, similarly-sized piece of work not yet started. Real image assets (Marina Bay, Gardens by the Bay, etc.) were not sourced or added — the pages use the existing flag-emoji/card-icon visual language already established on every other `/visa/{country}/` page rather than introducing licensed photography this session had no way to source. The government/service fee figures are deliberately blank pending a client-confirmed number, since no primary ICA source could be verified. This Singapore build is the first real use of the `country_content`/rich `visa_requirements` fields — rolling out to more countries means writing this same depth of real, researched content per country, not a mechanical copy of Singapore's rows.

## Visa Checklist Engine — country + visa-type document checklists, public/locked split, enquiry-gated unlock

Client requested a full "Visa Checklist Engine": independent, country+visa-type-specific document checklists on every `/visa/{country}/{type}/` page, ~40-50% shown publicly with the rest gated behind an enquiry, manually admin-controlled visa fees (never scraped), and unlock via the *existing* 5-step Enquire Now wizard rather than a new form — first built for `/visa/mexico/tourist/`, architected to extend to any country+type without code changes. A 50-section brief also asked for a full admin CRUD panel, Puppeteer-based dynamic PDF generation, fee-history UI, CAPTCHA/Turnstile, and analytics event tracking — given the realistic size of that (a multi-week build on its own), this pass delivered the real, secure, working **core engine** and flagged the rest explicitly below rather than attempting all of it thinly or claiming it was done when it wasn't.

**One brief-vs-reality call made before building, not silently overridden**: the brief suggested Puppeteer/Chromium for PDF generation. This project has no Node runtime in its deployment target — it's a zero-dependency PHP app shipped as a flattened cPanel package (`bin/package-cpanel.sh`), the same constraint that led to the hand-rolled `SimplePdfWriter` in `includes/pdf.php` rather than TCPDF/mPDF. Puppeteer would need a Node process running permanently on shared PHP hosting, which isn't available here — so PDF generation is deferred (see below) rather than built against infrastructure this site doesn't have, and the print-CSS/browser-print path is delivered as the working authorized "print" experience instead.

**Database** (`database/schema-visa-checklist.sql`, new): `visa_checklists` (one per country+visa_type, `UNIQUE(country_id, visa_type_id)` — never shared between pairs), `visa_checklist_sections`, `visa_checklist_documents` (each document flagged `is_public` individually, admin-curated rather than a computed "show N%" slice — matches the brief's own "don't hide basic info just to force a lead" rule), `visa_checklist_revisions` (a change-log, not a full snapshot-based version history — see deferred items), `visa_fees` + `visa_fee_history` (manually controlled, `set_visa_fee()` in `includes/visa-checklist.php` logs the old→new change before applying a new one), and `checklist_access_tokens` (stores only a SHA-256 hash of the access token, same discipline as the existing tracking-token pattern — a database leak alone can't forge access). All six tables reuse the existing shared `countries`/`visa_types` catalog rather than introducing a parallel one.

**Security — the part the brief was most insistent on**: the complete checklist is **never sent to the browser before authorization**, in any form (not blurred, not hidden via CSS/JS, not in a data attribute). `visa/index.php`'s type-detail block calls `split_checklist_for_visibility()` (`includes/visa-checklist.php`) server-side, which returns only genuinely public documents in full, plus a list of *locked section titles only* (never their documents) for the teaser. A visitor without access literally cannot see a locked document's name or description anywhere in the page source — confirmed by grepping the rendered HTML for locked-only document names before verifying anything else. Access is authorized via a per-country+type httpOnly, Secure, path-scoped cookie (`vg_checklist_{country_id}_{visa_type_id}`) set only after a real enquiry is saved — never localStorage/sessionStorage, matching the brief's explicit requirement. `has_checklist_access()` re-verifies the cookie's token against the database (hash match + not expired + exact country/type match) on every request; nothing is cached client-side as an "unlocked" flag.

**Reused the existing Enquire Now system end to end, added nothing parallel**: `pages/enquire.php` (the real Visa/Apostille 5-step wizard, already handling customer info, service+travel details, document upload, review, and AJAX JSON submission before this task) already had almost everything the brief described as new requirements — separate country-code + mobile-number fields, `?country=`/`?visa_type=` prefill, honest server-side validation, file upload, a declaration checkbox. The checklist button just feeds it context:
- `includes/enquiry-wizard-fields.php` gained two hidden fields (`checklist_reference`, `unlock_checklist`) rendered only when a checklist context is present.
- `pages/enquire-wizard-fragment.php` (the modal's lazy-loaded fragment endpoint) now reads `?country=`/`?visa_type=`/`?checklist_ref=` and prefills the wizard's selects accordingly.
- `pages/enquire.php`'s POST handler, after an enquiry successfully saves, checks `unlock_checklist`+`checklist_reference` against the enquiry's own *resolved* country+visa-type (never trusting the POSTed reference blindly) — if a published checklist genuinely matches, it calls `generate_checklist_access_token()`, sets the unlock cookie, and returns `checklist_unlocked`/`checklist_url` in the AJAX JSON response. The enquiry's `source_page` also records `/visa-checklist/{reference}/` instead of the generic `/enquire/` when applicable, so admin/CRM reporting can see which checklist page drove the enquiry (the brief's requirement #43) without a new column.
- `public/assets/js/enquiry-modal.js` gained a `data-open-enquiry-modal-checklist` trigger (skips the 4-tile picker, opens straight into the prefilled visa wizard — matching the brief's "existing 5-Step Enquiry modal opens" flow) and a "View Complete Checklist" button on the success panel once `checklist_unlocked` comes back true.

**`visa/index.php`**: the type-detail block now checks for a published checklist independently of the existing `visa_requirements` freeform system (a country+type can have either, both, or neither — Mexico Tourist has only the new checklist). When present: a checklist-specific hero (Visa Type / Applicant Country / Country / Checklist Reference / Last Reviewed, primary CTA "Enquire Now & Unlock Full Checklist", secondary "Talk to a Visa Expert"), a manually-controlled Visa Fee card (`visa_fees`, separate from `visa_requirements`' own freeform fee fields used by Singapore — deliberately not merged, since one is admin-curated-authoritative and the other is a general note), the numbered checklist sections themselves, and — only when locked content remains — the 🔒 teaser block with section titles and the unlock CTA; otherwise a plain "Print Checklist" button (`window.print()`, the same pattern already used elsewhere on this page). A pre-existing "Requirements not yet verified" fallback (from the older `visa_requirements` system) was **fixed mid-build**: it was rendering directly underneath the new checklist content whenever a page had a checklist but no old-style `visa_requirements` row — contradicting the checklist just shown above it. Now it only appears when *neither* system has content for that pair.

**Verified via Playwright** against the local dev DB, driving the real UI rather than just asserting DB state: loaded `/visa/mexico/tourist/` logged out and confirmed the 6 real public documents render (Core Documents in full, half of Application & Photograph) and grepped the full page source for five locked-only document names (Employment Letter, Bank Statement, Income Tax Returns, Travel Insurance, Consulate Appointment Confirmation) — zero matches; clicked "Enquire Now & Unlock Full Checklist" and confirmed the modal opened directly into the visa wizard (no picker) with Mexico/Tourist Visa already selected and the two hidden checklist fields present; filled and submitted the real wizard (name, mobile, email, city, state, passport details, a real file upload, declaration) end to end; confirmed zero validation errors, the enquiry actually saved (`VG-ENQ-2026-000001`), the success panel showed a working "View Complete Checklist" link, and a `vg_checklist_172_1` cookie was set (httpOnly: true, path scoped to `/visa/mexico/tourist/`); followed that link and confirmed the previously-locked documents now render in full and the 🔒 teaser is gone entirely. Confirmed Singapore's pages (which use the older `visa_requirements` system, no checklist) render unchanged, and a Mexico visa-type with neither system (`/visa/mexico/business/`) still shows the honest "not yet verified" fallback. `php -l` clean on every changed/new PHP file; `node -c` clean on `enquiry-modal.js`. Test enquiry, its status-history row, and its checklist-access token were deleted from the local dev DB afterward; no test document files were left in `storage/`.

**Deliberately not built this pass — real, sized work, not forgotten**:
- **Admin CRUD panel** (§26-27 of the brief) — creating/editing checklists, sections, documents, and fees is entirely SQL-seeded right now (see `database/seed-checklist-mexico-tourist.sql`), the same pattern this session used for Singapore's content. `set_visa_fee()` in `includes/visa-checklist.php` is the real, safe function a fee-editing form would call — building that form is wiring a UI to already-correct logic, not new logic, but it's still a real screen (or several) that doesn't exist yet.
- **PDF generation** (§29-35) — see the Puppeteer note above. "Print Checklist" today triggers the browser's own print dialog on the live page rather than a bespoke branded A4 layout; a proper letterhead-branded, dynamically-paginated PDF would need meaningful extension of the existing hand-rolled `SimplePdfWriter` (feasible — it already draws tables/checklists for enquiry PDFs — but a distinct, sequenced piece of work).
- **Full checklist version history** (§28) — `visa_checklist_revisions` logs *that* a version changed and a summary, not a snapshot of the old content field-by-field; true "diff any two versions" auditing isn't built.
- **Fee history is data-only** — `visa_fee_history` populates correctly through `set_visa_fee()`, but there's no admin screen to view it yet.
- **CAPTCHA/Turnstile** (§41) — the existing honeypot field + `rate_limit_check()` (5 submissions/15 min per IP) is the same anti-abuse level every other form on this site uses; Turnstile itself was not integrated.
- **Analytics event tracking** (§40) — no event pipeline (page view, section-viewed, unlock, print, download) was wired up; would need a decision on where those events should go (Google Analytics/GTM isn't currently configured anywhere on the site, per earlier audit entries).
- **Strict Indian mobile regex** (`^[6-9][0-9]{9}$`, §16) — the existing wizard's `normalize_mobile_number()` accepts 7-15 digit numbers with any country code, which already handles Indian applicants correctly but doesn't reject a malformed 9-digit or 11-digit Indian number the way the brief's stricter pattern would. Left unchanged this pass since it's shared by *every* enquiry on the site, not just checklist ones — tightening it is a real, separate decision worth its own verification pass rather than a side effect of this feature.
- Only Mexico Tourist has real checklist content; the other 9 Mexico visa types and every other country render the honest "not yet verified" state until checklists are written for them, same discipline as `visa_requirements` and the Singapore `country_content` system.

## Visa Checklist Engine — premium visual redesign (hero, quick-facts, tabbed layout)

Client shared a reference mockup for `/visa/mexico/tourist/` — a navy/gold hero with destination imagery, a floating quick-facts card (Visa Fee / Processing Time / Consular Office / Application Method), a tabbed layout (Overview / Document Checklist / Application Process / Fees / FAQ), a table-based document checklist (not a checkbox list), and a redesigned locked-checklist panel with a trust-badge row — and asked for the checklist page to match it visually.

- **New fields on `visa_checklists`** (`database/schema-visa-checklist-overview.sql`): `purpose`, `eligibility_summary`, `typical_stay`, `application_method`, `personal_appearance`, `processing_note`, `consular_office`, `application_steps` — the "Visa Overview" data the original 50-section brief asked for (§7) but the first pass hadn't built yet, since it was scoped to the checklist/fee/security core. Seeded for Mexico Tourist with real content (Embassy of Mexico consular office, 10-15 working day indicative processing, personal appearance required, the 7-step application process).
- **`public/assets/css/visa-checklist.css`** (new): the premium hero/quick-facts/tabs/table/locked-panel styling, built entirely from the site's real navy/gold tokens (`--visa-blue`, `--visa-gold`, etc. — not a new palette) rather than trying to reproduce the mockup's own colors from scratch. No licensed destination photography was available in this sandbox, so the hero uses a navy gradient + subtle diagonal pattern instead of a real Mexico City photo — flagged rather than silently substituted.
- **`visa/index.php`**: the checklist branch was substantially rewritten — a real tabbed interface (Overview / Document Checklist / Application Process / Fees) replaces the single long scrolling card stack; the document checklist itself now renders as an actual `<table>` per section (Document / Details-Guidelines / Type columns) rather than a checkbox list; the locked panel got the mockup's lock-icon header, dashed locked-section list, and a 3-item trust-badge row. FAQ was deliberately kept as its own section below the tabs rather than a 5th tab, to avoid duplicating the FAQ markup that already existed there — a scope trim, not an oversight.
- **`public/assets/js/visa-checklist.js`** (new): plain tab-switching (toggles `hidden` on the panel matching the clicked tab's `data-vc-tab`), plus the hero's "View Complete Checklist" button (shown to already-unlocked visitors) jumping straight to and activating the checklist tab.

**A real bug caught and fixed during this pass, not just during Singapore/About work**: the first implementation wrote the tab-switching script as an inline `<script>` block. This site's CSP (`includes/security.php`) is `script-src 'self'` — it silently blocks all inline scripts, so the tabs rendered correctly but literally did nothing when clicked, with no JavaScript exception (a CSP violation is a console warning, not a thrown error, so an error-listener-only test wouldn't have caught it either). Diagnosed via a Playwright test that captured `console` events specifically (not just `pageerror`), which surfaced `Refused to execute inline script because it violates the following Content Security Policy directive: "script-src 'self'"`.
