Each was already a pre-cropped 2048×736 WebP; converted to the site's standard JPEG-quality-82 hero format via PHP's GD extension (same `imagecreatefromwebp`/`imagejpeg` approach used for Mexico's PNG hero, since ImageMagick/cwebp aren't available in this sandbox) and saved to `public/assets/images/visa-heroes/{qatar,spain,netherlands,switzerland,vietnam,malaysia}.jpg`. `hero_image_url` was updated directly in the database and in each seed file (previously `NULL`, falling back to the gradient hero) — confirmed via Playwright screenshot that all six Tourist pages now render their real country photo instead of the navy/gold gradient placeholder.

## Autonomous top-market rollout continues — Indonesia, Turkey, Sri Lanka, Ireland, Portugal, and Hong Kong, all 10 visa types each

Fourth autonomous batch, continuing the "proceed automatically" instruction. Six research agents ran in parallel via web search, each independently confirming their target country's official government domains (evisa.imigrasi.go.id, evisa.gov.tr/mfa.gov.tr, eta.gov.lk/immigration.gov.lk, irishimmigration.ie/ireland.ie, vistos.mne.gov.pt, immd.gov.hk) were blocked at this session's network egress layer — every one of the six seed files carries an explicit sourcing-caveat header documenting this and naming the secondary sources actually used to triangulate each fact, per the pattern established for Qatar in the prior batch. Genuinely uncertain fees were left as `amount = NULL` with a "Confirmed After Assessment" label rather than guessed, following the same discipline as every prior batch's fee rows.

**`database/seed-checklist-indonesia-all-types.sql`** (country_id 68): India is explicitly NOT on Indonesia's visa-free list — every Indian traveller needs at least the e-VOA, unlike several ASEAN neighbours built in earlier batches. Indonesia's 2025 visa-index coding system (C1–C22, D1/D2, E23/E31...) is reflected directly in the reference codes and category names. A real, dated (28 May 2025) mandatory in-person biometric re-registration applies to any C1/e-VOA extension. A genuine Bali-specific tourist levy (IDR 150,000, via the official "Love Bali" portal) stacks on top of the national visa fee for Bali entries specifically. Work visas carry a distinctive recurring DKPTKA levy (USD 100/month of KITAS validity) rather than a one-time fee.

**`database/seed-checklist-turkey-all-types.sql`** (country_id 100): A genuinely conditional e-Visa eligibility rule — Indians only qualify for the e-Visa if they already hold a valid Schengen/US/UK/Ireland visa or residence permit, otherwise a full sticker visa is required. Turkey runs its own fully independent 90/180-day stay rule, never counted against a traveller's Schengen clock, despite EU-candidate status. Work permits genuinely double as the residence permit for their full validity — no separate document needed. Transit is not a general-purpose visa at all; the narrow exception is a real, dated (April 2025) e-ATV for onward travel to Mexico, Venezuela, Colombia, or Panama specifically. Dependent visas explicitly exclude parents, siblings, and grandparents — only spouse and dependent children qualify.

**`database/seed-checklist-sri-lanka-all-types.sql`** (country_id 94): A genuinely volatile ETA fee history — paid, then free-pilot for 7 countries from October 2023, then an expanded free scheme for 40 countries including India from 25 May 2026, explicitly stated as a fee waiver rather than visa-free travel (the ETA application itself stays mandatory). Honestly documented that Medical, Sports, and (in effect) Family Visit are NOT separate application tracks — Sri Lanka's own Tourist Visa definition explicitly covers medical treatment, sport events, and visiting relatives, so those three checklists describe the same Tourist ETA process rather than inventing distinct ones. Work and Student visas use a genuinely unusual two-hop Entry-Visa-then-Residence-Visa process. Dependent visas carry a real, explicit remittance formula (USD 1,500/month for the principal, USD 750 per dependent).

**`database/seed-checklist-ireland-all-types.sql`** (country_id 124): Ireland is NOT in the Schengen Area at all — a fully separate national visa system (AVATS portal, its own C/D visa categories, its own fee schedule), a genuinely different starting point from every Schengen country built in prior batches. India is one of only five countries in Ireland's biometric visa pilot. Work visas are genuinely two-step across two different government departments — DETE grants the Employment Permit first, then AVATS handles the entry visa; holding the permit alone does not authorise travel. A real, dated 1 June 2026 policy change removed the appeal right for most short-stay visa refusals. Post-study work rights are a distinct named permission (Stamp 1G) rather than a separate visa category.

**`database/seed-checklist-portugal-all-types.sql`** (country_id 140): Portugal closed two major, historically Indian-favoured entry pathways within months of each other — the standalone job-seeker visa (abolished 23 October 2025) and the informal "manifestação de interesse" tourist-to-work regularization route (terminated 31 December 2025), both real, dated 2025 policy changes. AIMA (SEF's 2023 successor) is documented as a live, acknowledged bottleneck rather than a resolved handover. Portugal's own VFS checklists explicitly merge Business and Conference into one product, and no dedicated Sports Visa checklist was found — stated honestly as a confirmed absence rather than assumed identical to other Schengen states. A genuine three-track family-migration system (short-stay Family Visit, DF for dependents filing simultaneously with a primary applicant, D6 Family Reunification now gated behind a new 2-year sponsor-residency rule) is documented distinctly across the Family Visit and Dependent checklists.

**`database/seed-checklist-hong-kong-all-types.sql`** (country_id 66): India is NOT simply visa-free — a mandatory, free, non-extendable Pre-Arrival Registration (PAR), in effect since 23 January 2017, gates every period of 14-day visa-free travel, genuinely different from the simpler visa-free/VoA systems of the Southeast Asian countries built in earlier batches. No separate visa categories exist for Business, Medical, Conference, or Sports — all four collapse into the same Visit Visa/PAR framework, stated honestly rather than inventing distinct processes. No Hong Kong consulate or embassy exists in India at all, since Hong Kong SAR is not a sovereign state; every application goes directly to the Immigration Department online. A newly restructured, tiered fee system (effective 26 February 2025) applies to Specified Schemes like the General Employment Policy, sitting alongside an older flat HKD 230 fee for ordinary visits.

**Locked-section audit**: run against all 60 new checklists before this batch was ever committed, using the same query introduced after the Saudi Arabia Transit bug — confirmed every checklist has at least one genuinely locked (`is_public=0`) document, with zero rows returned by the `HAVING locked = 0` check.

**Locked-content leak check**: applying the corrected, section-level methodology established in the prior batch (see above), each of the six countries' actual `is_public=0` Work-visa document names was queried directly from the database and confirmed absent from that country's rendered Work-visa page HTML — zero leaks found across all six countries on the first pass.

**UTF-8 encoding bug, found and fixed before commit**: the first Playwright screenshot pass surfaced visible mojibake (`â€"` in place of an em dash) on Hong Kong's rendered page. Root cause: this batch's six `mysql "$DB" < file.sql` apply commands ran without `--default-character-set=utf8mb4`, and this sandbox's `mysql` CLI defaults its client/connection/results charset to `latin1` (confirmed via `SHOW VARIABLES LIKE 'character_set%'`) even though the table itself is `utf8mb4` — every non-ASCII character (em dashes, curly apostrophes) in the six new seed files was silently double-encoded on the way in. A database-wide scan (`LIKE '%â€%'` across every text column on `visa_checklists`, `visa_fees`, and `visa_faqs`) confirmed the corruption was fully contained to this batch's 6 countries (69 corrupted `visa_checklists` field instances; the 4 prior batches' data, applied with the correct flag, was unaffected) — fixed by deleting all rows for country_id 66/68/94/100/124/140 across `visa_fees`, `visa_faqs`, and `visa_checklists` (cascading to sections/documents/revisions), then re-applying all six seed files with `mysql --default-character-set=utf8mb4`. Re-ran the mojibake scan (clean), the locked-section audit (still clean), and the full 60-page Playwright pass a second time against the corrected data before proceeding to commit.

**Verified via Playwright**: all 60 `/visa/{indonesia,turkey,sri-lanka,ireland,portugal,hong-kong}/{type}/` pages return HTTP 200 with `.vc-overview` and `.vc-locked-panel` present and `.vc-coming-soon` correctly absent; each country's Tourist page was screenshotted full-page to confirm layout renders correctly (hero photos not yet supplied for this batch — all six render the navy/gold gradient fallback as designed) and that special characters like em dashes now render correctly. `mysql` confirmed 60/60 checklists published across all six countries and 60/60 fee rows present, with the `NULL`-amount "Confirmed After Assessment" pattern used appropriately wherever a fee genuinely could not be corroborated this session (most heavily in Turkey, where 9 of 10 visa types carry unverified fees pending a follow-up pass with unblocked access to evisa.gov.tr/mfa.gov.tr).
